# Store history

The [Store spec](STORE-SPEC.md) defines the contract. This document records version changes, review decisions, and the original Store summary. Status labels describe the versions in which they were written.

v2.162 (store-put-ordered-build-3164, L95 F7): **`put_ordered` failed the ship gate.** The ascending `put` loop stays public. LLVM, 60 interleaved pairs per workload on shielded CPU 16, same Directory durability and inputs, both arms counted the loader and the candidate included `rows` construction. One 50,000-row S1 sorted insert batch, 4,096 SciFact documents in the S7 loader and 2,048 SIFT1M points in the S8 loader. Loop → candidate median wall time: S1 157.261 → 198.532 ms (+27.10% median paired), S7 39.254 → 42.051 ms (+8.13%), S8 6.203 → 9.446 ms (+53.84%). Median cycles: 412,729,275 → 572,203,439, 81,616,882 → 95,066,012, 19,838,662 → 32,028,870. Instructions: 998,842,745 → 1,492,707,292, 76,397,595 → 89,546,245, 47,252,712 → 91,472,576. No public `put_ordered` or `OutOfOrder` is built. F2 landed separately in `211992fa`.

v2.161 (store-tiered-idle-design-3161): text only, except L100 (d), which is executable on main: its no-crash and after-rename rows are green, and its before-rename row reopens a Tiered-idle store with no pager and is red on main with L100 (b)'s `Io(Closed)` until M3b-S1 (same cause, no new defect; this lane ships no store code). **Tiered-idle cuts decide whole packs; lease steps carry H's superseded ids** (§13.6 "Tiered-idle cuts" and "Progress", §13.7 "An overlay H supersedes", §3.5a "Advance", "Copy the tail" and "Replay", §13.10a catch-up, charges, L-IX catch-up and L100; amends `store-m3b-d7-design-3106/DESIGN-v2.md` §6, §7 rows 1, 4, 6 and 9, §10). **D-TI-1:** lane 3160 showed that v2.157's row-count idle step leaves a cursor whose byte position, index position and front-coded predecessor the inventory cannot supply, so a pin at the next cut kept a frame of the unread prefix in place and the resumed reading probe retired the pack under it. A Tiered-idle cut now retires every pack it takes whole, by I-TI, charging `probe_budget` its rows left and finishing a pack it starts. It never installs a cursor, so every installed cursor is a reading probe's, byte-exact, and promotion and resumption are unchanged. A later pin of an earlier Snapshot copies through its locator under its lease. No reconstruction, no funding, no new refusal; progress is ⌈N / `probe_budget`⌉ reading cuts or the first idle cut that takes the pack. Rejected: a logical cursor with reconstruction, PACK_INDEX-only idle reads, reset on transition, whole-pack-behind-cursor at promotion, per-frame inventory offsets. **D-CU-1 (3150 F1):** a lease step is recorded by its cut from H: catalog moves, superseded ids and H's own rows; it keeps no roots (a Tiered `before` root is not a participating root). The 0x0089 codec gains `superseded List[u32]`. `apply_steps` applies D-D7-5 to the prepare child: drop the overlay, install the record's empty root, queue the old base, then apply rows. The compare continues past a Tiered collection, and an unfinished deferred diff is `Drift`. "No epoch" is scoped to holder reopen and log@1 follower captures. Lands with M3b-S3, before M3b-S5. One rule for Memory and Tiered ids: a Memory demotion under a lease ships no per-entry removals. **3150 F2:** `remove_declared_index` runs only under `Migrate` and moves to the M3b-S5 site list; `rebuild_aggregate` is named under `demote_index`. **Charging (Sam's pre-1.0 rule):** a pin of a Snapshot captured before an idle retirement copies every frame it reaches in that pack (v2.157's unsound text kept post-cursor frames in place; main runs the reading probe there). This is the end state, not a temporary step. **Review 3157:** F1 is lane 3160's hole, found independently. Its count-plus-restart fix is rejected (a third reset, wasted idle work on every idle-to-non-idle change), and its whole-pack alternative is taken with the budget charge kept. F2 is taken: L100 (a)'s seed is now `idle_ignores_pins`, and the same-open cases (d1) and (d2) are spec text for M3b-S1. F3 (R4 compares the file's size) is declined: a class-3/4 pack's file length adds its HEADER, frame heads and PACK_INDEX to `payload_bytes`, so the row alone does not give it, and the host reads no file size (§8, Language findings, row 10). R4 keeps its presence check, and a short listed pack faults at the first probe or scrub. **Version:** 3138 took v2.158 and v2.159 and 3162 took v2.160, so this entry is v2.161. Reversibility: high (S1 and S3 not built).

v2.160 (store-d7-chain-3162, D7-S1): built. **Index reads by collection residency** (§13.10a, L98 (d)). The Tx index cursor opens through `collection_slot`, which returns the root slot and descriptor. In the L98(d) fixture, nonempty Memory cursor cases take 20 extra steps, empty takes 10 fewer, and the byte thresholds are unchanged. D7-S4 replaces the full descriptor lookup with a slot and residency-byte accessor. The index residency refusal stays in place.

v2.159 (store-put-ordered-amend-3138): text only, not yet built. **`put_ordered` restored as an opt-in checked batch behind a ship gate; one page layout; allocation-free replay priors** (§1.1 `Error`, §1.3 `put` signatures, Ascending ingest and Encoding and pages, §5 L95 O1–O4, §10 prior art; design in `store-write-path-design-v2-3103/DESIGN-v2.2.md` §2.3, §7, §8–§13; Sam 2026-11-26T21:36Z and DECISIONS 22:0xZ, which supersede v2.158 and the no-increase rule). (1) **F7:** `put_ordered` is opt-in, so it has its own deterministic contract and the `put` loop is not its oracle. It validates the handle, then order (A1), then row limits (A2), then totals and unique/`Sums` evidence against a private overlay (B), keeping no extracts across rows, then runs `put_impl` per row. Only allocation `Full` can follow validation. The precedence is by kind, then row: OutOfOrder > Limit > totals > Conflict/drift/miss > phase-2 `Full`. The frame check is exact, including negative `Sums` deltas, which answers astra 3148's 20 B case. Peak = max(A1, A2, B, L). It is built only if it beats the F3 `put` loop in time or peak on S1, S7 and S8. Prior art: LMDB `MDB_APPEND` and RocksDB SST ingestion ADOPTED. (2) **F8, recorded delta:** all three source entries build `apply_sorted_batch`'s pages again. Immutable replay's in-memory capture of 3136's five-row witness retains 5,480 B where it retained 4,984 B (+496 B, end state; no durable byte changes). (3) **F2, recorded refusal change:** immutable replay's allocation-free prior-0 pass returns `Corrupt(Malformed)` before any copy, and mutable replay checks priors inside `apply_sorted_source` with no `BatchEdit` copies (v2.150's rule), so 3136's witness at a tight grant now raises `Malformed` where it raised `Capacity` (end state). `log.try_reserve` is still to be deleted.
v2.158 (store-put-ordered-amend-3138): text only, not yet built. **`put_ordered` withdrawn; per-caller page oracles; replay refusal precedence** (§1.1 `Error`, §1.3 `put` signatures and Ascending ingest, §5 L95 O1–O4, §10 prior art; design in `store-write-path-design-v2-3103/DESIGN-v2.2.md`, which amends DESIGN-v2.1 §2.2, §2.3, §7, §8 and the named §9, §10, §12 and §13 entries; orchestrator ruling (b) after astra advisory 3148). (1) **F7:** `put_ordered` and `Error.OutOfOrder` are withdrawn. Build lane 3136 showed v2.153's pass A retained 16 MiB on 64 rewrites with unchanged distinct 256 KiB covering payloads, where `put` holds one row's. Any exact-refund install holds each replaced block with its replacement (W1: a draft-staged `Sums` row the batch changes; W2: a full row list the batch grows). Astra 3148 showed a narrower whole-input pass still returns a later `Limit(Value)` where the `put` loop returns an earlier unique `Conflict`, and its data-only frame estimate refuses a batch the loop accepts after a staged `Sums` removal. Callers stay on `put`, whose ascending run already takes F3's fast path. A checked batch API is deferred until a caller needs an atomic order refusal, with its own contract and charge account. `log.try_reserve` has no consumer and is to be deleted. (2) **F8:** one `apply_sorted_batch` oracle for all entries cost immutable capture +496 B on 3136's five-row witness (3 + 2, 5,480 B, against `std.page.bulk`'s 2 + 3, 4,984 B). Each entry now matches its caller's current page code: `bulk_from_source` (immutable replay only) matches `std.page.bulk`; `append_sorted_source` and `apply_sorted_source` (local materialization, mutable replay) match `apply_sorted_batch`, the empty base included. (3) **F2:** the replay prior pass only chooses the path; when a prior fails, today's `journal_collection_bulk` or `journal_collection_batch` runs on the unchanged base, so 3136's witness raises `Capacity` before `Corrupt(Malformed)` as today. Mutable replay gets the same rule.
v2.157 (store-zero-live-pin-3142): text only, not yet built. **Zero-live premise withdrawn** (§13.6 "Tiered-idle cuts" and "What drifts", §13.10a, L96 (e), L100). A promoted pin or fork can reach a listed class-3/4 pack whose `live` is 0, so `live` is never a reachability fact. A cut publishing a Tiered-idle generation (no Tiered holder, empty queue, no anchor) runs each selected probe without reading and needs no pager; it retires the same packs at the same cuts. The export lease names every listed class-3/4 pack (D-ZL-2: +1 u64 and +1 file copy per zero-live listed pack on a Tiered-idle store, the one input charged more). Fixes the pre-existing `Io(Closed)` checkpoint failure on a reopened store that lists a class-3/4 pack and starts no pager. Reversible until M3b-S4 lands.
v2.156 (store-d7-demote-replay-3143): schema-cut handling built in D7-S2, with L68 (f) and (g) through publication. D7-S3 builds the Tiered chunks and checks L68 (f) and (g) after the build and (h) after queue drain and compaction. **Index demotion and rebuild over a replay overlay (D-D7-5)** (§13.3, §13.6a cover rule, mandatory-candidate steps 2–3 and A22, §13.7 "An overlay H supersedes", §13.12 L68 (f)–(h); ruling in `store-d7-demote-replay-3143/AMEND-DEMOTE-REPLAY.md`, which amends DESIGN-v2 §4.2, §6, §7 rows 4–5 and §10). Lane 3141 blocked D7-S1 on two counterexamples. A demotion's schema cut folded the old overlay into the empty root H installed. A delete with prior 1 then failed `Corrupt(Malformed)` on a valid store, and an insert was resurrected under `Backfilling(None)`. The cut now classifies each overlaid (branch, id) by H as Kept (folded as before), Superseded (H removed or redefined the id without reading it: the fold discards the overlay on the candidate only, and its Cold base root goes to `deferred_frees`), or Transformed (`set_residency` follows the fold, and a build chunk into an overlaid Tiered index waits for it). No epoch: a schema commit is a cut, and every cut moves `first_live` past the whole suffix. Options not taken: fold-then-reset (charges more and lets repair fail on discarded content), an index epoch, a journaled demotion, a preliminary checkpoint. Prior art: PostgreSQL REINDEX relfilenumber swap and RocksDB dropped-CF skip at recovery ADAPTED, SQLite physical WAL and Goose NOT TAKEN. A Tiered descriptor's removal charges 126 to the Memory account, not its `row_bytes`. No format change.
v2.155 (vector-single-open-design-3101): built. **Single-open index entry scan that lends `List[i8]` codes** (§1.3, `MODULES.md` v3 L11, `core/programs/vector/README.md`). `entries` keeps its readiness and bound order and no longer opens a cursor. The first step opens one collection cursor and steps it with `std.page.cursor_read`, recording the copying scan's spans. `next_i8_entry` and `I8Code` lend a current-schema built-in `List[i8]` code from the page and decode every other row as `next_entry` does. `Index` gains a private `lendable` flag set from `std.codec.same_decoder` (PROG-9 identity, no allocation, independent of compiler revisions). Exact vector search uses it and never charges more bytes or reference steps than `c1595a59`. No format, encoding or admission-limit change. Reversible: library code and text only.
v2.154 (store-m3b-d7-amend-3129): text only, not yet built. **M3b/D7 amended after review 3125 (AMEND, 13 findings, 3 critical)** (§13.10a rewritten; pointers in §3.5a, §11.1's PREPARED kind table, §13.10, §13.12 L70, §15.0 and §15.1; design in `store-m3b-d7-design-3106/DESIGN-v2.md`, which replaces `DESIGN.md`). (1) Catch-up finishes deferred Tiered diffs at both captured roots, keeps the exact Tiered replay account, and applies Tiered round ops through a resolver on the import pin's root (call attempts on OLD's writer partition, chunked by `hold_import` cuts at `dirty_bytes`); the child does the same for its own ranges. (2) PREPARED is validated in two streaming passes; Tiered ranges are built by OLD as Hot pages of the import pin's tree under reserved stamps and emitted by chunked `hold_import` cuts, never held; a renamed chunk, keep or round cut's durable import pin is an orphan dropped by the first holder-free cut, distinct from an unrenamed cut's unlisted files and from activation; the target validator admits `account_memory` plus the plan reserve. (3) A new PREPARED kind 0x008B (0x008A is `SCRUB`'s) maps each `migrate_table` target to its base source id (absent on Memory-only images, which stay byte-identical; an unnamed new table is Memory); a replaced id keeps its residency with a rebuilt descriptor; a Tiered `migrate_table` target is built in the child by chunked commits. (4) R4, every cut under an export lease and the install cut check reachable edges class-aware against frames (ordinary cuts unchanged), keeping the settled-pack exception and the archive refusal. (5) PREPARED binds only through the runner's completion witness (its SHA-256, recorded after `write_prepared` returns); an unwitnessed file is discarded. (7) Each side of an index join resolves by its own descriptor. (8) `stage_rewrite` queues Cold checks for the consuming `waits` call; refusals map to `Missing`, `Changed` and `Full(Cache)`. (9) D7 takes Format 12 alone after 3048's Format 11 (DECISIONS 2026-11-26 20:3xZ); the rationale is `hot_child`'s fail; L99 covers both directions. (10) The export lease stays the positive-live location list; a listed zero-live class-3/4 pack may be absent from an image, and the probe, scrub and `check_capture` never read one (ratified; leasing every listed class-3/4 pack would charge more, and the byte-copied MANIFEST keeps listing it). (11) L98 compares results only when both residencies admit, and refusals across caches on one Tiered root; the Memory path branches on `collection_child`'s descriptor. (12) IVF on Tiered also needs `read_tail` over a Tiered source; the attempt gate measures real `look`. Slices reordered so every public lift lands with its law and Format 12 with the first slice that can emit it; `TieredRefusal` is unchanged until the Bucket-Tiered lane.
v2.153 (store-write-path-amend-v21-3124): text only, not yet built. **Store write path v2.1: astra re-review 3120's six amendments** (§1.1, §1.3, §3.3, §5 L94–L95; design in `store-write-path-design-v2-3103/DESIGN-v2.1.md`, which replaces DESIGN.md). (1) Immutable replay keeps its prior-0 pre-pass, deletes included, and skips zero-prior deletes, so a put of A with a delete of absent B, and a run of only zero-prior deletes, replay as on the writer. (2) `put_ordered` checks in two passes (pure checks, then base reads and probes against an overlay of the batch's released and acquired unique keys) with the index-edit plan, its index-row frame total and stamp cost in pass B as atomic `Limit`s, and allocates every block it installs privately, including the probe read records, with room in `tx.reads` reserved last through a new `log.try_reserve`, pass B's index reads seeing the batch's earlier pending index rows as a `put` loop would, so a refusal refunds everything and the install allocates nothing; v2.150's sequential `try_reserve` calls are withdrawn. (3) The ascending flag is bit 63 of `DataRows.count`, so no charged size grows (a bool added 8 B to a nested fold's `List[Staged]`); base priors use `put`'s point lookup, not a cursor; a deadline registration is bounded, prepaid within the unchanged `TASK_RECORD` or drawn from the waiting task's granted bytes, never refused at the call, here from the journal worker's slack. (4) `channel.receive_until` takes one decision per poll (cancellation, front item, `Closed`, `Empty`), so a queued item wins over an expired deadline and nothing is consumed otherwise. (5) A group with a deadline admits nothing past it or 64 KiB, deferring a later-dequeued frame; `Flush` goes before the frame-room wait only when it would block; close, poisoned close, I/O failure, abandonment and partial startup are written out. (6) §1.1 prices five journal channels at 416g + 576 and moves the 4 KiB timer and its channel to Bucket.
v2.152 (store-empty-commit-build-3075): built. **Implements v2.147's zero-mutation `run` commit skip with review fixes F1–F7** (§1.3, §1.6, §1.7, §3.1, §5 L91, §10). (1) **F1 (fencing on head-fenced stores):** on Bucket (`objects@1`, `sim@1`) and host-stream (`attached`) stores, when no prior frame is in flight (`journaled >= frame_seq`), a skipped zero-mutation `run` performs one conditional head GET (`If-None-Match` against the holder's last head ETag); if the head changed or this holder no longer owns the claim, `run` returns `Fenced(epoch)` and latches it the same way a failed head write does (`health` = `Fenced`, `failed = Some(Fenced)`). When a prior frame is still in flight, the skipped ticket gates on that frame's head write / stream batch, which fences before `durable` can return `Ok`. Directory (`log@1`) and `memory@1` stores skip with zero I/O. (2) **F2 (`Done.info: Option[CallInfo]`):** `Done.info` is `Some(CallInfo)` for every written commit and `None` for a skipped zero-mutation `run`, keeping `CallId` and `seed` unique per written commit and ruling out `recorded` aliasing by type (§1.3, §1.7). (3) **F3 (no cut on skip):** `store_body_at` skips `maybe_checkpoint` when a `run` skips its frame; a due cut (including one previously refused `Full`) is retried by the next real commit. (4) **F4 (memory ticket seq):** a skipped ticket carries the sequence of the newest written commit's ticket (`changes` on memory, `frame_seq` on disk). (5) **F5 (publisher release at unchanged version):** L91 E1 drives a zero-write attempt behind L61's latch and release at unchanged version, verifying reply-only socket bytes and no duplicate subscription frame. (6) **F6 (skip predicate completeness):** `is_empty_commit` also requires `settlement == None` and either no keyed draft or `keyed.edits` empty with `keyed.after == db.keyed_credit`. (7) **F7 (`head.at`):** §1.3 notes that skipped runs do not advance `at`, and callers that need a current time pass one. No format change (Format 10 unchanged). Reversible: code and text only.

v2.151 (store-m3b-d7-design-3106): text only, not yet built. **Tiered export and install (M3b) and Tiered index residency (D7)** (§13.10a, with pointers in §3.5a, §13.0, §13.10, §13.12 L70, §15.0 and §15.1; design in `store-m3b-d7-design-3106/DESIGN.md`). M3b: a Tiered image is §15.1's image plus its class-3/4 packs, which are copied byte for byte and never referenced. The export lease names exactly the packs the image copies. The prepare opener, restore R4 and the offline capture validate with the ordinary lazy open, which takes the Tiered plan reserve only when Tiered is present. R4 walks class-3/4 packs against their PACK_INDEX. A shipped range's residency is derived on OLD, so PREPARED gains no field. `stage_rewrite`'s Cold read is charged to `writer_pin_bytes`. D7: residency 1 is legal on index collections. `set_residency` takes index ids, and a new index on a Tiered table is Tiered. Existing indexes keep their residency. Tx index reads branch on the slot lookup they already make. The format word changes, shared with 3048 when they land together. M1 is answered: one doubling window per contiguous key range (v2.109, measured by 2543), and D7 adds no read primitive. IVF stores its cells in projector tables, so IVF on Tiered waits on M3b's Migrate lift, not on D7. No input admitted before v2.151 is charged more. Laws L96–L99 are planned.

v2.149 (store-write-path-design-3076): text only, not yet built. **Store write-path upgrades: bounded adaptive journal group-commit coalescing, separate `END` cadence, and checked ordered bulk ingest** (§1.1, §1.3, §3.2, §3.3, §3.3b, §5 L94–L95, §10; design in `store-write-path-design-3076/DESIGN.md`, aligned to `MODULES.md` `1f71060f`). (1) **Bounded adaptive group-commit coalescing and separate `END` cadence (`#8`):** Directory `Options` / `Overrides` gain `group_delay_us` (default `50` µs, max `1,000` µs, `0` disables timer wait), `end_groups` (default `64`, min `1`), and `end_interval_ms` (default `10` ms, max `3,600,000` ms, `0` restores immediate `END` on `Empty`). The Directory journal worker (`journal.DirJournalState` / `DirJournalMsg`) runs two dedicated 4 KiB timer tasks (`journal_coalesce_timer` and `journal_end_timer`, priced in `writer_plan`, zero post-spawn allocations) so no `receive_timeout` is needed. An adaptive concurrency gate (`coalesce_armed`, armed on any group ≥ 2 frames, disarmed after 2 solo timeouts) keeps `C = 1` overhead at **0 µs** while coalescing concurrent pipelined arrivals within an absolute `group_delay_us` deadline; groups `>= 64 KiB`, `group_commits`/`group_bytes` caps, `Barrier`, `Rotate`, and `Close` flush immediately. `j-<seq>.end` (`Durable` `O_DSYNC`) is decoupled from per-commit `Empty` and written only on four triggers: `groups >= end_groups`, quiet-idle `EndTick` (`end_interval_ms`), `Rotate`, and `Close`, while preserving BL5's rule that window steps run only when `end == end_named`. Durable writes per non-empty commit drop from `2.00` → `1.016` at C = 1 (and `1.00` → `0.254` at pipelined C = 4, `0.063` → `0.016` at pipelined C = 64) with zero change to Format 10, ticket semantics, crash recovery, or L61/L91. (2) **Checked ordered bulk ingest (`#10`):** `store.Error` gains `OutOfOrder`, and `Tx` gains `put_ordered[K, V](mut tx: Tx, t: Table[K, V], rows: List[(K, V)]) -> Result[(), Error]`, which checks strict ascending encoded key order in an atomic preflight (refusing duplicates or out-of-order rows with `Error(OutOfOrder)` leaving `tx` untouched; empty `rows` validates the table and stages 0 ops, preserving L91), runs table/index checks once per batch, and resolves base `prior` via a single empty/rightmost-key check (`std.page.max_key`) or forward cursor sweep. Ordinary `store.put` also tracks `DataRows.sorted: bool`, skipping `ops.slots` hashing until any out-of-order key triggers lazy fallback. `data_order` returns `InOrder` in O(1) with 0 allocation when `ops.sorted`, and `apply_collection_batch` feeds `DataRows` directly into `std.page.{bulk_from_source, append_sorted_source, apply_sorted_source}` with no `SortedSlots` permutation and no second `List[BatchEdit]`, guaranteeing bit-for-bit identical Format 10 `COMMIT` frame bytes and `std.page.canonical` trees. Laws L94 (`journal_coalesce_and_end_cadence`, J1–J4) and L95 (`ordered_bulk_ingest`, O1–O4) are owed by the build. Reversible: code and text only.

v2.148 (perf-store-read-design-3062): text only, not yet built. **Store read-path upgrades: multi-unit shared verified capture and scoped leaf visitors for resident scans** (§1.5, §5 L92–L93, §10, §11.19, §12.5, §13.4, §13.10; design in `perf-store-read-design-3062/DESIGN.md`). (1) **Shared verified capture (`#2`):** `open_shared(d, c, options)` captures one durable Directory head under §3.3b's verified reader protocol (single `validate_page_manifest` + AP20/AP21 check and single `capture_scan` replay, with a direct pack-to-slab fast path when the journal suffix is empty) and packs its immutable AP3 page images and blobs into a `shareable + send + cross` `SharedCapture` backed by `Shared[SharedBacking]` (`CONC-15`) over 4 MiB `Shared[Bytes]` slabs in a shallow fanout-16 `Shared` radix tree and flat plain index tables, carrying a fresh `Unique` capture identity. `follower_share(f, b)` freezes branch `b` of a resident `Follower`'s already-verified durable capture on the caller's limit (zero I/O, zero replay, untouched worker grant `N` and L85). `share_capture(cap)` shares in O(1) with 0 copies, 0 admissions, and 0 crossing content charge; every reader unit calls `shared_read_only(cap)` in O(1) to obtain a unit-local `ReadOnly` (`Reader`, `Snapshot`, `Table`, `Index`) over the shared backing, paying only its small unit-local metadata while 100% of backing bytes stay charged to the home unit (`MEM-19`–`MEM-28`, `MEM-33`, `CONC-15`). `open_shared` holds no files, locks, or leases after return, so readers never block compaction and crashed/exited readers retire mechanically via `Shared` refcount. Tiered stores refuse `Unsupported(0x0031)` on `open_shared` and `Tiered(Shared)` on `follower_share`. (2) **Scoped leaf visitor (`#3`):** `visit_scan` / `visit_scan_reverse` (on `Reader`, `Snapshot`, `Table`) and `visit_range` / `visit_range_reverse` (on `Tx`, `Table`) are non-`waits` resident leaf-run visitors that check reader, store, catalog (`I3`), and residency **once per scan** (refusing `ResidentTiered` with `Tiered(Scan)` before row 0) and pass a scoped `RowView[K, V]` to `scoped fn(RowView[K, V]) -> Result[Visit, E]` (`OWN-19`–`OWN-22`: no `RowView` or derived `View[Bytes] in row` can escape the invocation, while outer `mut` accumulators are permitted and `tx` is frozen). Lazy accessors (`row_key_parts`, `row_key_into`, `row_key`, `row_bytes`, `row_current_payload`, `row_decode`) decode or project only what the consumer needs, with zero per-row heap allocations and full D1 schema-evolution safety. Early `Ok(Stop)` and mid-scan `Error(e)` halt immediately and leave `Reader`/`Snapshot` clean (`Ok(Error(e))`, exact `L15`/`L26` prefix read spans on `Tx`); runtime failures (`fail`, `Capacity`, `OutOfTime`) propagate cleanly. No format change (Format 10 unchanged) and no change to journal-worker group or END policy. Laws L92 (`shared_capture`, S1–S5) and L93 (`scoped_leaf_visitor`, V1–V4) are owed by the build. Reversible: additive library code and text only.

v2.147 (store-empty-commit-design-3055): built in v2.152. **Zero-mutation `run` transactions skip the `COMMIT` frame and the sync while keeping durability-gate and ticket semantics** (§1.3, §1.6, §1.7, §3.1, §5 L91, §10). A `run`, `run_on`, or `run_with_reply` that returns `Ok(value)` with zero staged data ops (after folding callbacks and sealing log trims and cell edits), zero stream ops, zero keyed-claim edits, no `CALL` section, and no call to `store.info(mut tx)` encodes no `COMMIT` frame, leaves `frame_seq`, `file_end`, `head`, `at`, `changes`, `follow`, and journal/cut counters unchanged, and is neither refused by `journal_limit` nor blocked on `FRAME_LIMIT`. It returns `Committed(Done)` with `ticket = Ticket(store, open, Journal(frame_seq, branch, head))` — the **read version** gated on the durability of the newest prior frame (matching unchanged `seal`, ownerless `admit_claim`, `recover_claim`, and idempotent `rebuild`) — and `Done.info` equal to the read version's `CallInfo`, with `Done.reads` intact. Calling `store.info(mut tx)` inside an `Ok`-returning body consumes `(head + 1, at, seed)` and therefore writes a 0-op `COMMIT` frame (preserving §1.7 `CallId`/`seed` uniqueness, monotonic branch `at`, and L18). `serve` and `conclude` always stage a stream or keyed reply and `CallRecord` (`code.revision`), so they never skip. `check_dropped_claim_ops` explicitly commits a 0-op clock-floor frame whenever `at > head.at` (and awaits `durable` on the existing head ticket otherwise). No format change (Format 10 unchanged; replay still accepts 0-op `COMMIT` frames). Law L91 (`zero_mutation_commit`, cases E1–E7) is owed by the build. Reversible: code and text only.

v2.146 (store-perf-tx-range-3024): built. Transactions materialize staged resident row edits incrementally for range, count, and index cursors. `pop_first` and `pop_last` delete and return a table's edge row (§1.3). No format change.

v2.145 (store-retirement-cycle-design-3023): built. **Positive-inventory open cleanup breaks the retirement-pack cycle** (§2.6, §11.10 AP14, §11.12 AP16/AP21). Writing retired pack seqs into a class-2 retirement tree forced each cut after a drain to retire the previous cut's retirement pack into a new 127-byte class-2 pack forever, and mixed dead retirement frames into `pack_root`'s class-2 pack. Directory cuts now write `retirement_root = unlocated(0)` on every cut (matching genesis and `objects@1`), never building a retirement tree or drawing retirement stamps; unleased retired packs are unlinked in AP16 step 6 after `MANIFEST`'s rename and `sync_dir`, and leased packs stay in in-memory `carried` until their lease ends. At open, AP21 step 5 (`cleanup_page_manifest`) streams the store directory via `host.entries` / `host.next_entries` in O(1) memory (1,024-byte entry buffer, 64-item sorted batch, closing `HostEntries` before unlinking each batch) and removes every canonical `p-<seq>.tmp` and every canonical `p-<seq>` (seq ≥ 1) absent from the validated positive inventory L ∪ C, plus `MANIFEST.tmp` and `j-[prev_first, first_live)`. This reclaims step-6 crash orphans, formerly leased `carried` packs, and non-contiguous leftovers above `next_pack` in one pass. AP21 step 0 keeps validating a non-zero `retirement_root` if present in a Format 10 store, and its first cut retires that pack cleanly. Law T6 (`test_directory_drain_to_baseline`) goes green without change; the W2 crash harness adds `ENTRIES`/`NEXT_ENTRIES`/`CLOSE_ENTRIES`. No format change (Format 10 unchanged; removing the 36-byte `retirement_root` field is queued for Format 11). Reversible: code and text only.

v2.144 (search-form-b-2885; Store 2.4): built. **Search form B** (§1.2 "Search form B", OPTIMAL-STORE-ENDSTATE §III.3). `search@1` keeps its statistics aggregate and replaces the `Many` postings index with a projector `name` over the table. Its generations hold, per term, blocks of up to 128 postings in key order (`name.blocks`: last key, count, and per distinct tf the least token count; `name.postings`: one `Bytes` row per block packing offsets, tf and token-count pairs, eight-byte key heads and the keys), each term's newest block number (`name.heads`, so a fold appends with point reads) and each row's mention (`name.mentions`). `search.query` scores the tail's rows from the rows themselves, then reads each generation oldest first into one top-k heap, document at a time in block-max windows: each window ends at the least last key of the blocks the terms stand in, a window whose block bounds cannot reach the k-th score is passed unread, and otherwise the terms split as MaxScore splits them (Turtle and Flood 1995; Lucene's `MaxScoreBulkScorer`), the non-essential ones read only at rows the essential ones hold whose bound can still enter. Bounds are exact and sums follow form A's query-order addition; a bound equal to the k-th score prunes only rows whose keys lose the tie, so the hits are bit-identical to form A's. A row that would enter the heap is checked against the tail's keys, each newer generation's mention and then `allows`. **Decision** (reversible: package code): the brief named pivot BMW (Ding and Suel 2011); built and measured at 1M it ran only 2.1–2.5x fewer steps than form A on multi-term queries, so the windows with the MaxScore split replace it. Store gains `declared_table(t)`. `posting_index(s)` is deleted. Kill check at 1M rows, top 10, steps form A / form B published / B after ten projected churn batches: `w0` 9,633,792 / 235,008 (41.0x) / 303,104 (31.8x); `w1 w2` 13,205,504 / 2,146,304 (6.15x) / 2,228,224 (5.93x); `w50 w120` 5,767,168 / 968,704 (5.95x) / 1,056,768 (5.46x); `w900 w3` 6,258,688 / 395,264 (15.8x) / 461,824 (13.6x); `w5 w30 w300` 10,911,744 / 1,732,608 (6.30x) / 1,830,912 (5.96x); every top 10 bit-identical to form A's. With 1,000 rewrites left unprojected the tail costs about 8,600 steps per row (read_tail's walk about 7,600 of it), and form B is 0.60–1.22x form A; the default cadence (`tail: 1`) folds any backlog at the next step, so the tail stays at what the driver leaves between steps. A cheaper `read_tail` is not built. A `Bytes` field in a generated record decodes at about 8 steps per byte; a bare `Bytes` value moves in bulk, which is why a block's postings are one `Bytes` row. Law `block_max_differential` (search tests), red before this change, where the projector `body` does not exist. No format change. Reversible: package code and one accessor.

v2.143 (store-bucket-stream-build-2902): text only, not yet built. **§12.11 fixed per its sign-off** (bucket-stream-signoff-2879: six findings, eleven nits, and the head growth reverted). (1) **Orphan-row head poll (A2):** a row for (X, e) on a Place the service does not serve at e makes the service GET X's head every `idle_poll_ms` until rule (c) or (f) removes it; the close-race table row and §12.11.13 point at it. (2) **A Held row with no head task (A4):** when X has no connected head task and its head still names (S, inc) as `live` at the row's epoch, the janitor opens X through the reserved slot and closes it once the oldest Held row is `hold_ms` old; its H raises the epoch and rule (c) removes the rows (§12.11.6, §12.11.8, L90-O). (3) **Archival sealed-tail cursor (M6):** each entry that rewrites a sealed tail moves that sealed entry's `(from_seq, from_frame)` past exactly its frames, sets every remaining sealed `at` to the new position and drops the entry with its last frame (§12.11.11, §13.9, L90-E). (4) **INDEX's limit is a constant**, 16 + 68 × 10,698 = 727,480 (§12.11.2, §11.1); `max_slots` bounds only what a writer writes, and a claimant applies its own `max_rows` to a foreign register. (5) **L90-H (ii)** and (6) **L90-T** state schedules a correct build can meet. (7) **No head growth:** `HEAD_OBJECT_LIMIT` stays 65,776, because AUTHORITY's payload is at most 4,281 bytes, so HEAD, AUTHORITY and ATTACH together are at most 4,999; §12.9's head buffers stay 131,552 and every Place plan is unchanged (L90-Z0 checks it against §12.2 and §12.3). Nits: the claim reserves its fixed buffers at §12.11.5 step 2, before the hold (§12.9); `streamed` and `stream_reader` refusals are configuration errors and a dropped slot is freed; one typed `HostStreamId` names a stream everywhere (`StreamPlan.sid`, ATTACH, the register), and `Attached` carries `Option[HostStreamId]`, so direct service is `None` and not a zero sid; the plan's out-of-range limits are `Stream(Bound(field))`; a claim appends the incumbent's tail before the 8-entry overflow sets `live := None`; the ATTACH slice rule is checked at the tail read; the election copies `low` and `index`; the reader handle reads `reg` only on a 404; the janitor's foreign poll interval and charge are stated; close's settle drops ATTACH on its last head write, so close stays 2 A; L90-B4, L90-P, L90-N and L90-Z0 are made exact. Reversible: text only.

v2.142 (run-bucket-stream-spec-2877, Sam's rulings of 2026-11-26): text only, not yet built. **The Bucket host stream** (§12.11, `store-bucket-stream`). A host that opts in packs the commit frames of all its attached Places into shared create-only batch objects under one stream root, with one register `reg` per stream, so a commit costs 1 Class A and 1 Class B per batch instead of 2 Class A per Place and an idle head poll. It folds in AMENDED-BUCKET-STREAM, the Astra sign-off (A1–A5) and the Fable sign-off (M1–M12); §12.11.16 maps each item to its paragraph. Sam's three rulings: (1) Q4 amended for attached Places only: a claimant writes one revocable `Held` register row before its head replace, a standalone claim still writes nothing before it, and the attached residual is stated (§12.3 claim and residual, §12.11.5); (2) `Batching` has no default; (3) the typed API, `Config.journal: Option[StreamPlan]` and `streamed(a, slot)`. Changed elsewhere: §12's clock rule (the stream's timers); §12.1 (`Attached(sid)`); §12.2 (the head row, the ATTACH pointer, "What each head write sets", the provider contract pointer, step 5a skipped on a streamed genesis); §12.3 (One owner per advance, claim intro, residual, Recovery's history order, no idle head poll on an attached Place, `Health.Latched`, Local reads bound); §12.4 (weak levels refused); §12.5 (`fresh` is `Behind` while a hold is present, `fresh_snapshot`); §12.6 (reclaim never touches a stream); §12.7 (export and backup settle or cut); §12.9 (an attached claim reserves at §12.11.5 step 2); §12.10 (L90 pointer); §11.1 (kinds 0x0100–0x0105); §13.9 (archival writes the stream tails as entries). New kinds are MUST = 1 under §2.7, with no format bump: a reader without them refuses an attached head as `Unsupported(0x0100)`, and an un-streamed store writes none. Decisions taken in this text, each reversible until built: kinds 0x0100 ATTACH, 0x0101 INDEX, 0x0102 TERM (the terminal fence), 0x0103 REG, 0x0104 XINDEX, 0x0105 FOOTER and roles 11–14; claims never elect, and only a host janitor elects on another stream, as a Collecting incarnation (A4, M9); a commit after a latch returns `Latched(cause)`. Laws: L90 is listed in §12.11.15 and owed by the build.

v2.141 (run-store-per-store-limits-2852): built. **Per-store limits** (§11.1 AP4a). (1) `Options.limits` and `Overrides.limits` are `Option[StoreLimits]`, `StoreLimits { frame, value, meta }`: the journal frame limit (16 + the COMMIT payload), the stored-value limit and the MANIFEST record limit. Each is at most today's ceiling (`FRAME_LIMIT` 16,777,296, `VALUE_LIMIT` 16,711,680, `META_LIMIT` 16,777,216), with floors value ≥ 4,096, meta ≥ 4,096 and frame ≥ value + 65,616 (the ceilings' own gap, so a full key and a full value still fit one COMMIT); limits outside them are `Missing("value_limit")`, `Missing("frame_limit")` or `Missing("meta_limit")`. `None` is the ceilings at genesis, so defaults are unchanged. (2) **Genesis records them in the MANIFEST** (AP14's value, meta and transaction slots) and every later open reads them from there. They are durable because replay, the recovery floor and every cut must agree with the open that wrote the journal: a limit taken from the caller's Options on each open would let a reopen with larger limits accept a frame the writer never could, or one with smaller limits call a valid store corrupt. A crash between genesis and the first commit reopens with the recorded limits. (3) **A differing Config is refused**: an open, a read-only open or an object open whose `limits` is `Some(l)` with l unequal to the recorded limits is `LimitsDiffer(recorded)`, before the writer starts; `None` adopts the recorded limits. (4) **Every check uses the store's limits**: commit admission (`Limit(Value)` and `Limit(Transaction)` in put, Cell, Log, stream and index writes, and the migration builder's value check), the frame encoder and the `FRAME_LIMIT` wait, replay (a COMMIT payload over the recorded transaction limit or a data value over the recorded value limit is `Corrupt(Malformed)`), the MANIFEST size check (`manifest_limit` = the 316 fixed bytes + meta), S10's T against meta, the journal floor (`checkpoint_bytes` + frame), and import and install against NEW's limits (a PREPARED target whose limits differ from the installing store's is `LimitsDiffer`). (5) **sizes.hirsel uses the store's limits**: the cut step is a full key plus the store's value, and the journal and recovery terms use its frame, so `disk_reservation`, `directory_writer_need` and the Bucket plan shrink with them. (6) **Export and import carry the limits**: the exported MANIFEST holds them, PREPARED's target gains `limits: Option[StoreLimits]` (AP1b: 4 bytes for `None`, 28 for `Some`), a follower and an export replay read them, and the history genesis packet records the store's. (7) **A small Place asks for small limits**: platform `local`'s Place sizing passes `small_place_limits()` = frame 327,760, value 262,144 (std.wsock's default message), meta 65,536. Its Directory need (group 64 KiB, pack 64 KiB, resident 1 MiB, 64 commits) is 1,840,112 bytes, 34,934,196 at the ceilings; a 1-lane Bucket Place at the same group and pack plans 6,202,790 (writer 6,201,452), 72,787,342 at the ceilings. `Options` gains a 32-byte `Option[StoreLimits]`, which the follower's provision box and reply slot each carry, so the follower starter is 1,258 bytes (was 1,194) and the default Bucket place plan 72,787,342 (was 72,787,278); a default Directory need is unchanged. (8) **Format 10.** The MANIFEST's key and page slots (always 4,096) now hold the value and meta limits, and the transaction slot holds frame − 16; the key limit and page target stay format constants. A format-9 store is `Unsupported(9)`, with no migration. Kept at the ceilings, because they bound decoding of bytes no store's limits chose: READER_PIN_MIN, the projector and writer pin minima, the readers term, the per-kind decode ceilings (§2.3), the commit wire's blob ceilings, the history entry and read limits, the export and object-import file reads, the sealed MANIFEST kind limit, keyed-claims policy checks, prepared-import row validation (which has no store; install refuses differing limits), index payloads in the bulk builder (the put path checks the store's value) and the object follower's rebase replay. Laws: `journal_test_small_limits` (a commit one byte over a small frame limit is `Limit(Transaction)` and one at it is accepted, before and after reopen; a reopen with the default limits is `LimitsDiffer(small)`), `journal_test_genesis_limits` (a crash after genesis, before the first commit, reopens with the recorded small limits), `journal_test_replay_over_limit` (a frame over the recorded limit is `Corrupt`), `small_directory_place_need` and `small_bucket_place_plan_at_floor` (the hand-derived figures above; the default 64 MiB-group need is unchanged at 101,977,524). Reversible: the format word is one way; the limits are code and text.

v2.140 (store-pooled-throughput-2816): built. **A serving writer shares one sync among its clients' commits** (§3.3 "A serving writer"). This is text plus one law case: the group write already exists, and the caller's order of calls decides whether commits share it. S3's `journal-group` now writes two rows per commit and checks after recovery that each commit is whole or absent. The check is red on a store that answers a commit before its sync, because the bridge's acknowledged floor is then above the recovered head. **`bound_indexes` skips the catalog cursor for a table with no index.** `index_catalog_in` compares the resident catalog's ranks at the table's two index prefixes, and when they are equal it returns no records without opening a cursor (a put on an unindexed table opened one per put). `store.run` already takes the env as a view and never copies it; `copy_env` remains for callers that need an owned copy. No format change. Reversible: code and text only.

v2.139 (store-bl15-handoffs-2793): built. **RENAME, MKDIR and REMOVE run on the ring** (§3.3b op table), so BL15's handoffs are open 1, commit 0, read 0, the gate's own count. The confined parent fd is opened as before; the leaf checks that core.md's rename needs (the source is a regular file; the target is absent or a regular file; neither is followed) are a ring `STATX`(parent, name, AT_SYMLINK_NOFOLLOW, STATX_TYPE), and the op is a ring `RENAMEAT`, `MKDIRAT` or `UNLINKAT` (a directory found by `EISDIR` is retried with AT_REMOVEDIR). Off the ring (tied mode, helper threads, Darwin) the same functions are the plain syscalls. The ring probe requires the three opcodes. The open's 4 renames and 2 unlinks now go on the ring, and an strace of the open finds one helper syscall left, the ZERO_EXTENSION `fstatfs`, which stays (2784 counted 10 open handoffs). **BL15's phase windows are the program's own clock:** each phase prints its start, taken after its gate read, and its end, taken before the line is written, on `host.mono_ns`, and the driver maps them to CLOCK_MONOTONIC through the provider's `UDP_CLOCK_EPOCH`. 2784's commit and read handoff of 1 was the stdout write and the gate wakeup inside the old driver-marked windows. **io_uring_enter per commit:** 102,664 enters over 20,000 commits (5.1 per commit on the real disk). Each commit issues two dependent `WRITE`s on the completion ring (the segment's Direct write, then the END's Durable write, which must wait for it), so they cannot share one submit. Each completion-ring CQE also ends the readiness ring's multishot `POLL_ADD` on the completion ring's fd, and the reactor re-arms it with one more enter. Removing the re-arm needs a reactor change (an eventfd registered on the completion ring, or one ring per slot) and is left for its own lane. Measured once each on CPU 16 (release, real disk): R8-open i0 149.6 ms, i1 227.2 ms; BL15 sync p50/p99 76.2/121.3 µs alone and 80.5/140.2 µs with a follower; handoffs 1/0/0 in both; io_uring_enter open/commit/read 128/102,664/0 alone and 129/101,380/0 with a follower. Law: the runtime test `slot_ring_rename_mkdir_and_remove_need_no_helper` (red before this change: a `hirsel-host` thread appears). Reversible: a code change, with no format change.

v2.138 (store-catchup-catalog-moves-2778; Store 1.2/1.3): built. **Catch-up carries OLD's index moves; upkeep builds and verifies during a hot deploy** (§3.5a "Catch-up"). A schema commit (an index build chunk, a walker's demotion) is a MANIFEST cut and writes no journal frame, so before this change a build chunk under an export lease made the next round's replay meet the following main commit at head + 2 and fail `Corrupt(Gap)`, not the `Drift` the text claimed. (1) While a lease is held, each cut that moves main's head records a **step** (main's roots before and after). `advance_export` turns the steps since the lease last moved into the catalog records and collection rows each changed, each with its journal prior; a step that changes anything but the build state of existing index records and those indexes' entries (a merge into main, a stream or keyed change, a trim) is `Drift`. Step bytes count toward the `resident / 100` measure. It rewrites the kept import's delta `before` for each record a step moved, so the install's catalog check sees OLD's record at the cut. (2) `copy_export_tail` writes **STEPS** (role 10, `seq` the round's head; kinds 0x0088 steps metadata and 0x0089 one step) before `EXPORT`. (3) `catch_up_export` reads STEPS and replays each step at main's head + 1 before the first main commit or fork of main past it, the rest after the tail. A moved record the plan holds as OLD had it follows OLD into the plan; one the plan changed must be shipped or dropped by it, else `Drift`. The shipped ranges come from what PREPARED shipped, not from each round's catalog difference, so an index OLD finishes mid-deploy stays patched. (4) The export-lease gates are gone: `upkeep_build`, `verify_step`'s demotion and `repair_indexes` (with the operator refusal `ExportLeased`) run under a lease. A projector step still waits for it. An index the release keeps unchanged that OLD demotes during the deploy now fails the install with `NotReady` (§3.5a "Activation over Public indexes"). Law: L-IX catch-up's `uuus` case (build chunks under the lease around a write to a row the chunk indexed, the third round finishing OLD's build; after each round the child's catalog and by_v entries are OLD's at the cut); red before this change (the round's replay fails `Gap`). Reversible: code, text and two scratch frame kinds.

v2.137 (store-hybrid-3-4-2792; Store 3.4): built. **Hybrid fusion** (§1.2 "Hybrid fusion"). `hybrid@1` fuses ranked key lists by reciprocal rank fusion, `Σ 1 / (c + rank)`, comparing scores as exact fractions and ranking equal scores by the key's encoded bytes, so the fused order never depends on rounding. It reads no store. Reversible: a new package and text; no store code or format change.

v2.136 (store-tiered-changes-diff-2786): built. **`changes_page` diffs a Tiered collection by tree root** (§1.5). v2.116 merged a changed collection that is Tiered on either side over all its rows. When both sides are Tiered, the merge now reads only the spans a stamp diff of the two collection trees leaves (the leaves under changed edges, each side's replayed keys, a Log's moved window edges), so the cost is the pages on changed paths plus the rows of those leaves. The results are unchanged. L63 (j) gains a multi-level case: 20,000 Cold rows against eight edits, diffed whole and one change per page in both directions against the hand-derived edit set. On it one whole forward diff read 40,000 rows before; it now expands 10 interior pages and reads 1,376 rows. No format change. Reversible: library code.

v2.135 (store-vector-prefilter-3-3-2791; Store 3.3): built. **Vector prefilters** (§1.9 form B, OPTIMAL-STORE-ENDSTATE §III.3). Both vector forms filter before they score. `vector.declare` and `vector.declare_ivf` take `ordinal`, a described `fn(K) -> u64` that gives a row's bit. `vector.query(tx, v, q, k, filter)` and `vector.query_ivf(tx, v, q, k, probes, filter)` take `Filter = Every | Only(std.bitset.BitSet)`. A row is allowed when its ordinal is below the bitset's length and its bit is set. Form A skips a disallowed entry before it checks or scores its code. Form B skips a disallowed tail row before scoring it, and its key still masks the generations. It tests a generation slot after the 1-bit bound and before the masks, the 4-bit estimate and the 10·k admission. Its exact scan with no image skips the row before extracting its code. So the 10·k candidates and the rerank are all allowed rows. When the scan is exhaustive, a filtered result equals the unfiltered result with the disallowed rows removed. Form A is always exhaustive. Form B is exhaustive with no image, or when it probes every cluster and the allowed rows number at most 10·k. No index is kept per filter value. **Decision** (reversible: package code): the ordinal is a function of the key, declared with the index, not a stored column or a store-assigned row number. The store has no row ordinal. A key-only function gives a row one ordinal across overwrites, the tail and generations. A stored ordinal would go stale when the function changed, because a projector records no revision of its fold. Law `prefilter` (vector tests) is red before this change, where `Filter` does not exist. It runs over L2 and Dot, before publication, published, and in a run whose own writes delete the filtered best row and move an allowed row, a disallowed row and a row past the bitset onto the query. The ordinal is not the identity, and every cluster is probed. In each case both forms' filtered top 4 and top 10, for a 26-row filter and for an empty one, equal their unfiltered list over every row with the disallowed rows removed. No format change. Reversible: package code only.

v2.134 (store-vector-b-3-2-2774; Store 3.2): built. **Vector form B** (§1.9 form B, §1.14). `vector.declare_ivf(mut d, source, name, metric, code, Shape{dims, clusters, steps})` registers a projector over the finished source table. Its generations hold IVF clusters of RaBitQ codes. Each row's residual against its nearest centroid is rotated (three rounds of fixed random signs and an integer Walsh-Hadamard transform). Its sign bits are packed 32 vectors to a block, and its 4-bit extended code goes in the key's mention. A delete is a mask, a merge encodes each key's newest mention again under the output's centroids, and a drop deletes a generation's rows. `vector.query_ivf(tx, v, q, k, probes)` scores the tail's rows exactly, masks the tail's keys and every key a newer generation mentions, and reads `probes` clusters per generation with the integer FastScan kernel. It prunes by the 1-bit bound (ε0 = 1.9) against the 10·k best 4-bit estimates, then reranks those exactly through `fetch_many`. With no image, or no pin at it, every row is scored exactly. A Tiered source is refused as `Tiered(Tail)` until D7. Centroids are trained once by k-means on a reservoir of up to 32·clusters rows sampled across the whole source, with the projection name as the seed input (B06, D12). There is no drift retraining. Store gains `key_codec(t)`, the table's key codec, so a package ranks ties in the table's key order. Kill check (MEASURE-M4): kernel plus bound 40.3 steps per vector at D = 768 and 51.3 padded to 1024 (line 92); on siftsmall (10,000 × 128, 100 queries, 64 clusters) recall@10 against form A at 10·k is 0.946 at 8 probes, 0.993 at 16 and 1.000 at 32 and 64. Law `ivf_recall` (vector tests). No format change. Reversible: package code and one accessor.

v2.133 (store-ap21-readback-lease-2780; Store 1.2): built. **Every cut under an export lease reads back its own packs** (§11.12 AP21 "Settled packs at a cut", C-7). v2.131 read back only the import pin's cuts, so a pack that a plain checkpoint wrote after the export copy, whether from main's dirty pages or from import frames relocated out of a sparse shared pack, was first read at NEW's open, after OLD was retired. Now a Directory store's cut made while an export lease is held runs AP21 over the packs it wrote before its MANIFEST rename, with the published generation's packs settled; the import pin's own check was the same check and is now one case of it. A refusal leaves OLD serving, and its next cut rewrites the same pack seqs. Cuts with no lease are not walked, and the install's check is unchanged (1.8 ms at 10M rows). L-IX catch-up gains a checkpoint case: a pack damaged between its write and its cut's check refuses that checkpoint at the frame, and the deploy then installs. Reversible: code and text only; no format change.

v2.132 (store-ring-measure-2770): text only, drift fixes from store-ring-laws-close-2675. (1) BL5 part 2's trigger is the 64th group's own write failing; a window step runs only after an END that covers every ack, so it cannot fail with acks past END. (2) BL9 (iii) names the bugs it can catch (an END bound taken before the terminal fence, step 8's `prev_end`); with a correct reader the restart trace has no behind candidate, so the main-only guard stays (i)'s and (ii)'s. (3) Row 31 and §3.3b cite `reactor/uring/ring.rs`, where the ring is now set up.

v2.131 (store-catchup-pause-2758; Store 1.2): built. **An install's AP21 check reads only what its cut wrote** (§11.12 AP21 "Settled packs at a cut", C-7). Every class-0 or class-1 pack below the installed `next_pack` is settled: its `packs` row must equal the installed inventory's, and an edge into it must match the installed record map's place for that stamp exactly, which ends the descent. Step 0 (the list trees and every class-2 file) is unchanged. Every frame NEW reaches in a settled pack is one the installed generation, import pin included, reaches at the same place, so the install adds no unread byte; open still runs the full walk and finds a fault in a settled pack (L-IX catch-up's damaged case). Each import pin cut runs the same check over its own packs before its rename (v2.133: every cut under an export lease does). At 10M rows (2659's harness `10000000 1 200000`, real disk, shared host at load 18–25, the two binaries run back to back) the install cut falls from 1.117 s to 1.8 ms and the pause from 4.92 s to 3.68 s. Reversible: code and text only; no format change.

v2.130 (store-tail-2-3-2665; Store 2.3): built. (1) **The tail (§1.14 "The tail").** `read_tail(tx, decl, source, resume, limits)` reads, without waiting, the rows of a projector's source that differ between its published position and the Tx's own view, staged writes included. It diffs the tree root of the projector's pin against the Tx's tree, so nothing else records what changed (D9, M6). Every Snapshot and Tx carries the projector pins of its moment. A pin serves as the base only when it matches the image the projection row names. With no base the result is None, and the caller reads the source itself. A Tiered source is refused as `Tiered(Tail)`. (2) **Projectors are registered with the declaration and stepped by the head (§1.14 "Driving", RUNNER-SPEC v2.80 §12.5).** `projector(mut d, decl, source, from, fold, cadence, steps)` registers a projector, `seal` hands it to the Db, `project_step(mut db, name, limits)` is the only step, and `rebuild(mut db, name)` requests a rebuild. `Fold`'s callbacks are `StaticFn`s over a `send` environment. The task-local `Projector` handle, `tuned`, `with_steps`, `step(mut p, ..)` and `rebuild(mut p, ..)` are deleted: there is no second path. Every fold commit is bounded at the registered `steps`. L72 and L73 now step through `project_step`, and L-TAIL is new (`store_changes_laws`), red on two seeded bugs. No format change. Reversible: code and text only.

v2.129 (store-catchup-delta-2659; Store 1.2): built. **The kept import and delta rounds** (§3.5a "The kept import"). (1) OLD validates and keeps the whole PREPARED file while it still serves: `keep_import` (now `waits`) puts the shipped ranges under an **import pin**, a manifest holder disjoint from main (owner tag 3, §2.6 "Format 7"), and cuts, so every imported page is packed before the pause. (2) `write_prepared` ships the whole preparation once; every later `catch_up_export` writes **ROUND** (role 9; kinds 0x0086 round metadata and 0x0087 entry ops): the entries the round changed in the shipped ranges, never the ranges. (3) `validate_round(dir)` checks ROUND without touching the store; `apply_round(mut db, round)` (now `waits`) patches the kept trees on OLD's head, refuses any op that does not match them exactly, and cuts them as the new import pin. (4) The install takes the ranges from the kept trees, and its cut drops the import pin (`ProposedMain.drop_pin`), so it writes only main's paused tail plus top pages and the MANIFEST; its account leaves the pin out. (5) A cut that changes no holder drops an import pin no kept import owns (crash, abort, renewal, refused round). (6) Import pins do not make commits retain old versions. The whole-range-at-pause path is gone: no PREPARED is written or imported in the pause. Reversible: code and two scratch frame kinds; a MANIFEST holding an import pin is refused (`Corrupt(Malformed)`) by a binary before v2.129, which can happen only between a keep and its install or the next cut after an abort.

v2.128 (store-log-retain-1-6-2668; Store 1.6): built. **Log retention (§14.4a).** `declare_log` takes a `Retain`, `Forever` or `Keep(n)`. `retain_step(mut db, limit)` is one bounded sweep commit that trims each `Keep(n)` Log towards its newest n, at most `limit` entries a slice, lowest id first. Like an expiry sweep it is a data commit, so it runs while an export lease is held; the runner's head calls it on idle turns (RUNNER-SPEC v2.79 §12.5). The retention is part of the release's declaration, as an extractor is, so the catalog does not record it. No format change. Reversible: code and text only.

v2.127 (store-ops-1-4-2664; Store 1.4): built. **The operator's store commands** (§15.6). (1) `hirsel store inspect DIR` and `stats DIR` print each table and index with its state, revision, exact row count and charged bytes through a read-only open (`catalog_entries`); `stats` adds the journal's segments and bytes and the pack count. `hirsel store verify DIR` scrubs a copy. (2) `hirsel store verify DIR --release EXE` runs `verify_release` on a copy: the release's schema declared in verify mode (`declare_verify`, `seal_verify`: a changed revision is recorded by name and keeps its id and state), a full scrub tour, and a walk in the new `Report` mode (`begin_verify(mode)`: `Report` lists every finding, never demotes and never commits), then `walk_coverage`, each index not walked and why. (3) `hirsel store repair DIR --release EXE` runs `repair_indexes` on the store itself: refused while an export lease is held, else a `Repair` walk to its end and `index_build_step` until nothing is Backfilling, with each failed index named. (4) `MigrationBackfill.cause` names why `hirsel dry-run` rebuilds an index: `new index`, `unfinished build`, or the changed inputs (`rebuild_cause`). Aggregates stay out of the walk (§15.6 "Not built"). Laws: `walk_corrupt_case` (location_laws) runs a Report walk before each repair case and checks its findings against the hand-derived set, every index still serving and the head unchanged (red when Report demotes); `covering_revision_case` (store_declare_laws) runs `verify_release` over the Appendix A store, a covering index whose payload was written under an old default literal, and expects `changed = [cover]` and the stale entry found (red when Report demotes, and when verify mode drops the changed name). Reversible: code and text; no format change.

v2.126 (store-child-build-2660): built. **`seal` builds an entry index bottom up** (§1.2 "The builder", AP13). One pass reads the table and extracts every row's entries, charging each to the workspace as it is kept (`Full(Resident)` past the ceiling, as AP13 says); a sort orders them; and the collection `std.page.bulk` builds from them replaces the one the record had. Before, `seal` inserted chunk by chunk, which copied every page a chunk touched once per chunk and grew faster than the rows: the prepare child's build of a `Many` index took 3.85 s at 1M rows and 68.6 s at 10M; it now takes 0.71 s and 12.1 s (host load 65–85, CPUs 16–19). A `Backfilling` record that `Migrate` resumes is rebuilt whole, which gives the same entries because a write keeps its entries ahead of the cursor. A `Unique` duplicate is still `Conflict(name)` with nothing committed, and the pair it reports is the least repeating key's least two owners, as a chunked scan from the start found. Aggregates keep the chunked builder, because their group rows add up parts; `index_build_step` on a serving store is unchanged. The sort behind every bulk build (`sort_bulk_rows`) compares the first sixteen bytes of each key as two words before it compares the bytes, which took 10M rows from 13.6 s to 12.1 s. No format change.

v2.125 (store-ttl-4-1-2666; Store 4.1, plan §III.4): built. (1) **Expiry (§1.2 "Expiry").** `expire_rows(mut d, mut t, by)` declares a table's row expiry. The store keeps it as an ordinary Many index on the expiry time under the reserved name `hirsel.expiry`, so the builder, the walker, drift and catch-up treat it as any other index; no index kind and no format change. (2) `sweep_expired(mut db, limits) -> Swept` deletes the rows due at `keyed_time`, in (table, expiry, key) order, through the ordinary delete in one commit of at most `limits.rows` rows and `limits.byte_limit` row bytes (at least one row). An entry whose row is absent stops the commit as drift and demotes the index. Tiered stores answer `Missing("steps")`. (3) No read filters on expiry: an expired row is visible until the sweep's commit. The runner's head runs the sweep as an upkeep job (RUNNER-SPEC v2.77 §12.5). Reversible: code and text only; a store that never declares an expiry is unchanged.

v2.124 (store-changes-cold-l17-2650): text and a law only, no code change. (1) §1.5 `changes_page` says how the v2.116 merge reads a Cold page: through the same `retry_tiered_read` as a Snapshot read, on the caller's Reader and pin grant. A miss is served by the pager while the calling task waits, and the batch reruns from its first unread key, so a rerun returns nothing twice. The merge never parks: the Db task's call queue (§13.3a) holds calls, and a diff is a read. (2) L63 gains (j), the publisher's shape: a whole-range diff across a Cold Tiered collection and a Memory one, between a reopened Cold head and a commit through the pager, read whole and one change per page in both directions. It is red when the merge does not move on to the next collection (`no_collection_advance`, which the projector's single-collection diff never reaches) and when the merge is removed. Reversible: text and a test.

v2.123 (store-head-driver-1-3-2618; Store 1.3): built. (1) **The index walker (§1.2 "The index walker").** `begin_verify()` and `verify_step(mut db, mut walk, limit)` check every Public index against its table in both directions, one slice of at most `limit` rows or entries at a time. Both sides of a slice come from main's head as it stands, so a write between slices never makes a finding. A finding counts as drift and demotes the index, as §I.3's drift does, except while an export lease is held. (2) **The runner drives the store's upkeep** (RUNNER-SPEC v2.75 §12.5): index build chunks, scrub slices and walker slices run on the head's idle turns, so `index_build_step` needs no caller of its own. No format change. Reversible: code and text only.

v2.122 (store-catchup-1-2-2617; Store 1.2): built. **Catch-up in the prepare child** (§3.5a "Catch-up"). (1) `advance_export(mut db, scratch, attempt)` moves the held export lease to OLD's current head and returns `Tail(tail)`, or, when the journal written since the lease is more than 1/100 of OLD's resident bytes, releases it and returns `Full(job)`, a fresh copy for the same attempt and activation. (2) `copy_export_tail(tail, dir)` appends that journal to the export directory, across any rotation, and rewrites `EXPORT`. (3) `catch_up_export(dir, mut source, mut staged)` replays the tail from where the last round ended on the child's own load and brings each index range the preparation ships to the new head from the rows the tail changed; PREPARED then names the new head. (4) **The lease keeps every segment from its first** (§11.17 step 2): the interval is [first, ∞), so no cut removes the journal a later round copies. (5) **Activation over Public indexes only** (§1.2): `install_prepared` is `Store(NotReady(name))` when the release would install an index that is not `Public`. (6) A same-id index that is not `Public` on OLD ships as a replaced range, and OLD's PREPARED check accepts an index entry's payload (a `Unique` owner, a projection or a covered row). Before this an index with a payload could not be shipped at all (`ImportMalformed`). (7) **A Failed index is not restarted automatically**: the next deploy that declares it, or `rebuild_index`, restarts it. Reversible: all code; no format change.

v2.121 (store-search-a-2-2-2640; Store 2.2, D14 = A): built. (1) **Search form A** (§1.2 "Search form A"). `search@1` declares BM25 search on a store table as one `Many` postings index and one aggregate of corpus and term statistics, both written in the row's own transaction, backfilled by the builder and revisioned through one extractor. `search.query(tx, …)` is a plain function of a `Tx`, so entry and query bodies call it, and equal scores rank in primary key order. The five-table `upsert` API (`spec`, `Spec`, `Index`, `upsert`, `delete`, the `Reader` and `Snapshot` form of `query`) is deleted with no compatibility path. (2) **`primary_key(ix)`** returns an index's table key codec, so a package that merges several index ranges orders rows by their key bytes. (3) **Aggregate overflow during a build stays a stall** (§1.2 "Aggregates"): no terminal state is added. Search's term groups cannot overflow, and its corpus sum needs more than 2^64 − 1 tokens. No format change. Reversible: library code and spec text.

v2.120 (store-status-1-8-2619; Store 1.8): built. **What an operator can see (§II.3).** `store.attention(db)` reports, besides drift and reclaim failures: `indexes`, every index on main's head with its build state (`IndexState` is public: `Backfilling(cursor)`, `Public`, `Failed(first, second)`), in catalog order; `IndexDrift.demoted`, the demotions the writer committed for that index since this open (one per demoting schema commit; none when the index is already `Backfilling(None)` or the drift was on a branch); and `reclaim_pending`, true from a failed Bucket reclaim pass until a pass succeeds. `attention_mark(db)` changes whenever `attention` may answer differently, so a caller reads it cheaply and calls `attention` only on a change. The runner's `Status.store` carries the answer (RUNNER-SPEC v2.75). The cursor is reported as the raw primary key, not as a fraction of the table: no row count exists to divide by. Reversible: code only, no format change.

v2.119 (keyed-l17-l21r-2608): built. `READER_PIN_MIN` = `VALUE_LIMIT` + 1 MiB names §13.4's least Reader `pin_bytes` on a Tiered open. The cache's admission check uses it, and so does every runner Reader, which asked for 0 and was refused `Missing("pin_bytes")` on a Tiered open.

v2.118 (store-aggregate-2-1-2620; Store 2.1, D12): built. (1) **Aggregate indexes** (§1.2 "Aggregates"). `aggregate(mut d, mut t, name, by, key)` declares an index whose extractor gives each row `(G, u64)` parts; per group it keeps the count of rows and the sum of their values, maintained by every `put` and `delete` and built by the §1.2 builder through the same `Backfilling`, `Public` and `Failed` states. `totals(tx, a, g)` reads a group's `Totals(count, sum)`; `rebuild_aggregate(mut db, a)` demotes it. (2) **Format 9.** An index record's kind byte gains `2`, an aggregate. A format-8 store is `Unsupported(8)`, with no migration. Reversible: the format word is one way (a format-9 store does not open under format 8); the aggregate kind is code.

v2.117 (macos-s8-darwin-durability-2586; macOS decision D5, Store D13): §3.3c is built for sync, `Durable` and `Direct` handles (their `write_at` and `zero_at`), ZERO_EXTENSION and clone; preallocation is not in the runtime on any host yet. The implementation settles what §3.3c left open. (1) A `Durable` or `Direct` write whose `F_FULLFSYNC` fails completes with nothing written and the flush's errno: no byte of it is known durable. (2) A refused `F_FULLFSYNC` is answered to the program as its errno; the provider never falls back to `fsync` or `F_BARRIERFSYNC`. (3) A file system that refuses `F_NOCACHE` keeps a buffered handle under `Durable`'s completion rule, as a Linux file system without usable `STATX_DIOALIGN` keeps `O_DSYNC`. (4) `fclonefileat` refused for any reason but an existing target falls back to a copy, as `FICLONE` does. (5) Maintenance commits use the one durability class and do not await their own sync (D13). (6) The link-time ban is also a law over the Darwin runtime archive, run on Linux. The format, recovery, the commit law and every Linux behaviour are unchanged.

v2.116 (tiered-followups-2609): built. A projector runs on a Tiered source (§1.14). (1) `with_steps(p, steps)` bounds each commit that runs `apply`, `merge` or `drop` at `steps`, because an unbounded `run` on a store with a Tiered collection is `Missing("steps")` (§13.3). An overrun is `ProjectError.Stopped(OutOfTime)`. (2) `rebuild` sets `again` with the store's own body, which touches only the projection row, so it needs no bound. (3) `read_projection` reads the projection row (a Memory table) through a reader with no pager pool, so it needs no `pin_bytes` once the store has a pager. (4) `changes` and `changes_page` with a base work over Tiered collections (§1.5). The page walk still skips by subtree stamps. For a collection whose descriptor changed and that is Tiered on either side, the rows are merged from both Snapshots in key order, each read through its own pin root, at O(rows) for that collection. Before this, the walk reached a Cold child with no resolver and failed. L72 gains the Tiered-source arm. L63 (d) is built for the Tiered prior, and L63 gains (i), a seeded schedule law (TURSO #2). No format change. Reversible: `with_steps` and the merge are library code.

v2.115 (store-index-states-2559; Store 1.1): built. (1) **Format 8.** An index's catalog record ends with its build state: `Backfilling(cursor)`, `Public` or `Failed(first, second)` (§1.2 "Index states"). A format-7 store is `Unsupported(7)`, with no migration. (2) **The chunked resumable builder** replaces the one-shot backfill: each chunk reads the table at the current head and commits its entries and its cursor in one schema commit, and a read of an index that is not `Public` is `NotReady(name)`. `index_build_step` and `rebuild_index` are new. (3) **Drift, step two (§I.3).** A remove whose index entry is absent, on a row the index claims to cover, fails the transaction with `Drift(name)` and demotes the index to `Backfilling(None)`. Step one's counter (`store.attention`) still counts it. (4) **§13.10.** `Tiered(Migrate)` does not refuse in-place index repair: `index_build_step` reads a Tiered table through the pager. A schema-changing deploy of a Tiered store is still refused. Reversible: the format word is one way (a format-8 store does not open under format 7); the builder and the drift rule are code.

v2.114 (tiered-activate-2596): built. **Tiered is active:** `set_residency` (§13.8) is public store API on Directory (log@1) stores, so a program moves a Table or Log to Tiered and back. The refusals §13.10 names stay (`migrate`, `export` and `install` until M3b; `Tiered(Memory)` on a memory store); Tiered on objects@1 and sim@1 is not built, and `set_residency` there is refused `Tiered(Install)` before anything changes. **T8's two retries are named apart (closing v2.111's open item).** The *mandatory-only cut* is a cut whose Tiered compaction step (AP16 item 3's T3 part) was refused: it publishes T8 steps 1, 2, 3 and 5, every added row with its own value. AP16's *redo*, after a later item or step 3 is refused with relocation in the candidate, restores the retry base. On a Tiered store that base already holds the staged Tiered steps (T2's emission, T3's moves, the queue rewrites, the drain and the retirements), so the redo carries them unchanged and drops only the class 1, 0 and 2 relocation. This is correct because the staged steps are a value built before the first attempt, which the first attempt reads and never edits: the redo writes the same class-4 and class-3 packs at the same seqs, and every rewritten queue row names a frame in a pack the same MANIFEST lists. It is simpler than re-staging, which would draw a second set of Tiered stamps and lay out the Tiered packs again. L87 (c) gains a crash after the redo's rename. No format change. Reversible: spec text and the `pub` only.

v2.113 (store-delta-generations-2572, Store 2.5, D8★ = A): built. A projector keeps its derived state as a short list of immutable generations instead of one image updated in place (§1.14). A step folds the source's tail into a new small generation, so a one-change step writes one row instead of rewriting a page of a 1M-row generation, and a tail larger than one page is folded over several pages instead of forcing a rebuild. Generations are merged in pages through a new `merge` callback once a run is at least as large as the generation before it (ratio 1), which keeps about log₂ D generations live after D folded changes. A merge drops delete masks only when its run starts at the oldest generation. Readers take the newest generation that mentions a key; merging segments and masking rows at read time stay in the package (search, vector). The 1-page in-place path and `Updated` are deleted; `Stepped` gains `Folded`, `Merging` and `Merged`; `live` returns the generations and their position; `Cadence` and `tuned` set the tail threshold and merge ratio. M3 sets `cadence()` to tail 1 and ratio 1 (§1.14 "Calibration"). L72 now checks, after every step of a churned history, that the generations plus the tail read the same rows as one full rebuild at the head, on memory and on a reopened Directory store; it is red on three seeded bugs. The `Projection` row's encoding changes: a v2.110 projection row does not decode, and its projector must be re-declared and rebuilt. Reversible only by restoring the in-place path.

v2.112 (store-composite-revision-2548; store end-state Phase 0 row 0.2, Sam's D1=B, D2=A): built. An index's catalog revision is the **composite** `code.revision(by) ‖ codec.key_revision(K) ‖ codec.revision(V) ‖ codec.key_revision(J) ‖ codec.revision(P)` (§1.2), so a changed default literal or `via` `to`/`from` body of any codec the index reads through is `Changed(name)` under `Open` and `Follower` and a rebuild under `Migrate`, as an edited extractor is. A codec revision is the code revisions of its encoder, decoder and describer (SEM-10). The catalog field keeps its layout (a length-prefixed blob); a disk store an earlier build wrote holds only the extractor's 32 bytes there, so its first `Migrate` rebuilds every index once and its `Open` is `Changed` until then. Law L76 gains the default and `from` cases. Reversible: the revision is a catalog value, recomputed on each declaration.

v2.111 (compact-row-queue-roots-2583): built. Compaction moves frames that only queue rows reach (§13.6 "Queue rows are their own roots"). Before, the probe stopped at such a frame until the drain consumed its row, so a pack that only the queue reached never retired (L87 (c)). A Page row's match copies the path from the queued page, and a Blob row's match copies the BLOB. Within one cut, a later index row descends from the row's moved value. T2 emits the copies after the roots' copies, outside any root, so a stamp that a root also moved keeps the one location the roots take. A moved Blob row keeps its `body` ordinal, which no frame reads. T8 step 3 writes an added row's new value at its `deferred_next` key and rewrites an installed row at its own key. The drain skips both. A refused probe step drops the rewrites with the root moves. Not built yet: the mandatory-only retry still carries this cut's moves, now with the queue rewrites, where T8 step 3 writes each row's own value. The moved copies' packs ship in the same MANIFEST, so the published state is consistent. No format change. Reversible: compaction code only.

v2.110 (store-delta-generations-2572, step one): built. A `memory` store's cut no longer counts resident page images. It walked every page under every head and pin on each cut, and a local projector cuts once per publishing step to move its pin, so a one-row step cost time linear in the store: 21–52 ms at 1M rows and 120–174 ms at 10M (M3). Now that walk runs in `accounts`, the only reader of the figure, and admission, which counts row versions, is unchanged. A one-row step now costs 0.46 ms at 1M rows and 1.2 ms at 10M (§13.8). A disk store already kept the figure incrementally and is unchanged. `accounts` on a `memory` store now costs one walk of every page. No format change. Reversible: one function moves.

v2.109 (store-scan-prefetch-2569): built. (1) A scan in a `look`, `run` or `serve` body on a Tiered collection reads ahead as a Reader's scan does: each attempt's sibling window is `prefetch` × 2^k pages, where k counts the call's deliveries at this root. Before, a Tx attempt had window 0, so a scan of n Cold pages cost n reruns. (2) A demand names the values the read at its key goes on to read (§13.3 "Values"). The pager reads, in one more batch, the Cold BLOB of a point read's row, or a forward scan's row and its later rows' BLOBs within the attempt's free room. So a BLOB-valued row costs no rerun of its own, and `fetch_many` over BLOB-valued rows costs one rerun, not two. (3) A delivery's path frames, its values included, are held, and only a path frame that does not fit makes a call crowded. A sibling or a prefetched value that does not fit is left out. Measured with the M1/M2 probe under `look`: a cell read of 8 to 32 rows takes 2 to 7 attempts, where it took 10 to 45. `fetch_many` over 16 BLOB rows takes 2 attempts, where it took 3. No format change. Reversible: attempt and demand state only.
