v2.108 (store-fetch-many-2550; Store 1.7): built. `fetch_many(tx, t, keys)` returns what `fetch` returns key by key, and on a Tiered store it demands every Cold row its keys miss as one batch, so the body reruns once for them, not once per row (§13.3 "Batched point misses"). A point demand carries one key path per missed key, and the pager descends them together, one GET per pack per level. A multi-path delivery never evicts its own path frames. Measured on a 20,480-row Cold table under `look`: k separate `fetch` calls take k reruns for k = 1, 4, 16, 64; `fetch_many` takes one. No format change. Reversible: the API is additive and the batch is attempt state only.

v2.107 (store-spec-phase0-text-2542; store end-state Phase 0 row 0.5): text only. (1) §1.2 states that reserved tables carry no indexes. (2) §1.15's statuses say what runs: L75 and L76 are built, and L72, L73 and L74 are partial, each naming its test and what it does not yet check. No format or behaviour change. Reversible: the spec only.

v2.106 (store-backup-restore-2549; Store 1.5): §15 specifies backup and restore. A backup is an export that no prepare child consumes, written into an empty target directory. A restore copies a backup with `EXPORT` first, validates it with the prepare opener, and removes `EXPORT` last, so an interrupted restore never opens. The restored store keeps the source's identity. The CLI is `hirsel store backup` and `hirsel store restore`, run by `libexec/hirsel/store_tool`. The served-store route through the host is specified and not built. Law L89. No format change. Reversible: a backup is the existing export image.

v2.105 (tiered-activate-2490): built. (1) §13.5 steps 2 and 3 run in the spec's order: a promoted holder's overlay is materialized after its resolution and records no fold effect, because its branch's fold already queued and filed them (L87 (e2)). (2) A pin whose cut materializes it takes that cut's stamp counter as its watermark, so no holder reaches a frame at or above its watermark, and a fork of the pin's Snapshot inherits the materialized pages below its origin. (3) A Tiered load checks stamp < the generation's `next_stamp` (M6), never a holder's watermark. No format change. Reversible: the watermark rule only moves bytes later out of `live`.

v2.104 (macos-s0-spec-2495; macOS decision D5): §3.3c states how a macOS host executes every Store sync: `F_FULLFSYNC`, and nothing weaker. Text only; not built (macOS slice S8). The format, recovery, the commit law and the handle contracts of §3.3b are unchanged: a `Durable` or `Direct` completion means durable on every host. `F_BARRIERFSYNC` is rejected.

v2.103 (run-activation-fate-amend2-2444; RUNNER-SPEC v2.66): recheck 2430's store amends to §3.5a. Text only; not built. **B1:** on a table the staging replaces, `stage_rewrite` edits the row in the staged replacement range and writes no 0x0085 row; this is build slice F1.5, after F1, which lands refusing it. OLD's M1–M3 check is unchanged: a frame row on a table the import replaces or drops is `ImportMalformed`. **B2:** the 0x0085 frame is the file's last, in role 6, and OLD's key check reads the catalog and collection roots the export lease captured. Reversible: the spec only.

v2.102 (run-activation-fate-amend-2425; RUNNER-SPEC v2.64): review 2409's store amends to §3.5a. Text only; not built. **A4:** the prepare child stages each activation rewrite with `stage_rewrite` on its `StagedMigration`, which makes all four checks against the staged catalog (NEW's current schema number, every index NEW declares). OLD's M1–M3 check only the frame's order, that the table and key exist, and `VALUE_LIMIT`, because OLD holds no extractor for an index NEW adds. L51 (e″) moves the changed-index case to `stage_rewrite`. **A5:** a rewrite list that does not fit the frame fails prepare `Limit(triggers)`. **A8:** a rewrite on a Tiered table is a row of the same activation cut. Reversible: the spec only.

v2.101 (run-activation-fate-spec-2393; RUNNER-SPEC v2.62): **activation rewrites** (§3.5a). The activation cut may rewrite the value of rows that already exist, provided no index entry changes. On the prepared path they travel in PREPARED as kind 0x0085. On a standalone install they are staged with `store.stage_rewrite`. The runner uses them to settle held trigger rows in the activation commit. Not built. The F1 slice builds kind 0x0085, `stage_rewrite` and law L51 (e″), which is UNTESTED until then. Reversible: the format is pre-1.0, and the frame is absent when there are no rewrites.

v2.100 (store-scrub-pins-2308) charges bytes examined by a scrub even when validation fails (§11.17). Format 7 gives application and local projector pins distinct durable owners (§1.9, §1.14, §11.10).

v2.99 (store-list-collections-2287) adds read-only enumeration of the staged migration catalog (§1.2). The catalog format stays the same.

v2.98 (store-ring-amend2-2269): text only, **not yet built**. It closes the Fable recheck of v2.95 §3.3b (store-ring-recheck-2249: NO-GO with 1 blocker, 1 high, 4 medium and 3 low), in the shape Sam ruled for hirsel.4 (Option A). The fence held. The holes were in the reader protocol and in law case (d). The reader protocol is rewritten as one current-state rule (§3.3b "Reader captures"), and every item is now a numbered build law (§3.3b "Build laws", BL1–BL18) that the slot-ring build satisfies and tests.
- **The terminal segment's fence decides the bound (2249 M3; BL5, BL6).** A reader tries the terminal segment's fence first. If it is free, no handle will write that segment again, so the reader syncs it and reads to the torn tail, whether or not `LOCK` is held. Only a refused fence leads to the `LOCK` probe: with `LOCK` held the capture is END-bounded, and with `LOCK` free it waits out a dead owner's last write. A poisoned owner's journal task has closed its segment, so followers reach every acked commit while that Db stays open. v2.95's lag bound under poison was false. The poison case needed no exception, so none is stated.
- **Publication is monotone per branch (2249 B1; BL9).** The worker compares each candidate with the published capture over every branch and `next_branch`. An END-bounded candidate that is behind is not published (`Captured(None)`): `catch_up` keeps the prior position, `fresh` returns the prior snapshot at once, and `at_least` keeps polling. `Diverged(b)` is reserved for torn-tail and objects@1 candidates. The main-only guards in `catch_up` and `fresh` are deleted.
  - B1's first trigger (torn tail, then END over the same segment) cannot occur once the fence decides the bound.
  - Its second trigger (an unreadable or racing terminal END) is closed by the monotone rule. That rule is the only mechanism left.
- **Header before fence (2249 M1; BL7, BL8).** A reader reads each segment's END file, then its HEADER, and takes no fence on a segment without a good HEADER. So no reader ever holds a fence a creator needs.
  - v2.95's stopped-reader stall and its logged decision are deleted.
  - The creator's poll stays, because genesis can still meet a dead genesis's in-flight creation block. It can meet nothing else.
- **The capture key replaces the journal witness (2249 M2; BL10).** A capture with an unchanged key (MANIFEST digest, segments walked, terminal END bytes, bound) reads nothing more, with no `sync_dir` and no segment sync. An END-bounded capture never syncs a segment.
  - `log_journal_witness` and its laws are deleted. v2.95's bound bit is now the key's bound field.
  - A waiting follower no longer flushes the writer's device every 10 ms.
- **Case (d) is two events (2249 H1; BL11–BL14).** The sim fs and the crash bridge give every issued direct write a readable event and a complete event, and a power cut may fall between them. Lock state is per handle on a file (inode), shared or exclusive. That is new work in `core/tools/store_crash_bridge.rs`, which today keys exclusive locks by name.
  - New cases: (d1) a late group landing, (d2) a late landing that fails, and (d3) a creation block in flight.
- **A free fence means completed, not durable (2249 M4; BL13).** A failed write releases the fence with bytes that are readable but not durable. The sync after the fence is what makes them durable, or it fails, so the sync is required on every provider. v2.95's reasons for keeping it are withdrawn. The page-cache residual of §3.8 is stated for a new process in the same boot.
- **Lows (2249 L1–L3).**
  - BL15: the gate measures R8 commits with a follower attached.
  - BL16: rows 19 and 26 rely on a page-cache invalidation that can fail.
  - BL17: `docs/spec/core.md` states what `Direct` completion fences.
- No format change. Reversible: the spec only.

v2.97 (tiered-v296-check-2251 H1): a pin that passes the AP17 step 6 live-head test takes the head's current shares. A cut can change physical locations without changing any of the three root stamps. Taking the capture's shares in this case could reintroduce a reference behind an unfinished probe's cursor, or emit a Hot copy of a frame already sealed. L88 (h) covers both cases. The pin keeps the capture's watermark. No format change.

v2.96 (tiered-v295-2244): text only, **not yet built**. It closes astra's partial attack on v2.94 (tiered-v294-attack-2242), whose three findings each broke AP6 or a stated bound. The brief named this v2.95, but store-ring-amend-2239 took that number first. Rule (b) stands: converge by stamp, with one location per stamp. See §13.6a "astra 2242 disposition".
- **No promotion shortcut (2242 #1).** v2.94's fast path ("g is the installed generation") is withdrawn. Every promotion walks against the lookup roots, which include every earlier promotion of the same cut, so one cut keeps one stamp-to-location answer. In the old fast-path case the walk visits only the Snapshot's pages that the head has replaced since the capture. `dirty_bytes` bounds those, and an unchanged head ends the walk at each `CollectionRoot` (§13.5 step 2, L88 (e)).
- **Promotions restart no probe (2242 #2).** A promotion references a listed frame in place only when the frame lies at or after the cursor of every unfinished probe of its pack. A frame behind a cursor is copied with its stamp, as a frame in an unlisted pack already is. So each probe's carried claim holds without a restart. Only a crash, or one `compact_ratio` re-selection of a running recount, resets a cursor, and a probe finishes within ⌈N / `probe_budget`⌉ cuts that run AP16 item 3 (§13.6 "A probe across cuts", L88 (f)). This replaces v2.94's mandatory restart.
- **A residency change re-stamps (2242 #3).** Memory → Tiered gives every page and blob of the collection a fresh stamp as T2 emits it. Tiered → Memory gives every loaded page and blob a fresh stamp. So a stamp lives in one class family (data pages 0 or 3, blobs 1 or 4), and a pin, fork or anchor that keeps the old descriptor shares no stamp with the new tree. The head's old Tiered root goes to `deferred_frees` (§13.8, §11.4 B3, L88 (g)). The other answer, resolving every historical descriptor at the change, would rewrite frozen holders and load their Cold trees, so it is not taken.
- No format change. L88 gains cases (e)–(g), and (b)'s seeded bug is now `promote_behind_cursor`. Reversible: the spec only.

v2.95 (store-ring-amend-2239): text only, **not yet built**. It closes astra's NO-GO on v2.92 §3.3b (store-ring-attack-2228). Kernel facts are checked against Linux v7.0 (028ef9c).
- **Exit does not drain a direct write (2228 #1).** `do_exit` calls `io_uring_files_cancel()` (`kernel/exit.c:916`), which is `__io_uring_cancel(false)` (`include/linux/io_uring.h:17-20`). That loop stops as soon as `inflight_tracked` is 0 (`io_uring/cancel.c:566-570, 598`), and only requests on io_uring files are tracked (`io_uring/io_uring.c:1579-1585`). A direct `WRITE` that returned -EIOCBQUEUED can therefore still be at the device after the process's files close and `LOCK` passes. v2.92's claim that exit waits for it is withdrawn.
- **The fix is the segment fence** (§3.3b "The segment fence"). The handle that writes a segment holds an exclusive `flock` on it. A ring request holds its own reference to that file from `fget` (`io_uring/io_uring.c:1577`) to its free (`:1111`), and the lock is dropped only in `__fput` (`fs/file_table.c:461`). So the lock outlives every write issued through the handle, including after the process dies. `open` takes the fence, shared, on every segment it reads, waiting with no deadline, before its sync and scan. A torn-header segment that step 8 re-creates is fenced too.
- **Everything else is drained by exit.** Work punted to io-wq (FSYNC, RENAMEAT, UNLINKAT, MKDIRAT, creating opens, buffered writes including END and `Durable` files) is waited out inside the same `io_uring_files_cancel`: `io_uring_clean_tctx` calls `io_wq_put_and_exit`, which waits for every worker (`io_uring/tctx.c:207-224`, `io_uring/io-wq.c:1338-1371, 1394-1399`). Inline ring ops and helper syscalls finish before their thread exits. Only direct I/O stays in flight past `exit_files`, and only segments are written directly. The op-class table in §3.3b records each class and what covers it.
- **Readers without `LOCK` probe `LOCK`, then the fence, both shared (2228 #2).** Readers take only shared locks, so two readers never exclude each other.
  - If `LOCK` is held, an owner is open: the reader applies frames only up to END's `d`, as v2.92 said, and does not wait.
  - If `LOCK` is free, the reader takes the last segment's fence shared, which waits out a dead owner's last write within the call's deadline. It then reads to the torn tail, as §3.4 does. That keeps §12.5's offline contract.
  - `at_least` on log@1 now re-captures on each poll. v2.92 said it waits for END, but it never polled.
- **Rejected alternatives.** An owner epoch is NOT TAKEN: it can detect a late write but cannot stop open from reading a dead write's bytes from the device's volatile cache. `IORING_OP_ASYNC_CANCEL` before release is NOT TAKEN: it cannot recall a bio already at the device, and a crash skips it.
- **Self-attack.** The seven items 2228 did not reach are §3.3b rows 23–29. Rows 30–32 attack the fence itself.
- **No format change.** The writer side uses `try_lock` and `unlock`. One host call is new: `try_lock_shared` (TRY_LOCK_SHARED, `flock(LOCK_SH|LOCK_NB)`, inline). Per-write cost is unchanged.

Reversible: the spec only.

v2.94 (tiered-ap6-2238): text only, **not yet built**. It resolves the contradiction that tiered-write-c-2226 found between AP6 and historical promotion. Snapshot S reaches leaf A at stamp s in pack P. A compaction cut copies A to A′, keeping s, and P stays listed because its cursor has not finished. A pin of S then took v2.59's fast path ("no Tiered pack has left since g") and referenced A in place, so one generation reached stamp s at A and at A′ (L88 (a)). Copying A when P had left made the same duplicate.
- **Promotion converges by stamp.** A pin or cut-path fork of a Snapshot that is not the live head resolves its tree top-down against the generation the cut publishes (§13.5 step 2).
  - A slot whose stamp a participating root already reaches takes that root's location, and the subtree under it is shared.
  - Only a frame that no root reaches is referenced in place (pack listed) or copied with its stamp (pack gone).
  - A page above a re-pointed slot is re-emitted with its stamp, as relocation re-emits a path.
- **Hot slots resolve the same way,** so T2 never emits a second copy of a stamp that a head has already sealed (L88 (c)). The rule now covers a Snapshot whose roots are all Hot.
- **Resolution runs after the candidate queue's added rows and before T2,** so queue rows are lookup roots (§13.6a mandatory candidate, step 4). Promotions of one cut see each other's resolved roots (L88 (d)).
- **The fast path is now "g is the installed generation"** (no cut has published since S's capture). v2.59's "no Tiered pack has left since g" is withdrawn.
- **A promotion's probe restart is mandatory.** The mandatory-only retry restarts every unfinished probe too. Before, it kept the cursor, so a frame the pin referenced in place could sit behind the cursor and its pack could retire under the pin (L88 (b)).
- **Why (b) and not physical aliases.** One location per stamp keeps a single answer for every reader, the scrubber, the walker and retirement. Classes 0–2 already converge by stamp through the stamp-keyed record map (AP16), and this gives Tiered frames the same rule by key descent, which is the probe's own machinery. Aliases would need a second, per-stamp location set that the probe, cells and the walker would all have to reconcile.
- L87 (c) and its trace put a PAGE in a class-4 pack. Y is now in class-3 pack 6 (tiered-write-c-2226's finding).
- No format change. New law L88. Reversible: the spec only.

v2.93 (tiered-amend-2213): text only, **not yet built**. It closes astra's attack on v2.91 (tiered-cut-attack-2207) and the fold gap from tiered-write-b-2197; see §13.6a "astra 2207 disposition".
- **The cut's probe sees every root the published generation has, the queue included.** The mandatory candidate forms the queue's added rows before T3. Queue rows are their own roots: a Page row is compared and then descended by index key, and a Blob row is compared directly. A match rewrites the row's value (2207 #1, #3).
- **No path or positive verdict outlives its cut.** A compaction probe moves each match at the cut that finds it; only the cursor and a recount's running sum carry (2207 #2).
- **Fold ownership follows the actual replacements, inherited at FORK.** A child folds its fork-time overlay first and records nothing for it. No fold page is shared across a fork, and the fold's lists match its published trees, not the lost live lists (L50). The overlay entry's `first` and the evidence row's ordinal and branch are removed (2207 medium).
- **Fork stamps stay above their watermark.** Replay raises the counter to each FORK's watermark, and each promoted holder gets its own materialization (check 5).
- New law L87. Reversible: the spec only.

v2.92 (store-ring-spec-2208, io_uring roadmap step 3, PLAN.md §3 rank 3 and §1b): text only, **not yet built**. Store I/O moves onto the slot's ring (§3.3b), and the journal changes how it writes.
- **One write discipline on every backend.**
  - Segments are opened `Direct`: O_DIRECT|O_DSYNC, or O_DSYNC alone where the provider refuses direct I/O. END files are opened `Durable` (O_DSYNC).
  - A write's completion means its bytes are durable, so the journal task issues no `sync` and no FSYNC, except `sync_dir` at segment creation.
  - Every write to a segment covers a 4 KiB-aligned range: the in-memory **tail image** (the segment's bytes from ⌊e⌋₄ₖ to e), then the group's frames, then zeros up to the next 4 KiB boundary (the **padded tail**). It is written from one 4 KiB-aligned group buffer allocated at spawn.
  - The host checks the alignment itself, on every provider and backend, so tmpfs laws exercise the rule.
- **No format change.**
  - The padded tail is zeros past e, which v2.80's reader already reads as the torn tail.
  - END stays 96 bytes.
  - A fresh segment is 4,096 bytes on disk instead of 72.
  - The bytes past a segment's frames are at most T + 4,095, where v2.82 had T. So v2.82's "no per-segment term" is withdrawn, and C-4 gains up to 4 KiB per retained segment.
- **Window steps** use the new host call `zero_at`, one durable write per step. The Z chunk is deleted.
- **Live capture is bounded by the END's `d`** (v2.83, v2.84). A direct write's bytes never pass through the reader's page cache as dirty data, so the reader's second sync no longer proves what it read is durable. A Live reader now applies only frames below the terminal END's `d`, on every backend, and its second sync is deleted. A follower can lag the writer by up to 64 groups under load, and by nothing once the queue is idle. **Amended by v2.95:** the END bound applies only while `LOCK` is held. With `LOCK` free, a reader takes the terminal segment's fence shared and reads to the torn tail.
- **Ring ops.** OPEN, OPEN_DIR, STAT, `stat_file`, CLOSE, READ, WRITE, `zero_at`, SYNC, SYNC_DIR, RENAME, MKDIR and REMOVE move to the ring; the last three go through a confined parent fd.
  - Locks run inline (they never wait).
  - Directory listing, `remove_tree`, temp names and the zero-extension probe stay on the helper.
  - The gate's "helper threads = 0" becomes 0 handoffs during commits and reads, and 1 per open (§3.3b, the op table).
- **The journal task now gets its tail image from the creator.** It is handed each new segment's HEADER bytes: `Rotate` gains a `Bytes`, and so does the spawn input.
- **END is written before every blocking `receive`** whenever it lags e.
- **What is deleted.** `os_sync.rs` and `HIRSEL_SYNC_HELPERS` go entirely: on a host without the ring, SYNC and SYNC_DIR take the `off_slot` seam like every other file op. Each slot ring caps io-wq at 2 bounded workers and 1 unbounded.
- **EIO and short writes.**
  - EIO is reported for the page it hits and never read as zeros.
  - A short direct write continues at the returned offset when that offset is 4 KiB-aligned.
  - Any other short write, a zero-byte write, or an error poisons the store (§3.8).
- A 23-row self-attack table closes §3.3b.

Reversible: the journal format is unchanged. A pre-v2.92 reader opens a v2.92 directory, and v2.92 opens an older one. Dropping the discipline is a code change.

v2.91 (tiered-cut-spec-2192): text only, **not yet built**. It specifies the Tiered cut publisher (§13.6a) and shared lineage boundaries (§13.6).
- **Boundaries are derived from the holders of the published generation, never stored.** The holders are pins, anchor rows and the parent sides of fork rows.
  - A boundary (w, 1) exists while at least one holder gives w. There is no count and no discriminator.
  - The cut whose generation removes the last holder at w applies the drop rule. Removing one of two pins of one Snapshot changes nothing.
- **`lineage` holds two kinds of row:**
  - fork rows, `0x01 ‖ child`, giving parent, watermark and `adopted`, with ghosts kept while dependents remain;
  - cells, `0x02 ‖ branch ‖ k ‖ side ‖ m ‖ pack`, where `m` is a watermark interval that replaces v2.89's ordinal `birth`.
- §13.6 gains the filing, drop, `adopt`, relocation and probe-completion rules.
- §13.6a amends AP16 for classes 3 and 4:
  - the record map stays Memory-only;
  - a mandatory Tiered emission walk;
  - the layout order 4, 3, 1, 0, 2;
  - the PACK_INDEX key made precise as a stored key or separator, because a range lower bound read as v2.89's "low key" could let the probe retire a pinned frame;
  - the order of the lineage and queue edits;
  - the split account and M3 fields in the MANIFEST;
  - a journal cover rule, with its crash argument, so a cut never publishes past acknowledged but unfolded Tiered writes (tiered-write-2188's repro).
- The replaced-list row is 24 bytes with its branch.
- The dropped list is writer memory of fixed 48-byte slots (`DROPPED_ROW`). It has three variants: a Page (36-byte PagePtr), a Blob (the full 42-byte BlobRef, which v2.89's 40 bytes could not hold) and a Hot share. A removed Hot blob takes no row. The `deferred_frees` wire rows are 41 and 47 bytes, set independently of the slot.
- New cases: L68 (e) and L69 (a)–(c), hand-derived.
- A forked store written before v2.91 has no fork rows and opens `Corrupt(Malformed)`, so test stores are re-created from genesis.

Reversible: the spec only.

v2.90 (journal-tail-2187): **built.** A zero step is due only when the journal task's last 64 groups were each under B = 65,536 bytes (§3.3a Bulk). A group of B or more already amortizes its file extension, and refilling behind it writes every frame byte twice. At 4 KiB × 64-frame bursts on four stores that doubled the device's sync-write bytes, and each store's 1 MiB idle step (2.2 ms alone, 3.8 ms beside three others) outlasted the 0–2 ms gap before its next burst, which queued behind it. The rule only removes steps: every §3.3a invariant, the Disk bound and the commit law hold as written. Bursts of eight or fewer frames and every 97-byte row write groups under 32 KiB and keep the window. See CHANGES-v2.90. Reversible: one counter in the journal task.

v2.89 (log-retire-2165, MEM-29 Log retirement): **built**. A call's issued node ids are one high-watermark `Cell[u64]`, not a `Log[u64]` folded after the body: a reservation is one `cell.replace` (two after an overrun's rollback lowered the end), and finalization is one `cell.into_inner`. The draft's issued node is 8 bytes, not 24, so L33's 1024-byte plan leaves 48 bytes, not 32, beside the reply, and L22's `look` admits at 64 bytes, not 80. AM5 charges six fixed steps per metered draft attempt. Issued-ID finalization no longer has a variable step term. Reversible: the code and spec only.

v2.88 (store-mux-2189, owed by m3-s5-cell-2164's L63(d) block): **built** in the store and the runner's Head. The public rerun is `resume_parked`, not `resume`, because store code already uses `resume` as a local name (LANG-7). A new writer demand also waits while a detached answer is still undrained, and a demand the full writer slot refuses stays unsent until the next poll. Both are within the at-most-two-batches bound below. **Not built:** a runner-level law for a parked call, and the Tiered-prior half of L63(d) (case (g) moves the head with a Hot write). The Db task gets a **call multiplexer** (§13.3a), so a served call that misses parks instead of making the Db task wait for its delivery. What parks is the call's latched attempt state and its pin map. The queue lives in `Db` and holds no body, so the caller hands back its `env` and `body` to `resume_parked` (one input, passed to every attempt, §13.3). A call is running, parked, ready, held or cancelled. Three things wake a parked call: its pager delivery, a revocation, and a head move. The pager keeps at most one writer demand in flight, on a one-slot channel of its own, and posts one coalescing notice after each answer. The Db task polls with `try_receive` only and never waits on a send. A revocation or a cancel detaches the delivery. The Db task alone decides whether a delivery still belongs to its call, and the pager is never asked: a detached delivery is drained and dropped, and it never installs. Parked writers rerun in queue order, before any call not yet started. At most one call is held. A call is held from its third attempt, or at once when its delivery does not fit beside the other parked calls' pins. Every parked call ends within three of its own attempts. Payers:
- Queue entries are fallible allocations on the Db task. A refused entry holds the call instead of parking it, so it changes scheduling and never an answer.
- Pins share `writer_pin_bytes` across every parked call.
- A delivery in flight is pager-cache frames.

A parked call has written nothing, so a crash needs no replay: its admitted position is re-admitted as `Abandoned` (§1.8, AL1). `run`, `run_on` and `run_with_reply` stay inline. They are held from their first miss, with the revocation. `serve` gains `Served.Parked(Parked)`, and `resume_parked`, `poll_parked`, `cancel_parked` and `park_notices` are new. The runner's Head relays `park_notices` as `ParkWake` and resumes ready calls before `next_waiting` (RUNNER-SPEC note in §13.3a). Laws: L63 (g) and (h), L64 (a) and (b) as written. Prior art: TigerBeetle's grid read queue and FoundationDB's `FlowLock`; Goose has no request queue (§13.3a "Prior art"). Reversible: a code change, with no format change.

v2.87 (m3-s5-cell-2164, M3 S5): **built** for `serve`. Each attempt drafts at the head over the pin map its driver holds, and a latched miss is delivered into that map before the call drafts again, as for `run`. A latched pin limit is `NoRoom(pending, Cache(..))`, and a latched allocation limit is `Failed(pending, OutOfBytes(..))`, as for any refused allocation. Each attempt is metered by the plan's grant on its own. A served miss on a store with no pager is `Before(Io(Closed), None)`, and its completion is admitted again as `Abandoned`. `steps: None` is `Missing("steps")` only on the public `run`, `run_on` and `run_with_reply` (§1.5 #5). `admit`'s own body writes only the stream and runs unbounded, so an admission into a store with a Tiered collection is no longer refused. v2.85's "not yet built" list loses `serve`'s rerun. Law: L63 (a) for `serve`. Reversible: a code change, with no format change.

v2.86 (m3-s5-cell-2164, owed by tiered-cache-2139): **built** for Directory stores. `open` of a Directory store whose holders include a Tiered collection validates the MANIFEST with Tiered roots allowed (the walk reads the collection map and the pack list, never a Cold page), and so does a cut's check of its new MANIFEST. Once the holders are attached, and before the journal is read or any task starts, an open whose Options do not fund the plan is `Missing("cache_bytes")`. Funded means W ≥ Wmin, C ≥ W and r ≥ C + K + D + P + 64 MiB (§13.8). A funded open starts the pager before the journal tasks: its task reserves C + K + P, and `Db.pager` holds its requests. `close` drops the store's sender and cancels the pager, because a Reader may still hold one. At the default floor r = 182,375,272 the open reads a Cold row, and at r − 1 it is `Missing("cache_bytes")`. The pager starts only when the store already has a Tiered collection. A store that becomes Tiered later (promotion, not yet public) gets no pager from this open. **Not built:** a Bucket store's Tiered open, whose history validator still refuses Tiered roots, and the lineage, deferred-free and archive fields of the MANIFEST (M3 S7), which every opener still refuses. Law: L70's Tiered open case. Reversible: a code change, with no format change.

v2.85 (m3-s5-cell-2164, M3 S5): **built** for `run`, `run_on` and `look` with a point `fetch` of a Tiered collection in the body. Only a call or read whose root holds a Tiered collection gets an attempt record; on an all-Hot root the driver makes none, so a Hot body pays nothing for §13.3 (the abort budget's 80 bytes still hold). A body's point read of a Tiered collection resolves through the attempt record and the pin map the driver holds. On a latched miss the driver delivers the batch into that map and reruns the body at the same head. A latched limit decides the call: `run` gives `Refused(Cache(..))` or `Refused(Allocation(..))`, and `look` gives `Error(Full(Cache(..)))` or `Refused(Allocation(..))`. A writer's partition is `writer_pin_bytes`, and a miss on a store with no pager is `Io(Closed)`. **Not yet built:** the parked and held queue (a writer's miss is served inline, so the Db task waits for that delivery), `serve`'s rerun (a latched limit is `NoRoom`, and a latched miss is an interim `Aborted(StoreFailure(Io(Closed)))`), plan before encode, fold-demand parking, and a body's Tiered range, index and Log reads and its writes into a Tiered collection, which still fail loudly at the Cold edge. `reexecute`'s latched attempt is `Unknown(StateGone)`. Laws: L63 (a) and (b). Reversible: a code change, with no format change.

v2.84 (readonly-barrier-2178, follower-durable-2172's open item): **built.** `open_read_only` read the journal with no sync at all, so it could return a head built on frames a power cut takes back: a writer's COMMIT written but not yet group-synced, or a dead writer's page-cache tail. v2.83's barrier and refresh now cover it. `JournalRead` is `Locked | Live`; `Live` is v2.83's `LiveDurable`, taken by the read-only open and the log@1 follower alike, and the unsynced `Live` read is deleted. Both kinds sync each segment before its stat; `Live` syncs the terminal segment again after its scan and refreshes the size before `Truncated`. A read-only open now issues `sync` on journal segments it reads, and nothing else. No DISK_FORMAT change. Law: S2 crash scenario `read-only-durable` (red before: the read-only open returned head + 1, the locked reopen kept head). See CHANGES-v2.84. Reversible: restore the unsynced `Live` variant and pass it from `read_only_recover`.

v2.83 (follower-durable-2172, journal-prealloc-attack-2168's "Other" SAFETY and §6 "Concurrent observation"): **built.** A log@1 follower may read beside a running writer, and two rules make that capture sound (§3.2 live capture, §12.5). **The barrier:** after reading the terminal segment's frames and before publishing, the follower syncs that segment again on the same handle, so no frame it applies can be taken back by a power cut. Its earlier sync-then-stat left a window in which the writer could write a whole COMMIT inside the stat size and pause before its group sync. **The refresh:** a live reader that finds an END or successor boundary above its stat size stats the same handle again before it calls the file truncated, because a writer can extend the file between the stat and the boundary read. Under the opener's `LOCK` a boundary above the size stays damage. No DISK_FORMAT change. Law: L55's log@1 follower case, the S2 crash scenarios `follower-durable` and `follower-boundary`. See CHANGES-v2.83. Reversible: one barrier and one stat in `journal_recover` and the witness.

v2.82 (journal-prealloc-spec-r2-2173, astra journal-prealloc-attack-2168): text only, **not yet built**. Revises v2.80's zero window on six points (CHANGES-v2.82):
- **Admission.** The window runs only where the directory's provider guarantees that a byte newly exposed past the old file end reads, after a crash, as zero or as the byte written. The host answers one probe at open: ext4 with a positive `data=ordered` or `data=journal`, tmpfs, and the sim fs and crash bridge say yes; everything else says no. Elsewhere T = 0, which is today's append path.
- **Commit law.** acked ⊆ recovered ⊆ issued, with recovered a commit-order prefix. A window zero can complete an issued frame before its ack, as a fresh segment already could.
- **Follower witness.** The walk binds its stop, never canonicalizes a full nonzero head that does not fit, witnesses a short HEADER by its bytes, and reads each chunk once. It builds on follower-durable-2172's live-capture barrier.
- **Disk.** T = min(W, ⌊f / 4,096⌋ × 4,096) and a step never runs past e + T. So the bytes past a segment's last frame never exceed its frame bytes f, at every crash cut. No truncate is needed, and a segment under 4 KiB of frames has no window.
- **Latency.** A burst gate measures queue-inclusive latency.
- **L86.** L86 states the reader's byte-image contract; writer invariants move to real-worker cases.

Reversible: code, as v2.80.

v2.81 (m3-s5-cell-2164, M3 S5): **built** for reads and the cut's fold edits. The attempt record of §13.3 is one `core.Cell[TieredTouchSet]` that the driver creates outside the body's isolation and step bound, so a step stop or a caught failure anywhere in a touch leaves it whole. A first touch commits by one assignment of an `intent`, made after every step that can stop or refuse, and every access to the record settles a pending intent first. The 24 B/slot exact-growth reservation and the `touch_slots` option are deleted: the touched set grows one reservation per first touch and is priced as `charge.map(bytes[u64], bytes[()], c)` at the map's capacity c. The attempt's frame list is deleted: the pin map the driver holds unchanged for the attempt anchors every returned view. Reversible: a code change, with no format change.

v2.80 (journal-prealloc-spec-2153, io_uring roadmap step 1, IOC-3): text only, **not yet built**. **Revised by v2.82:** the target, the disk bound (no "at most double", no + 4,096 term), admission, the witness walk and L86 below are superseded there. The journal task keeps a **zero window** past its segment's last frame: bytes it wrote as zeros and synced, off the ack path, so a group commit overwrites written blocks instead of extending the file. On this host's ext4 a synced 4 KiB append costs 96 µs and a synced overwrite of written blocks 20 µs. The window's target T grows with the segment's frame bytes f, from 4 KiB up to W = `group_bytes` rounded up to 1 MiB (4 MiB at the defaults). A synced step refills the window whenever it falls below T/2: while the queue is empty, and once at each 64th-group END under load (§3.3a). A segment's window never exceeds its frame bytes by more than 8 KiB, so the windows at most double the journal's frame bytes on disk. **No format change:** zeros are a kind-0 frame head, which §3.4 step 5 already reads as the torn tail past `d` and as `Corrupt` below it. **Recycling retired segments is not taken:** a recycled file's stale bytes can hold a frame head whose kind is reserved, which the reader refuses before any check, so a recycled store could fail to open (CHANGES-v2.80). The journal cap U and C-4's disk statement count frames, not file sizes. The three readers that use a segment's file size (the follower witness, the export copy and the recovery scan) are restated in CHANGES-v2.80. Reversible: an old reader opens a windowed directory unchanged, and dropping the window is a code change.

v2.79 (cell-2140, Sam's ruling 1A, 2026-11-24): text only. §13.0 Q1 records one core primitive for M3, the task-local `core.Cell[T]` (MEM-29), net zero once part 2 retires `Log[E]` as a primitive. Reversible: the spec only.

v2.78 (bucket-phases-2151): **built.** Bucket sizes its writer and place plans from the lifetime grant plus the largest of recovery, journal write and cut (§1.1). The journal drains before the cut takes its session, while its task grants and §12.9 workspace stay charged. The cut reserves its own history workspace, the output it keeps through upload, and the greater of multipart staging and MANIFEST encoding. The one-lane floor law in `store_size_laws.hirsel` uses 64 KiB for pack and group. It refuses 72,787,085 B, admits 72,787,086 B including capture, and opens, commits and cuts within the 72,785,908 B writer grant. Reversible: one sizing function.

v2.77 (s3-any-provider-2111, Sam's ruling of 2026-11-24): objects@1 opens on **any https S3-compatible endpoint**. §12.2's provider allow-list is deleted: no host matching, no AWS encryption gate, no Tigris location table. What remains checks the transport (https, the loopback simulator's proof, the sim@1 client kind) and the protocol: genesis refuses an endpoint that applies a PUT whose `If-None-Match` or `If-Match` failed. Linearizable conditional writes stay a provider requirement, and for a regionally consistent multi-region bucket the operator keeps every writer and follower on one region endpoint. `Unqualified(String)` becomes `Endpoint(EndpointRefusal)`. See CHANGES-v2.77. Reversible: an allow-list could come back, but Sam ruled it out.

v2.76 (admission-plans-2113, budget-complexitysmash findings 3, 5 and 6): text only, **not yet built**. Store returns its admission plans, and every price in them comes from a MEM-24 recipe through MEM-33's `bytes[T]` and `charge.*`:
- **The plan.** `sized_reservation` is replaced by `planned`, which returns a `PlacePlan`. A `PlacePlan` holds the sized `Options`, the writer's grant T with its maximizing phase and terms, and, on `Bucket`, the capture's `FollowerPlan` and X = S + R. Its `planned_bytes` is the place's store term T + X. Store solves the share with X inside it, and `writer_plan` prices a given `Options` with the same function. Runner and Platform name no Store figure: RUNNER v2.76 deletes `disk_store_bytes` and its own X, and Platform's `host_bytes` doubles `writer_bytes`. The 64-byte drift that RUNNER law L60 carries (49,616,253 against 49,616,317) came from a Runner literal that restated Store's `follower_starter_bytes`. That class of drift has no place to live now: a Store figure is pinned only in a Store law, and a caller's law composes with the published figure.
- **Derived constants.** The follower constants (`FOLLOWER_*`, §12.5), the journal channels (§1.1), the journal worker's and timer's task charges, and the writer's fixed state are derived from their types. The writer's fixed state is a new lifetime term, which prices part of v2.75's open item.
- **Tiered cache from the remainder.** C's floor is W + U, and C takes up to 4q of what r has left above the other floors. The default floor drops from 268,030,824 to **182,375,272** bytes (§1.1, §13.8). K is unchanged. §13.8 prices each cut step's peak on both payers, and the cut phase gains `CUT_STEP_BYTES` for a step's copy. The writer's growing lists are charged where they grow, and their bounds are still open.

Bucket kept its previous inequality through v2.77. Reversible: the spec only.

v2.75 (admission-plans-2113, budget-complexitysmash finding 1): **built.** A Directory grant is its lifetime terms plus the **largest** of its recovery, write and cut phases, no longer a sum that included `journal_limit`. The old sum left the journal worker's own grant unpriced, so a 64 MiB `group_bytes` sized inside a 39,839,262-byte share whose worker alone requests 67,174,752 bytes. `journal_limit` stays the append rule's cap on uncovered journal bytes on disk (§3.4) and is not a memory term. Bucket's sum is unchanged. Law: `directory_grant_covers_journal_worker` (§1.1). Reversible: one sizing function.

v2.74.2 (store-spec-small-2097, closing CHANGES-v2.74's open items 1 and 2): text only, **not yet built**. A Tiered → Memory `set_residency` is a schema commit, so it already publishes through a cut (AP16), and its page load is now a **load step** of that cut. Each step pins one Cold frame on the cut pin quota K, copies it into a fresh Hot image and releases the pin, so the load never holds more than one PAGE or BLOB pin and never exceeds K (`BLOB_PIN` < K). The images, the descent stack and the rewritten slots are on the paused writer's own limit, and the GET range buffers are the pager's. The admission before the cut is unchanged and refuses `Full(Resident(..))`; an allocation refusal refuses the cut with `Full(Allocation(..))`; both change nothing. No §1.1 or §13.8 term changes. L64(e)'s premise now reads as parked calls' pins filling `writer_pin_bytes`; its claim and `fold_on_writer` are unchanged. See CHANGES-v2.74.2. Reversible: the spec only.

v2.74 (store-cut-payer-2083, m3-s3-slots-2010's fold funding finding, orchestrator ruling): text only, **not yet built**. v2.38 deleted the checkpoint task, but §13 and §14 still charged a Tiered cut's reads to it. A cut's Cold-page pins now draw on a **cut pin quota**, separate from `writer_pin_bytes`. The Db task owns it, and it is reserved once inside `resident_limit` as the backing for the pager's frames. Its size is a constant derived from the cut's access path: K = `CUT_PIN_BYTES` = 4 × `HEIGHT_MAX` × `PAGE_PIN` + `INDEX_PIN` + `BLOB_PIN` = 4 × 65 × 8,400 + 1,048,592 + 16,711,704 = 19,944,296. A cut pin never outlives its step, and a walk copies whatever it carries between steps into its own state. Every cut still runs inline on the paused writer (C2, C4 unchanged). Walk state, queues and the cut's fresh pages stay on the writer's own limit, where a refusal refuses the cut and loses nothing. A well-formed page graph never needs more than K, so a cut has no `Cache` refusal. §1.1's Tiered plan and §13.8's floor gain K. No `Options` field is added. See CHANGES-v2.74. Reversible: the spec only.

v2.73 (m2-open-spec-2072): sim@1 is the objects@1 protocol over a `std.sim.s3` client, with an ordered level declaration and law-held weak barriers; a weak level survives only its process. The holder's fresh read never overlaps an advance, and §12.10 lists the cases M2 still owes. Every early end of `look` carries its reads. See CHANGES-v2.73. Reversible: the spec only.

v2.72 (runner-capture-build-2068). See CHANGES-v2.72.

v2.71 (m2-finish-2021): follower commands that lose their worker or service return `Retired`. `Io(e)` carries the provider failure, including `Io(Closed)` from a log follower. An unchanged log capture reuses its roots after syncing and checking the durable inputs. See CHANGES-v2.71.

v2.70 (lane store-spec-d1d2-2058, closing RUNNER v2.54's open items D1 and D2): D1 and D2 are built. D1 follows the v2.72 amendment. **D1:** `open` takes its Authority and hands it back, whole, with every failure: `open(tasks, take a, c, r, o) -> Result[Db, Refusal]`, where `Refusal` carries the `Error` and the `Authority`. No refusal case keeps it, so a refused open leaves the caller the transport it had, and the Runner's capture uses that one client instead of starting a second. **D2:** `follower_starter_bytes()` is the exact charge a `FollowerService`'s starter pays outside `bytes`: the service task's 288, the owner endpoint's 0, and the largest single request's transient storage. Two rules make it exact: a `FollowerPlan` holds no heap storage, and a request's storage is refunded before its call returns. It is 1,098 at the current build's types. See CHANGES-v2.70. Reversible: the spec only.

v2.69 A (lane store-spec-v268-2051, orchestrator ruling on m3-s3-slots-2010's S5 gap): text only, **not yet built**. A branch split and a branch borrow move the divider already stored in the branch up to the parent, rotating it on a borrow, and never compute a separator from keys below the branch. So an interior split reads no page outside §13.3's plan pins. Leaf splits and the bulk build keep the shortest separator. After deletes a branch divider may be longer than the shortest one, and that is allowed. See CHANGES-v2.69. Reversible: the spec only.

v2.69 B (lane store-spec-v268-2051, astra mem30-apply-grant-2043 rank 1, from m2-finish-2021's MEM-30 red): built for log@1 and objects@1 followers of Memory collections. L85 passed on Cranelift. Each follower applies on **one owner-provisioned worker** that a `FollowerService` started outside every step bound, funded by a `FollowerPlan`'s envelope from the §1.1 sizing, and bound to one open by a move-only `FollowerSlot`. So a bounded caller's `catch_up` no longer spawns its apply under its own fuel, which returned `Io(Closed)`. The worker prepares a candidate; only the live call publishes it, in one swap. `follower` takes a slot and `expect` instead of `Tasks` and `Options`. Law L85. See CHANGES-v2.69. Reversible: the spec only.

v2.68 (lane runner-slot-union-spec-2050, with RUNNER-SPEC v2.53): text only. §14.9's cover semantics now point to RUNNER §3 "Slot images": a slot's rows are the difference of two images, and `window_changes` is that difference only for a tail-only slot whose widest t is the same on both sides. See CHANGES-v2.68.

v2.67 (m3-s3-slots-2010, reader-reserve-advice-2039): Reader constructors wait for one pager admission on Tiered opens. A Reader owns a grant marker and releases it mechanically. See CHANGES-v2.67.

v2.66 (lane bucket-backing-t1-2022): §12.2 qualifies the **loopback simulator**, a `std.sim.s3` bucket that other processes reach over real loopback sockets, through a per-answer proof that only a holder of the bucket credential can compute. No other provider rule changes, and there is still no override flag. See CHANGES-v2.65.

v2.64 (lane replay-delta-fix-2023, astra replay-delta-attack-2020's ten findings on v2.63): text only, **not yet built**. It fixes all ten and keeps v2.63's ruling: the journal carries what replay needs, open decodes no class-3 or class-4 frame, and every store of the format uses one COMMIT op encoding. **M3's format word is 5** (§13.1, AP1): format 4 was written by main with format-3 data ops, and its bytes can also parse under the prior grammar, so format-3 and format-4 stores are `Unsupported` at the first HEADER read, before claim, cleanup or rotation, on log@1 and objects@1, and old format-4 stores, the checkbox deployment included, are re-created from genesis. A prior is 1 + the full encoded length of the replaced value for every kind, so a Many entry's covering payload counts, and its ceiling is the format's, FORMAT_VALUE_MAX + 1 = 16,711,681, not the API write limit. Replay charges priors to `account_tiered` only; `account_memory` is recomputed exactly at open, because a commit that settles under AP15's proposed-root admission can lower it, which no prior can say. The fold applies without charging, then settles both classes like any cut. A Tiered trim keeps per-trim evidence through the fold; a Memory trim is compared at apply; the removed count is min(b, n) − f, bytes are subtracted once, and a regressing floor is `Corrupt`. A pin or cut-path fork of an overlaid Snapshot materializes its overlay before publishing. The fold rebuilds per-branch ownership from the ordered suffix. A corrupt fold takes the store error path, never `NoRoom`. See CHANGES-v2.64. Reversible: the spec only.

v2.63 (lane m3-replay-delta-spec-2012, orchestrator ruling 2026-11-24): text only, **not yet built**. It closes a §13 durability gap: a format-4 COMMIT put carried only its key and new value, so lazy open (AP21-T, which reads no Cold frame) could not tell an insert from an overwrite on a Cold leaf, and AP15's exact account could replay one row off. Every format-4 data op now carries its **prior**: a put is (key, prior u32, value) and a delete (key, prior u32), where prior is 0 for no row and 1 + |v_old| otherwise. A Log trim carries `dropped` u64, its removed rows' charge, so it is 21 bytes. One grammar on every store and residency; streams ops are unchanged. The writer takes priors from the plan's pinned leaves, so the plan precedes the encode. Replay applies priors to `CollectionRoot` and the class accounts, and leaves each written Tiered collection as its Cold tree plus a **replay overlay** that every reader merges. The first cut after open is a **fold cut** that merges the overlay through the checkpoint task's partition and rebuilds the replaced list. Writes into an overlaid collection wait for it. A follower keeps its own overlay. ~~No new format word … L48 is unchanged.~~ (Withdrawn by v2.64: the word is 5 and L48 gains the format-4 fixtures.) See CHANGES-v2.63. Reversible: the spec only.

v2.62 (W3a B2, m3-s1-read-api-2005): §1.2 stages a declaration from a Db view and seals it through a fresh mutable lend after an exact base check.

v2.61 (lane w3b-spec-fix-2003, astra w3b-check-astra-1998's 14 findings on §14): text only, **not yet built**. It fixes all fourteen. COMMIT ops are decoded by (section, op), so a serve holding Log trims and its Replied put is valid (#1). `changes` gains bounds items, so an empty Log's moved bounds wake its reader, and a page clips trimmed spans to its range (#2, #3). The tail window is [n − min(t, n − f), n), and a rerun diffs the old registration against the new (#4). A trim is a cut and join that leaves no unary non-root branch, reading at most 2h − 1 pages and touching at most 4h (#5). Rollback layers inherit enclosing bounds and masks, and a fold keeps a revert's mask (#6, #9). The dropped list holds the full PagePtr and blobs (#7). The transaction size is exact and journal-only (#8). An inline Cell counts as one version per distinct stored value in a multi-root settle (#10). Cells and Logs publish through `published_cell` and `published_log` (#11). **D-Q2-8 is restored to Sam's ruling:** a Tiered Log's archive is its anchored packed leaves, their blob closure and the committed ops between them, inside §13.9's `archive` tree (#12). L83's oracle advances in seq order (#13), and costs count logical page visits per attempt (#14). The runner side is RUNNER-SPEC v2.47 with one `Changes.bounds` amendment (report of w3b-spec-fix-2003). Reversible.

v2.60 (lane w3a-spec-fix-1997, astra w3a-check-astra-1995's store findings, with RUNNER v2.46 and v2.48): text only, **not yet built**. §1.14's projector is rebuilt: the row binds its source, publishes one complete image with its exact source position, advances it in place only by a one-page diff from a base at exactly that position, and otherwise rebuilds privately into a new generation that its last page publishes; `live` is None until the first image; callbacks are plain functions with an inert environment and outside effects go through an outbox row (#1, #4, #5, #6, #8). §1.1 derives the Tiered byte fields from one equation inside `resident_limit` and counts Bucket's buffers once (#14). L72 and L73 gain the findings' histories and the greedy page oracle (#15).

v2.59 (lane m3-spec-fix-1994, astra m3-check-astra-1990's 15 findings on §13, with RUNNER-SPEC v2.47): text only, **not yet built**. It fixes all fifteen and adds Sam's coalesced bucket GETs (2026-11-23). The probe descends every participating root by key and is never pruned by stamp, and its verdict belongs to the cut that publishes it (#1). Archive membership is a durable index of position ranges in the MANIFEST, written by the cut that a toggle makes, and every cut seals the window it covers, including a disabling one (#3). `drop_archive` never erases above the captured floor (#2). Reclaim's rule 1 is a bounded sweep with a durable cursor, so `retain` = 1 and skipped passes leave no orphan behind (#12). Pinning or forking a historical Snapshot on a Tiered store holds its closure and copies frames out of packs that have left the inventory (#4). The ARCHIVE frame is withdrawn, and the MANIFEST's archive scalars are replaced inside the format-4 change, with no new format word. Store bodies are plain fns with an `inert` env on every store, so a rerun cannot replay a side effect and ordinary writes are admitted (#5, D-M3-2). A miss latches the attempt, and every later operation of it fails, so a body cannot catch the abort (#7). Each pin batch carries the key path to the leaf, and logical reads, not page touches, form the read log (#6, #14). Held mode revokes the other parked grants first (#8). PACK_INDEX rows give a BLOB's length as 8 + BlobRef.len, and the pin charge counts the frame head (#10). Settle 1 records the stop kind and the seed, and replay compares stops first (#11, inside format 4). Every Snapshot read that can fetch takes a Reader, and Tx cursors step through the attempt (#13). A miss names the target, and hints are optional (#15). The pager's cache is private to it, and it never issues a bucket GET under 128 KiB (L84). The runner side (#9, M3-O1, M3-O2) is RUNNER-SPEC v2.47.

v2.58 (lane w3b-cell-log-spec-1991, Sam's 2026-11-23 Store DX ruling Q2 = A): text only, **not yet built**. §14 specifies `Cell[V]` and `Log[V]` as collection kinds 3 and 4 of format 4, with no new format word. A Cell lives inline in its collection-map entry up to 256 bytes and spills to a one-row tree above that, and a write of its current value is elided. A Log keeps `next_seq` and `first_seq` in its descriptor, appends with no lookup, splits its rightmost page (n − 1, 1) and (n − 2, 2), and trims in O(height) with one 13-byte COMMIT op. `changes` reports a trim as one `Trimmed` span, and `window_changes` gives the runner's tail cover its rows. `Prop` is dropped, `Edge` is a helper and `Queue` waits. Laws L77–L83. See CHANGES-v2.58. Reversible: the spec only.

v2.57 (lane w3a-dx-wire-spec-1982, Sam's 2026-11-23 Store DX rulings Q1 and Q3–Q6 with computed views and projectors): text only, **not yet built**. One `Table[K, V]` handle reads and writes, and index extractors live in a per-`Db` registry, so `Shape`, `shape` and the epoch are deleted (§1.2). A program declares its schema once through `Declare` in mode `Migrate`, `Open` or `Follower`; the catalog stores each index's kind, payload schemas and the `code.revision` of its extractor and projection, so an edited extractor body is `Changed` or rebuilt. Indexes are `Many` or `Unique`, an extractor returns `List[J]` (partial, standard and multi alike), and covering indexes carry a payload. A unique `put` preflights the whole edit, and a `Conflict` leaves the Tx abort-only. Reads gain `range_prefix`, `lookup_prefix`, `count` and `entries`; `lookup` records gaps in full (J, K) order. `get_bytes`, `scan_bytes`, `ViewRows` and `ByteRows` are deleted. `defaults()` becomes `sized(kind, share)`. Projectors (§1.14) tail `changes_page` and write derived rows and their cursor in one transaction. See CHANGES-v2.57. Reversible: the spec only.

v2.56 (lane m3-tiered-spec-1981, Store M3; Sam's 2026-11-23 rulings M3 Q1–Q4 and the archival addition): text only, **not yet built**. §13 specifies Tiered, larger-than-RAM collections on format 4. They answer astra m3-review-astra-1941 and Fable m3-review-fable-1942. Steps are a per-build bound and are not observable across residency. A per-attempt touched-page counter decides `Refused(Cache)`, which `serve` maps to NoRoom. A cold miss aborts the attempt and reruns it, and bodies become plain `fn`s with a `shareable` env. Every Snapshot read takes a per-task `Reader` and waits. Holder leases, follower stamp domains and probe-gated compaction keep reclaim safe. Archival retention is opt-in and durable, and is off by default. `migrate`, `export` and `install` on a Tiered store are refused until M3b. Laws L62–L71. Reversible: the spec only.

v2.55 (lane store-runner-soften-1974, Sam's 2026-11-23 Spec Q1, Q4 and Q5): text only, **not yet built**. `rollback_writes_on_overrun` is specified in §1.3 by bounds (O(1) entry, O(depth) reads, O(rows + depth) merge) and collapses the layer an overrun or refusal froze, so overruns do not deepen reads; no count is pinned (Q1). The cut seals pages and a commit need not; live and replayed trees share rows, snapshot answers and the account, not partitions, images or stamps (AP2, AP3; Q4). A `memory` store has no frame and no transaction size limit, and L29 is the journal's law alone (Q4). HEAD's 32-byte `image` is renamed `binding` and holds the runner's release identity digest, not the digest of the encoded `Describe`, so builds of the same code by other backends open one store; L54's histories match by identity, and two records of one identity are chosen by the deploy's outcome (Q5, RUNNER v2.42). Reversible: the spec only.

v2.54 (lane store-m2-spec-1970, Store M2): text only, **not yet built**. It answers the v2.53 check (astra m2-v253-astra-1967, two broken and two new), which found v2.52's four items closed. L54's allowances are 524,288 bytes configured and 1,048,576 hosted, so OLD can serve under the 16 MiB session ceiling. The hand install's private options come before `--hirsel-install`<!-- doc-ref: external -->, the only order the native launcher reads. A start's last answer survives host loss with its end in the launcher's inventory, and launch.md states the mark's way from `Adopted` back to `Unmatched`. The store's rules and formats are unchanged; Sam's rulings Q1–Q5 stand. See CHANGES-v2.54.

v2.53 (lane store-m2-spec-1966, Store M2): text only, **not yet built**. It answers the v2.52 check (astra m2-v252-astra-1965, two broken and two new). A launch that names an assembly record runs at the launch's allowance in every mode and must state it, so the documented hand install reproduces the record's `Describe` (RUNNER-SPEC v2.40 §6). The no-record mark has two states, and `Status.failing[].last` is formatted from an `Unmatched` mark ahead of every start's own outcome. A refused start's cause compares the store's activation with the activation that start requested. The cause reads "No recovery candidate matches the store at activation a". The store's rules and formats are unchanged; Sam's rulings Q1–Q5 stand. See CHANGES-v2.53.

v2.52 (lane store-m2-spec-1962, Store M2): text only, **not yet built**. It answers the v2.51 delta check (astra m2-v251-astra-1961, items 3 and 5); uniqueness, Installed at the attempt's version and the `Select` removal held. A group that a decided deploy left with no matching record now recovers through its own retries: each, once refused, reads the store without a witness and adopts a retained record whose `Describe` digest is the read's image (RUNNER-SPEC v2.39 §14.3). Its cause names the fact that matters, "No retained assembly matches the store at activation a". The store's rules and formats are unchanged; Sam's rulings Q1–Q5 stand. See CHANGES-v2.52.

v2.51 (lane store-m2-spec-1960, Store M2): text only, **not yet built**. It answers the v2.50 delta check (astra m2-v250-astra-1959): all three findings came from two assembly records matching one `Describe`. The runner's `Describe` now names the assembly record of the launch, so an activation names exactly one record, and the pending choice and `Select` are deleted. An Installed outcome records NEW at the attempt's version whatever the read's activation. Roots and the log@1 read held and are unchanged; Sam's rulings Q1–Q5 stand. See CHANGES-v2.51.

v2.50 (lane store-m2-spec-1954, Store M2): text only, **not yet built**. **v2.51 withdraws its pending choice and `Select`.** It fixes the three host-side failures of the v2.49 delta check (astra m2-v249-astra-1953, cited by its items 1a, 1b and 3); the row decision and the claim's pair held and are unchanged, and Sam's rulings Q1–Q5 stand. A log@1 probe refused at the post-open comparison reads the attempt's row under its own `LOCK`, so a hosted log@1 deploy is decided after another install as on objects@1. The host never picks between two assembly records that match the store's `Describe`, and a failed attempt drops its NEW only when OLD serves. See CHANGES-v2.50.

v2.49 (lane store-m2-spec-1951, Store M2): text only, **not yet built**. It fixes the two failures of the v2.48 delta check (astra m2-v248-astra-1950, cited "Check #n"); retain-1 recovery held and is unchanged, and Sam's rulings Q1–Q5 stand. An unknown install is decided from row t of one follower capture even after the activation moved on, apart from choosing what serves; a claim keeps step 1's (epoch, holder) as its pair for the whole open, and a head GET before its replace applies returns `Lost` on another pair; the replace's own Q5 GET first tests for its exact body (§12.3). See CHANGES-v2.49.

v2.48 (lane store-m2-spec-1949, Store M2): text only, **not yet built**. It fixes the three failures of the v2.47 delta check (astra m2-delta-astra-1948); Sam's rulings Q1–Q5 stand. An unknown install is reconciled by an attempt witness in the release row, at the activation commit version; a claim fences only the exact head it recovered and admitted; a cold read checks the floor of the head's own checkpoint and never a predecessor's. See CHANGES-v2.48.

v2.47 (lane store-m2-spec-1940, Store M2): text only, **not yet built**. It fixes the two blind re-checks of v2.46 (astra m2-recheck-astra-1932, Fable m2-recheck-fable-1933); Sam's rulings Q1–Q5 stand. HEAD names the latest entry by its own epoch, reclaim derives the chain it erases from, an install whose head write is Fenced or Undecided has an unknown outcome, the live client is qualified, the locator is a tree, and a Fenced store reaches an idle runner. See CHANGES-v2.47.

v2.46 (lane store-m2-spec-1930, Store M2): text only, **not yet built**. It applies Sam's rulings Q1–Q5 of 2026-11-23 (DECISIONS.md, "Store M2 gavel rulings") and the fixes from the astra attack (m2-attack-1924) and the Fable review (m2-fable-1926) to v2.45. §12 is the contract for authorities: one grant keeps the whole history; a conditional replace of the history head is the fence; a claim is one GET and one head replace, then a CLAIM entry; every head write resolves an unknown outcome by an exact resend (the Q5 rule); each authority declares named levels, and the runner releases at the strongest; followers replace §11.19's read-only open. objects@1 runs only on a provider whose conditional head write is linearizable (§12.2). §11.18's Bucket layout is deleted with no compatibility reader. See CHANGES-v2.46.

v2.45 (lane store-m2-spec-1919): text only, never built, superseded by v2.46.

v2.44 (lane store-readonly-open-1860): **built.** AP21 validates format-3 opens, export preparation and install output. §11.19 adds an offline reader for Directory stores and current Bucket mirrors.

v2.43 (lane store-bucket-1836): **built.** Rows 11 and 12 are built for one authority, a bucket. §11.18 is the contract: `open_bucket` keeps the packs and every MANIFEST generation in an S3 bucket through `std.object`, the no-clobber publish of `MANIFEST-<gen:020>` fences a second writer, and the CLAIM and AUTHORITY frames ride the MANIFEST. The journal stays local. Directory stores are unchanged.

v2.42 (lane store-scrub-1786, store durability round 2 item 4): **built.** §11.17 adds an online scrub. `begin_scrub` leases the packs the current generation lists, and `scrub_step` rereads them from disk under a byte budget, apart from every resident page. It checks each frame's envelope, and each reachable frame against the pointer the generation holds to it. It reports faults with their generation, file, offset and kind. It never repairs and never writes. See CHANGES-v2.42.

v2.41 (Store M1-4): **built.** The store opens and writes format 3 (superseding the "not yet built" tags on v2.32–v2.40 below). The format-3 contract is §11. M1-7 is part of this slice after R1 failed Guard B on the page-copy candidate. AP9 stages row and stream writes on immutable heads, and AP10 applies the staged operations on the writer. The journaled fork (AP17–AP19) and the public scoped-view API (M1-6, AP12) are built.

v2.40 (lane store-spec-v240-1693, from store-c-review-1692 findings 1–13 and the orchestrator's two capacity rulings of 2026-11-22): text only, **not yet built**. §11 is corrected so M1-4 can be built from it:
- PREPARED's 0x0081 payload has a format-3 layout carrying NEW's four limits (AP1b).
- A cut encodes one prospective set of holder records (AP16).
- `serve` and `conclude` have their own journal refusals, which never become resident `NoRoom`.
- Segments are read by a bounded streaming reader.
- The journal floor has a restart precondition and overflow-free arithmetic.
- The cut's plan has a dependency order and a retry base.
- AP21 precedes every cleanup, the prepare opener and NEW's output. (Since v2.131 the check of NEW's output reads only the packs the install wrote: AP21 "Settled packs at a cut".)
- A CALL's stream has one op, and W2 uses the last published generation.
- Three laws get independent oracles, the cap check moves to final f, and the index list is bounded by occupied bytes.

Every capacity refusal now carries `StoreCapacity = Journal | Resident | Allocation` through its operation's own result (§11.16). See CHANGES-v2.40.

v2.39 (lane store-m1-spec-c-1671, from schemasmash-durable-1683 findings 1 and 2): text only, **not yet built**. **AP21 is restored** as the format-3 reader's validation procedure, which v2.38 removed by mistake. A parsed MANIFEST and its decoded pages become a validated inventory and live graph only after its checks, and open, cleanup's retirement and an install's pre-activation check consume that validated value. **A journal CALL now rides the one Replied stream op that owns it** (AP1a): COMMIT section tag 2 is retired in format 3, and the streams section gains op tag 3, a Replied put carrying its call, so no CALL can name an Admitted put, a delete or a missing op. `recorded` keeps its result and the one-CALL rule.

v2.38 (lane store-m1-spec-c-1671, Sam's gavel ruling 1 of 2026-11-22, option C): text only, **not yet built**. §11's checkpoint becomes **bbolt-style paused incremental copy-on-write** (§11.0 choice 7, AP16): the writer plans and writes every cut itself while it is paused, writing only the frames no earlier cut wrote, and the format, L5's write bound and atomic `delete_branch` are unchanged. **A refused cut loses nothing**: the old MANIFEST plus the journal still recover every committed write, and the journal keeps growing until a cut succeeds. **Writes are refused at once at the journal cap** (`journal_limit`, default 256 MiB), with c = `Capacity{requested: U + f, available: journal_limit}`: `Refused(c)` from `run` and `run_on`, as a `resident_limit` refusal surfaces, and `Full(c)` from `admit` and a live-head `fork` (AP15). The checkpoint reserve, the credit charged on commit, migrate, fork, pin, open and install, the checkpoint task and its fixed workspace, generation-delta staging and bare rotation are deleted, and the v2.36 and v2.37 clauses that existed only for them are withdrawn (CHANGES-v2.38). v2.35's accounting wording stands: `resident_limit` bounds the logical account, journaling buffers have their own fixed bounds, page memory is reported and not admitted, and a live-head fork is O(1), one group sync. It answers the fifth rereview (`store-wholehog/m1-review-1625/REREVIEW-5.md`, U1–U13) by removal or by the rows below; `SHAPE-1644.md` beside it has the comparison of options.

v2.37 (lane store-m1-spec-fix-1629, round 5 of the M1 review): text only, **not yet built**. **v2.38 withdraws its reserve, staging, plan hand-off and bare rotation.** §11 answers astra's fourth rereview (`store-wholehog/m1-review-1625/REREVIEW-4.md`). **Planning is funded by a checkpoint reserve proportional to the physical state** (§11.0 choice 7, AP16, T9): every existing pack, unlocated frame and root holder holds credit, paid before the operation that creates it publishes, and the size proof in AP16 shows every plan fits, so no covering cut is refused by the writer or its task, and a `delete_branch` spends credit it already holds. Data relocation is the one discretionary step: it is funded when the closure is known and deferred if the writer's limit refuses it. **Staging is a generation delta** (T1): the installed view is untouched until the ack, and the only exits after staging are the ack and poison. Records hold no body share, so private edits stay in place (T4); the payer and release of every piece of writer state are tabulated (T5). Every segment is bounded by rotation alone, with a bare rotation while a cut is in flight (T9). The plan is an owned value moved to the task, its checksums filled children first (T8); list pages moved unchanged keep their stamps and only reachable list pages are emitted (T3). Part 1 of v2.37 (earlier on main) fixed the lease removal interval (T2), pack-contiguous emission and the workspace W(`pack_bytes`) (T6), commit-op ordering by index (T7), install's pre-activation checks (T10), class-2 pointer confirmation (T12) and L28 (T11).

v2.36 (lane store-m1-spec-fix-1629, round 4 of the M1 review): text only, **not yet built**. **v2.38 withdraws its checkpoint task and fixed workspace.** §11 answers astra's third rereview (`store-wholehog/m1-review-1625/REREVIEW-3.md`). **The writer plans every cut and the checkpoint task executes the plan in a fixed workspace** (§11.0 choice 7): staging, selection, the closure, the list edits, the list pages' stamps and every frame's location are decided on the writer, over checkpoint records the writer allocates with the frames they describe, and the task only encodes, writes, syncs, renames and removes. So a checkpoint is never refused for memory by its task: `checkpoint_task_bytes` has a fixed minimum that open enforces, and §3.5 Q1's `Refused` ack has no M1 cause (N8, S4, S5). The planning's own list pages, pack rows and retired seqs are the named residual: writer memory with no reservation. List pages take their stamps on the writer before the watermark is captured (S1); the retirement list is rebuilt whole at every cut, so retiring a class-2 pack can never empty another (S2); open checks that listed, retired and reached class-2 packs are pairwise disjoint and below `next_pack` before any removal, and streams each reached class-2 file once (S6, S7); the encoder writes each frame into one output buffer (S3); L5 counts list pages (S8); list rows are inline whatever the threshold and the resident report starts from the list roots (S9); replayed FORKs keep the record-byte total (S10).

v2.35 (lane store-m1-spec-fix-1629, round 3 of the M1 review): text only, **not yet built**. §11 answers astra's second rereview (`store-wholehog/m1-review-1625/REREVIEW-2.md`). **The MANIFEST's pack and retirement lists become two page trees** in class-2 packs found from two MANIFEST roots, so the MANIFEST is 152 bytes plus branch and pin records, one size expression is checked by every producer of a record, and no cut is ever refused for MANIFEST size (N8, H14, R5; §11.0 choice 6). No single figure bounds process memory any more: the frames handed to the worker, the frame being built and the worker's group buffer each have their own fixed bound (R1). `migrate` charges its catalog rows and publishes the exact account of its proposed roots (R2); install classifies each id as remove, replace or keep, so a prepared drop passes (R3); the fork preparation belongs to the live-head arm only (R4); location-table links name stamps, so relocating a parent changes no child's link (R6); `accounts.exact` is cleared by a conservative delete (R7); the bulk build passes real separators up (R8); and H8, H9, H13 and N3's laws are corrected.

v2.34 (lane store-m1-spec-fix-1629, round 2 of the M1 review): text only, **not yet built**. §11 answers astra's re-review of v2.33 (`store-wholehog/m1-review-1625/REREVIEW.md`). The conflict between delete-only admission and exact disk frame accounting is settled by one end state: `resident_limit` bounds the logical account alone, and journal frames in flight have their own `FRAME_LIMIT` with backpressure, so a delete-only commit is never refused on disk or in memory and a frame never causes a refusal (N2, N3; §11.0 choice 5). A cut's ack adjusts the account by its own capture's difference, so a delayed ack never erases newer charges (N1). Install gains a **keep** edit for collections whose identity and contents survive a schema-only release (N7). Also: the MANIFEST budget splits into branch/pin records and lists (N8, H14); `fork` prepares everything before it sends and installs infallibly after the sync (N5); relocation stages fresh records before selection (N13); the private page holds one body per live BlobRef (N11); the incremental split keeps two entries per piece (N6); the settle is one root walk of the map (N10); and the partly resolved B4, B5, H1, H2, H5, H6, H8–H10, H13, H14, M2–M4, M7 are closed in their laws. The built §1.11 sentence that frames sent and not acked count in the account is superseded for M1 by AP15.

v2.33 (lane store-m1-spec-fix-1629, the M1 review): text only, **not yet built**. §11 answers the astra review of v2.32 (`store-wholehog/m1-review-1625`): the admission unit becomes the distinct row version, so the account is independent of page partition and copy-on-write history and a delete-only commit is still never refused (B4, B5); a live-head `fork` syncs its FORK frame before it returns the branch (B1); relocation finds its closure by live parent links over physical frames, and open resolves only live edges by location (B2, B3); a collection-map leaf owns its collections' roots (B6); plus the fourteen high and eight medium fixes. Clauses AP1–AP21, laws L37–L51. See CHANGES-v2.33.

v2.32 (lane store-m1-spec-1618, the M1 target): text only, **not yet built**. §11 specifies the one format cutover of the store roadmap's M1 (`store-wholehog/MASTER-SPEC.md`): packed copy-on-write pages, per-collection roots, `PagePtr`, `BlobRef` with a 1 KiB inline threshold on 4 KiB leaves, the reserved `base` root and the journaled live-head `fork`, as clauses AP1–AP20 and laws L37–L48. §0–§10 still describe the store as built, and the M1 cutover slice flips §11 to built. See CHANGES-v2.32.

v2.31 (durable-cut spec lane; tape Durable mode is removed, so the store is the one durability contract): text only. `admit` now reserves its open entry before it commits, so §1.8 holds as written (AO1). §1.8's `Admission` comment no longer cites REPLAY-SPEC rule S3 (AO2), and §1.13 names the trigger refusal `StaleAttempt` and the run state "held" (AO3). See CHANGES-v2.31.

v2.30 (Sam's ruling C, auth-remove-1423; RUNNER-SPEC v2.19): the store does no authorization. Programs decide access with the Caller. AN1 deletes key scopes, the policy, the authority register and every scope digest. AN2 gives a stored reply 41 + R bytes and the format word 2. See CHANGES-v2.30.

v2.25 (gap study 1117 #6, lane service-ops-spec-1127): keyset pages over a Snapshot, `scan_page`/`find_page` with row and byte limits and an in-process continuation `Resume`, resumed by `scan_next`/`find_next` strictly after the last full logical key, with `Moved` for another Snapshot and `Stale(id)` for another incarnation (§1.5, L36). Text only; nothing about the format changes.

v2.24 (Sam ruled C on request identity, DECISIONS 2026-11-17; the rule is astra-req-digest/ADVICE.md, "C's complete rule" and "Byte cost"): the store binds each admitted position to a request digest. `admit` takes the digest; the `streams` value becomes (position, digest, Admitted | Replied(reply)); at an equal `p` the digest is compared before `Duplicate` or `Abandoned`, and a mismatch is `RequestMismatch`; below latest the answer is `HistoryUnavailable` (AH1, AH2). This replaces AG2's client-only rule. **The format changes:** a `streams` value grows by 32 bytes (AH3). No store writes a `streams` row yet: on main (f6d9de64) `store.hirsel` keeps an empty `streams` tree, and the runner's S5 stand-in (`core/programs/runner/runner.hirsel:349`) keeps positions in an ordinary table. So nothing migrates. **Decision of this version, not of the ruling:** the digest's byte encoding and the place of `request_digest` (a store function, so the runner and the store hash one way) are chosen here. Reversible until S5 lands; RUNNER-SPEC should cite §1.8 for the encoding rather than define one. The names `RequestMismatch` and `HistoryUnavailable` are RUNNER-SPEC v2.11.3's `Refusal` tags 6 and 7, mapped 1:1. The join-path checks (RUNNER H1 and V2) are RUNNER-SPEC v2.11.3's, referenced here and not restated. SHA-256 is std's, built by std-sha256-1071; this spec names it and does not specify its API. Model: new `model/model_v224_client.py` (RUN, receipt `model-v224-client-run.txt`). L35's digest values are RUN by Python's `hashlib`, a separate implementation.

v2.23 (wire-spec-1059 v2's items S-a, S-b and S-c, filed after wire-attack-1060's F6–F8 and F10): text only. Two parts: client position rules in §1.8, "Client positions" (AG1, AG2), and the published value of a row with `published_value`, plus `span_changes` for a Cover's initial rows, in §1.5 (AG3). F10 agrees with RUNNER-SPEC v2.11 §3 "Rows" and L16 `rows_normalized`, read at 23:37. The store source cited is BY READING `core/programs/store/store.hirsel` at 6c90185b: `row_value` :1811-1825, `put_impl` :1878-1884, `Change` and `diff_emit` :3587-3622, and the extractor erasure :1753-1760. The codec format is BY READING `core/std/api/codec.md`. No byte of the format changes. Model: new `model/model_v223_client.py` (RUN, receipt `model-v223-client-run.txt`). L34 is hand-derived. WIRE-SPEC should cite "STORE-SPEC §1.8 Client positions" from now on; line numbers move with each version.

v2.22 (the orchestrator's answers to v2.21's two questions, 2026-11-16): text only. AF1: every commit path settles unsettled charges before it checks `resident_limit`, as `run` does; `admit` and schema commits no longer skip it (§1.11, §1.8, L27). AF2: store-build-1010 builds v2.19; the v2.19 header is corrected. No byte of the format changes, and no model changes: the account is not in any model, and L27 is hand-derived.

v2.21 (the orchestrator's S5 rulings, 2026-11-16, the "orchestrator -> store-spec-1001" entry in followups.md): text only. Streams AE1–AE3 (§1.8, §1.11) and scopes AE4 (a)–(e) (§1.4). No byte of the format changes. Models, both RUN: `model/model_v221_streams.py` (`model_v24_streams.py` plus AE1–AE3; receipt `model-v221-streams-run.txt`) and `model/model_v221_scope.py`, which is new because no scope model existed (receipt `model-v221-scope-run.txt`).
