Built in S5.

No model changes. Built in the slice after S3; S3 lands as queued.

v2.29 (RUNNER-SPEC v2.18, lane caller-scope-1128, astra's third attack, ATTACK-3). This version is text and format; no store has been deployed, so the cutover is a fresh store.

- **`serve`'s contracts are completed:**
  - a refusal keeps the run (AM1);
  - a migration is one commit (AM2);
  - the first delivery comes back in a receipt (AM3);
  - errors before and after handoff are told apart (AM4);
  - the reserves are derived (AM5);
  - a concluded fault keeps its CallInfo (AM6);
  - the launch, namespaces and sizes are completed (AM7).
- **The store gains the release design's three hooks (AM8):** authority tables with their counter, `authorize`, and the counter's reader.
- **Two RUNNER-SPEC slices consume this version.** AM3 and AM8 are small and are built first, as slice RA. RS then takes AM1, AM2 and AM4–AM7, and RR builds on AM8 (RUNNER-SPEC §9). Neither RS nor RR waits on the other.

v2.28 (RUNNER-SPEC v2.17, lane caller-scope-1128, astra's second attack, ATTACK-2): text and format; no store has been deployed, so the cutover is a fresh store. `serve` runs an admitted position: it runs the policy and the body as two isolated executions over one snapshot and writes the reply, and the record when there is one, in the body's transaction; a fault keeps the run's digest in a `Pending` that `conclude` writes with the fixed reply (AL1). A duplicate returns the stored reply with its scope digest (AL2). The scope digest begins with the StoreId (AL3). `migrate` is its own phase over a `Migration`, and `Schema` only opens (AL4). The CALL section carries the authority bytes (AL5). The sizes follow the 32-byte scope digest, and `serve` keeps no read log (AL6).

## CHANGES-v2.90 (journal-tail-2187: no zero steps behind bulk groups)

| Item | v2.90 | Where |
|---|---|---|
| Bulk | B = 65,536, a build constant. The task counts the groups since its last group of B bytes or more, saturating at 64, and starts at 64. Both triggers (idle and the 64th-group END) run a step only when the count is 64. A cut or rotation does not reset it: it describes the load, not the segment | §3.3a When |
| Why | Measured on this host's ext4 `data=ordered`: a group of ≥ B bytes saves one extension (~76 µs) and costs ~2 µs per KiB of zeros, so the window loses past a few tens of KiB per group. Group writes at b ≤ 8 and at 97 B are under 32 KiB; at 4 KiB b = 64 they are 32–512 KiB | REPORT journal-tail-2187 |
| Durability | Removing steps changes no invariant: w moves only after a step's sync, groups go at e, T and the Disk bound are as v2.82. A bulk load past w extends the file, today's append | §3.3a |

## CHANGES-v2.84 (readonly-barrier-2178: the read-only open returns only durable frames)

- **Like sites.** `read_only_recover` reads with `Live`, the follower's durable live read. `JournalRead` loses its unsynced variant and `LiveDurable` is renamed `Live`.
- **Law.** S2 crash scenario `read-only-durable`, run once uncut through the W2 crash bridge next to `follower-durable`: at the read-only open's recovery stat of the terminal segment, the adapter writes the next COMMIT unsynced; the open must return head + 1 and the locked reopen after restart must keep it. The bridge's read-only guard now admits `sync` on a segment and still refuses every other operation.

## CHANGES-v2.83 (follower-durable-2172: a live log@1 capture publishes only durable frames)

- **Barrier.** `journal_recover` takes a `JournalRead`: `Locked` (the opener, under `LOCK`), `Live` (a read-only open) or `LiveDurable` (a log@1 follower). `LiveDurable` syncs each segment before its stat, as before, and syncs the terminal segment again on the same handle after its scan succeeds and before the capture is built. A middle segment needs no second sync: rotation synced it before the successor's HEADER named its `prev_end`. A size snapshot or a second MANIFEST read cannot replace the barrier: the frame lies inside the observed size, and MANIFEST does not change for a journal commit.
- **Witness.** The witness is taken before recovery and hashes a prefix of what recovery then reads, which the witness's own sync made durable. A stored witness can only be older than the capture's state, so the next capture sees a different witness and recovers again; it never reuses a state for bytes it did not apply.
- **Refresh.** `journal_recover` (both live reads) and `log_journal_witness` stat once more on the same handle when the selected boundary `d` (END's, or the successor HEADER's `prev_end`) is above the stat size, and only then call the segment Truncated. `Locked` does not refresh: with no writer, `d` > size is damage.
- **Cost.** One extra `sync(segment)` per follower capture that recovers (an unchanged witness still reuses the capture with no second sync). It is measured in the lane's REPORT.
- **Law.** L55's log@1 case gains two S2 crash scenarios in `store_crash_laws.hirsel`, run once each uncut through the W2 crash bridge, whose adapter plays the writer: at the recovery pass's stat of the terminal segment it writes the next COMMIT. `follower-durable` writes it unsynced before the stat returns; the restart keeps only fdatasynced bytes; the reopened head must be at least the follower's published head (red before the barrier: 161 recovered, 162 published). `follower-boundary` writes and syncs it with an END naming it after the stat took its size; the follower must publish it (red before the refresh: `Truncated`).

## CHANGES-v2.82 (journal-prealloc-spec-r2-2173: the zero window after astra's attack 2168; not yet built)

Astra's review of v2.80 (`journal-prealloc-attack-2168/ATTACK.md`) returned IMPLEMENT WITH FIXES. Each row answers one of its sections. The prize is unchanged: on this host's ext4 a synced 4 KiB overwrite of written blocks costs 20 µs and a synced append 96 µs (research-1124 `iouring-completion/PLAN.md`). This host mounts every ext4 file system `data=ordered` (`/proc/fs/ext4/*/options`), so the window is admitted where Store runs here.

| Item | v2.82 decision | Where |
|---|---|---|
| Writeback exposure (ATTACK §2, §5) | The window runs only where the host's `zero_extension(d)` probe says the provider guarantees zero-or-written extension: ext4 with a positive `data=ordered`/`data=journal` in `/proc/fs/ext4/<name>/options`, tmpfs, the sim fs and the crash bridge. Elsewhere T = 0, the same code as today's append. The mount property is probed, not a store option: the mount is outside input, and an option that is wrong on a writeback mount makes one crash refuse `open` | §3.3a Admission |
| Commit law (ATTACK §1) | acked ⊆ recovered ⊆ issued, recovered a commit-order prefix. This is the contract before v2.80. A window zero adds a way for an issued frame to recover before its ack. "Exactly acked" is not the law | §3.3a, §3.6, L86 |
| Follower witness (ATTACK §6, Other) | The walk rule below. The live-capture durability barrier and the size refresh are follower-durable-2172's rule, and the window's implementation lands after it | §3.3a, below |
| Disk (ATTACK §7) | T rounds down: T = min(W, ⌊f / 4,096⌋ × 4,096) ≤ f, and a step never writes past e + T. So bytes past a segment's frames are at most its frame bytes, at every cut, with no per-segment term. No truncate on close or cut. v2.80's Σ(T_i + 4,096), its factor of two and its one-window `ENOSPC` claim are withdrawn | §3.3a Disk, §11.11 C-4 |
| Step size (ATTACK §7 SPEC-TEXT) | s = min(E, T − (w − e)), never zero when due. §3.2's "write E" becomes s. "Allocates nothing" is narrowed to the step and its chunk | §3.2, §3.3a |
| Latency (ATTACK §8) | A frame's ack can wait behind the step already running plus each 64th-group step due ahead of it. The one-step claim and "once per E of frames" are withdrawn. The gate measures queue-inclusive burst latency (IMPL) | §3.3a Delay |
| Unused-space damage (ATTACK Other) | Media damage turning the first window byte into a reserved kind makes `open` refuse. Stated, not repaired | §3.3a |
| Recycling (ATTACK §3) | The exclusion is narrowed to retaining unsanitized payload. A scrub-then-publish scheme is reader-compatible and not taken | §3.3a |
| L86 (ATTACK SPEC-TEXT) | The reader's byte-image contract. Writer offsets, ack counts and the bound past e move to the real-worker cases | §5 L86 |

**The follower witness (v2.82).** `log_journal_witness` (`store-manifest.hirsel` 894) must make equal witnesses imply equal recovery results, so that a reused capture is the capture a fresh read would make. Middle segments are unchanged: they hash [0, `prev_end`). For the terminal segment:
1. Read the END boundary first and hash it, as today. Call it d, or 72 when there is no good END.
2. **A short HEADER.** When the size is below 72, hash a short-header tag, the size and the bytes [0, size), and stop. There is no walk and no 72-byte extent. The existing recovery rule decides what such a segment means (§3.4 step 5).
3. **The walk.** From 72, read heads, hash [0, z) and stop at the first position z where one of these holds:
   - **short:** fewer than 16 bytes remain before the size;
   - **zero:** the head's kind is 0;
   - **no fit:** the head's kind is nonzero and its frame (16 bytes plus its `len`, §2.1) runs past the size.

   A frame that fits is stepped over and hashed, whatever its kind, flags or checksum. The walk raises no reader error: it never calls `disk_read_frame_head`. So a reserved kind, a bad flag or an oversize declaration is bound to the witness as bytes and left to recovery.
4. **The stop record.** Hash z. Then:
   - if z ≥ d and the stop is short or zero, hash one canonical torn token;
   - otherwise, hash the stop's class, the bytes present in [z, min(z + 16, size)), and the size.

   Recovery reads a short or zero head at or past `d` as the torn tail, whatever its bytes. So zeros a step adds past the stop leave the witness unchanged. Below `d` the same heads are different errors (`Truncated`, `Corrupt`), and a full nonzero head that does not fit can be `Unsupported`, `Flags`, `Malformed` or torn depending on its bytes. Those are bound, never canonicalized. ATTACK 2168 §6's images A (16 zeros at e) and B (a reserved-kind head declaring one byte, with only the head present) differ.
5. **Recovery's stop is at or before z.** Recovery also stops at a fitting frame with a bad check. Where it stops, and with which result, is then a function of the hashed bytes [0, z), the stop record and d.
6. **Reads.** One 64 KiB chunk is kept with its offset. The walk reads the next chunk only when a head or the hashed range passes the chunk's end, and each byte is read once. A head that straddles two chunks is assembled from both. A host read shorter than requested is continued and is not EOF before the size.
7. **Live capture.** The witness covers the bytes the capture actually read. follower-durable-2172 makes those bytes durable on the same handle before a live follower publishes, and refreshes the size on that handle before it concludes truncation. v2.82 builds on that rule and does not restate it. The window widens the window of that race: frames written in place fall inside the stat size. So the window's implementation lands only after 2172's SHA is on main.

**The export copy (v2.80, completed).** For each middle segment, `copy_log_export` copies exactly its successor HEADER's `prev_end`, read through `journal_header` (`store-manifest.hirsel` 859). An early EOF is an error. A successor HEADER that is missing or torn while the lease holds the range is also an error: export withholds EXPORT completion and propagates it. The lease keeps every successor it needs, including the captured terminal.

**L86 in full.** The §5 row is the summary.
- **Images.** HEADER(role 2, seq, `prev_end`), A acked COMMIT frames, then one group of three or more COMMIT frames, one of them larger than 4 KiB. Each image is a resulting byte string R:
  - The fresh kind keeps group bytes [0, k) and ends the file there.
  - The windowed kind uses w − e ∈ {0, 1, a group's length − 1, a multiple of 4,096 up to W}. It keeps bytes [0, k) and leaves the rest of the window zero.
  - Page-subset images keep a later 4,096-byte page of the group and zero an earlier one.
  - k runs over every frame boundary, ±1 around each, and every 4,096-byte page boundary inside the group.
  - Each image is written once with `j-<seq>.end` naming the acked end, and once with no END.
- **Missing bytes that equal zero.** One group frame is ATTACK 2168's 42-byte empty COMMIT for the next branch version, whose last four payload bytes are zero (`store_crash_laws.hirsel` 814). The images hold its first 41, 40 and 38 bytes over zeros. The same cuts are also built with the file ending there. Every other frame is chosen so that the byte at each chosen cut is nonzero, so a case meant to stop stops.
- **Expected.** Walk the group's frames in order at their known offsets. A frame is present when R holds exactly the bytes the law encoded for it at its offset, with every byte inside the file. Recovery applies the A acked frames plus the group's frames up to the first absent one. The law computes this from its own encoded frames and R. It never calls `journal_scan`, `journal_read_frame` or `disk_read_frame_head`. So the 41-of-42 image over zeros recovers that COMMIT, and the same cut at EOF does not. The ordinary case, the whole group present before its ack, is one of the images.
- **Witness clause.** These images are built by hand, with no writer:
  - two images that differ only in zeros past the stop at or past `d` have equal witnesses;
  - images A and B above have different witnesses;
  - two images that differ only in a frame written in place over zeros have different witnesses;
  - two terminal segments shorter than 72 bytes and of different lengths have different witnesses.
- **What moved to the real-worker cases.** No byte image can show these writer invariants:
  - where a group is written (at e, never at w);
  - when w moves (after the step's sync);
  - that window bytes are never counted in `Journaled`, U or `d`;
  - the bound past e;
  - which operation a failed step stops at.

  A `window` scenario in the crash laws (`store_crash_laws.hirsel` `disk_crash_main`), with L3, L8, L10 and the journal worker laws, observes write offsets, ack counts and file sizes (IMPL).

**Prior art (v2.82).**
- **TigerBeetle** (`/tmp/ref-tigerbeetle` a2a3c70) writes every WAL slot at format time (`src/vsr/replica_format.zig` `queue_format_wal`). It then writes with O_DSYNC, and with O_DIRECT where the file system supports it (`src/stdx/io/linux.zig` 1456, 1529, 1563). It uses `fallocate` only to reserve space and warns that without it "an ENOSPC will panic". So it never relies on what a file system exposes past a crash-time EOF: every byte it reads back was written first. ADAPTED: the window also writes before it relies, and keeps rotating segments and the format. The written zeros' own extension needs the provider guarantee, which TigerBeetle avoids by formatting once.
- **RocksDB** (`/tmp/ref-rocksdb` 8582ee9) preallocates WAL space with `fallocate(FALLOC_FL_KEEP_SIZE)` (`env/io_posix.cc` 1674), which does not move i_size, so a crash cannot expose it. It truncates the extra space when the file is closed (`include/rocksdb/options.h`, `allow_fallocate`). Its reader skips a zero-type, zero-length record from preallocated regions (`db/log_reader.cc` 611). `recycle_log_file_num` needs recyclable records that carry the log number. Open disables it with `kTolerateCorruptedTailRecords` and `kAbsoluteConsistency` (`db/db_impl/db_impl_open.cc` 109–125), because it relies on reading a corrupt record as the end of new data.
  - KEEP_SIZE preallocation: REPLACED. It measured 69 µs per synced write here, because the unwritten extents still convert at the first sync.
  - Truncate at close: NOT TAKEN. The target rule bounds what it would reclaim, and a crash before it keeps the bytes anyway.
  - Recycling: NOT TAKEN, as above.
- **Goose** has no WAL preallocation (CHANGES-v2.80): nothing to take.

**Format verdict:** NONE. `DISK_FORMAT` is unchanged. A reader of any version opens a directory with or without windows.

Reversibility: code. The probe and the target are one function each, and dropping the window is T = 0.

## CHANGES-v2.80 (journal-prealloc-spec-2153: a zero window ahead of the journal's write head; not yet built)

**Revised by v2.82** (CHANGES-v2.82). The Window target, Extension size, Crash-cut stale-block row, Disk bound, Memory claim, the witness row and L86 below are superseded there. The rest stands.

IOC-3, step 1 of the io_uring roadmap (research-1124 `iouring-completion/PLAN.md`). Measured on this host's ext4: a synced 4 KiB append is 96 µs, a synced overwrite of written blocks 20 µs, and `fallocate` alone 69 µs, because its unwritten extents still convert at the first sync.

| Item | Change | Where |
|---|---|---|
| Window | The journal task keeps [e, w) of its open segment as zeros it wrote and synced, where e is the frame end. It writes each group with `write_at` at e, over the window where the window covers the group, and extending the file past w otherwise. Target T = min(W, ⌈(e − 72) / 4,096⌉ × 4,096), with W = max(E, ⌈`group_bytes` / E⌉ × E) and E = 1,048,576 | §3.3a |
| Extension | A step runs when w − e < T/2. It writes s = min(E, ⌈(T − (w − e)) / 4,096⌉ × 4,096) zero bytes at w, in writes of at most 32,768 bytes from one zero chunk the task allocated at spawn, and then syncs. w moves only after the sync. Steps run while the queue is empty, before each blocking `receive`, with a `try_receive` before each step, and as one step at each 64th-group END. No step runs before a segment's first frame. `disk_new_segment` writes no window | §3.3a, §3.3 |
| Counters | The window is not frame bytes. `Journaled(n, bytes)`, U, F, `since`, `d` and `prev_end` count frames only, as before | §3.3a, §11.11 |
| Recovery | Unchanged rule. A zero head is kind 0, a bad frame, so in the last segment recovery stops there (the torn tail), and below `d` it is `Corrupt`. Middle segments end at `prev_end`, and the window lies past it. A window never makes a segment shorter than `d` | §3.4 step 5 |
| Crash cuts | A step cut before its sync leaves the region past w absent, zero or (only under `data=writeback`) stale, which is the same residual as a torn append today. A group cut inside the window leaves any subset of its pages over zeros, and recovery stops at the first frame not wholly present | §3.6 |
| Failure | A failed step write or sync poisons the store, `ENOSPC` included (§3.8's one rule) | §3.8 |
| Disk | The journal cap is unchanged, since U was never a file size. C-4 adds a bound: a segment with f frame bytes carries fewer than min(W, ⌈f / 4,096⌉ × 4,096) + 4,096 ≤ f + 8,192 window bytes past its last frame. Windows are never truncated. A segment with no frame (an open's rotation followed by a cut, or a refused cut) has none. So the windows of the live segments total at most their frame bytes plus 8 KiB per segment | §11.11 C-4 |
| Memory | The zero chunk is 32,768 bytes inside the worker's existing `JOURNAL_WORKER_SLACK` (65,536). No grant figure changes. The worker allocates nothing after spawn (Q1a) | §3.3a, §11.11 |

**What each file-size reader uses (v2.80).** These are all the places that read a journal segment's size. Store-reexec's two scans (`store-reexec.hirsel` 57 and 130) and the object and Bucket paths have no local segments or read none by size.

| Reader | Today | With the window |
|---|---|---|
| `journal_input` (`store.hirsel` 6261), feeding `journal_scan` (6354) in `journal_recover` (6565), `read_only_state` (5544) and `replay_export` (4348) | `stat` size. It bounds `journal_prefix`. It is the scan's stop on the last segment, `d > size` is `Truncated`, and a middle segment stops at `prev_end` | unchanged. The last segment's scan stops at the first zero head, at or before the size. `d ≤ size` still holds, because the window only adds bytes past frames |
| `replay_export`'s terminal check (`store.hirsel` 4363: size > boundary is `Malformed`) | reads the exported terminal copy | unchanged. The export copies the terminal segment through `stamp.end` only, so the copy has no window |
| `log_journal_witness` (`store-manifest.hirsel` 894), used by `log_follow_capture` (`store-followers.hirsel` 78) to reuse a capture | hashes the terminal segment over [0, size) | hashes the terminal segment over [0, z). z is where a walk of frame heads from 72 stops: at the first head with kind 0, or the first one whose frame does not fit inside the size. The walk checks no checksum, so recovery's stop is at most z, and equal witnesses still imply equal recovered states. A step that only adds zeros leaves z, and so the witness, unchanged. Hashing to the size would recapture after every step. Hashing to `d` would be unsound, because `d` lags the frames |
| `copy_log_export` (`store-export.hirsel` 124) | copies each middle segment to EOF and the terminal one through `stamp.end` | copies each middle segment through its successor's `prev_end`, read from the successor's HEADER. A window copied whole would add up to W + 4,096 dead bytes per segment. Replay already ignores them |

**Recycling retired segments (rename plus HEADER rewrite): NOT TAKEN.** A recycled file holds an earlier segment's frames past the new frame end. They fail the new segment's checksum, because the check binds `(store, role, seq)` and the offset, so a stale frame is never applied. But `disk_read_frame_head` (`store.hirsel` 4072) returns `Unsupported` for kinds 0x0020, 0x0030 and 0x0050 before it reads any checksum, and `journal_scan` passes every error except `Corrupt` straight out. The scan's stop can land inside an old frame's payload, where the bytes are user data. A `20 00` there makes `open`, `read_only_state` and both re-exec scans refuse a healthy store. Making a reserved kind past `d` count as torn would change the reader rule, so an older reader could refuse a store a newer one opened. IOC-3 requires reversibility with no format change, so (1b) waits for a design that pairs recycling with a format-versioned reader rule. RocksDB's `recycle_log_file_num` needed a new record format with the log number in every record for the same reason.

**Prior art.**
- TigerBeetle preallocates a fixed WAL at format time and writes it with O_DSYNC. ADAPTED: Hirsel keeps rotating segments and grows a bounded window inside each one, so the format and segment rotation stay as they are.
- RocksDB's `recycle_log_file_num` (recyclable record types that carry the log number): NOT TAKEN, for the reasons above.
- Goose (`/tmp/ref-goose` d5ae5bb) has no WAL preallocation (no `fallocate`, no preallocation, no `fdatasync`): NOT TAKEN, nothing to take.
- `fallocate(FALLOC_FL_KEEP_SIZE)` alone is REPLACED by written zeros: it measured 69 µs per synced write, because the first sync still converts the unwritten extents.

**Law.** L86 in §5 (`journal_window_prefix`). Format verdict: NONE. `DISK_FORMAT` is unchanged.

Reversibility: code. An older build opens a directory with windows, and a newer one opens a directory without them.

## CHANGES-v2.77 (s3-any-provider-2111: any S3-compatible endpoint)

Sam (2026-11-24): "for the brittle S3 allow list ... remove all of that garbage. We should never have an allow list like that." It supersedes v2.47's provider qualification (astra #17, Fable S2) and v2.66's "No other provider rule changes".

| Item | Change | Where |
|---|---|---|
| Deleted | Host matching (`s3.<region>.amazonaws.com`, `fly.storage.tigris.dev`, `t3.storage.dev`), the AWS `GetBucketEncryption` gate, the Tigris `GetBucketLocation` allow-list, and `Unqualified("unknown")`, `aws:*` and `tigris:*`. `std.object.bucket_encryption` and `std.object.endpoint_host` go with them: nothing else used them | §12.2 |
| Transport | Every open still checks the live client before any write. A non-loopback endpoint must be `https` (`Endpoint(Insecure)`), because the service identity is the TLS-authenticated host. A loopback client must carry the simulator's proof (`Endpoint(Unproven)`). sim@1 still needs a `std.sim.s3` client (`Endpoint(NotSimulator)`). These checks concern what the client is, not which company runs the endpoint | §12.1, §12.2 |
| Protocol | Genesis step 5a: after it creates entry 1 and before the head exists, genesis sends entry 1's exact bytes back with `If-None-Match: *` and again with `If-Match` on a tag no object carries. Both must be refused. An `Applied` reply is `Endpoint(IgnoresIfNoneMatch)` or `Endpoint(IgnoresIfMatch)`. The probe is safe because the entry is immutable: an endpoint that ignores the condition rewrites identical bytes. A refused genesis leaves only debris, which the next genesis skips | §12.2, §12.3 genesis |
| Not probed | An open of an existing store does not probe. Its entries may already be reclaimed, and a correct provider would then apply the create, so a probe there could refuse a correct endpoint. Moving a store to another endpoint is the operator's act, and the requirement below is theirs to meet | §12.2 |
| Consistency | Conditional writes must be linearizable for the bucket as every writer and follower sees it. Single-region buckets and buckets the provider documents as globally strongly consistent meet this. For a bucket that is strongly consistent only within a region (for example Tigris Global and Dual-region), every writer and follower must use the same region endpoint. The store does not check brands. The operator is responsible | §12.2, AV14 |
| Encryption | The AWS SSE-KMS / SSE-C refusal is deleted. The store never treats an ETag as a digest: it compares tags only for equality, and Q5 reads back the bytes after a 412. What it needs is the non-recurrence requirement, which is now a provider requirement and not an AWS fact | §12.2 |
| Errors | `Unqualified(String)` is replaced by `Endpoint(EndpointRefusal)`, with `EndpointRefusal = Insecure \| Unproven \| NotSimulator \| IgnoresIfNoneMatch \| IgnoresIfMatch`. Pre-1.0: no compatibility path | §12.1 |

- **Prior art.** Apache Arrow `object_store` (used by SlateDB and delta-rs): `S3ConditionalPut::ETagMatch` is a configuration choice that the operator makes for R2, MinIO and AWS, and nothing probes it (`object_store-0.14.2/src/aws/precondition.rs:120`). ADAPTED: like it, Hirsel needs no brand list. Unlike it, Hirsel refuses an endpoint that visibly ignores the condition, because objects@1 has no option to turn conditional writes off. Its weakness, a misconfigured endpoint that silently splits the head, is what the probe catches at genesis.
- **Rejected.** (1) Probing on `head`: on an endpoint that ignores the condition, the probe would itself overwrite a newer head. (2) Probing at every open: a reclaimed entry turns a correct reply into a refusal. (3) Keeping AWS's encryption gate: no rule reads an ETag as a body digest.
- Reversibility: code and text. The probe is two requests at genesis and can be removed on its own.

## CHANGES-v2.74.2 (store-spec-small-2097: the set_residency payer and L64(e)'s premise; not yet built)

Spec text only. It closes the two items CHANGES-v2.74 left open. It supersedes v2.74's sentences that the Tiered → Memory load "is not a cut" and "takes no cut pin" (CHANGES-v2.74's Tiered → Memory row and open item 2, and §13.8). Nothing goes to Sam.

| # | Finding | Fix | Where |
|---|---|---|---|
| 2083 | **2083 open: set_residency payer; L64(e) premise.** (1) `set_residency` Tiered → Memory "loads every page of the collection through the pager" (§13.8), and no text named who pays for those loads, their pins or the images they build. (2) L64(e) assumed the writer partition is full of a held call's pins when the fold runs. §13.3 lets no call enter held mode while an overlay exists, so the fold can meet only parked calls' pins | (1) The load is a **load step** of the schema commit's own cut, and it is paid as below. (2) L64(e)'s premise reads "full of parked calls' pins". The claim and the `fold_on_writer` mutant are unchanged | §13.2, §13.8, §13.11, §14.3, L64 |

| Item | Change | Where |
|---|---|---|
| Already a cut | Every schema commit is cut-made and publishes only when its cut reaches AP16 step 5 (§11.12 "Who sees a refusal"). `set_residency` is a schema commit (§13.8). So the load needs no new path: it runs inside that cut while the writer is paused, and it is a cut's read like any other | §13.8 |
| Load step | Before the cut's plan reads the collection, the load walks the collection's Cold tree top-down. Each step resolves one Cold slot, a PAGE or a BLOB, as a cut pin. It copies the verified payload into a fresh Hot image, rewrites the Hot parent's slot to `Hot`, and releases the pin before the next step. If the collection holds a replay overlay, this cut is the fold cut (§13.7), and the fold applies the overlay first, so the load reads the folded tree | §13.8 |
| Pins | K, the cut pin quota. A load step pins one frame, at most `BLOB_PIN` = 16,711,704 bytes, which is below K = 19,944,296. A cut runs one step at a time, so one load step's pin always fits K. Read-ahead goes through §13.2's hints, unpinned | §13.2, §13.8 |
| Images and walk state | The paused writer's own limit (C2): the Hot images of every page and blob the collection reaches, the descent stack and the slot rewrites. These are the cut's fresh pages. They are bounded by the collection's own bytes, which the admission below counted. An allocation refusal refuses the cut with `Full(Allocation(c))` and loses nothing: the collection stays Tiered, and the images are dropped with the candidate | §13.11 |
| GET buffers | The pager task, as for every cut demand (§13.11) | §13.11 |
| Admission | Unchanged, before the cut: the change is admitted against `resident_limit` from `account_tiered`'s figure for the collection, else `Full(Resident(Capacity { requested, available }))` with nothing changed. This is §11.12's "refused by its own admission before the cut" | §13.8 |
| Corrupt | A Cold load that fails §13.7's checks is `Corrupt` for that read. `set_residency` returns that error, and the cut publishes nothing | §13.8 |
| Terms | No §1.1 or §13.8 term changes. K already bounds one pin, C + K + D + P stay reserved as the plan reserved them at open, and the admission already counts the collection's bytes | §1.1, §13.8 |
| Pause | `probe_budget` and `free_budget` do not bound the load. The pause grows with the collection's Cold bytes, which the caller chose to load. No option is added | §13.8 |
| Memory store | On a memory store Tiered is `Tiered(Memory)`, which has no Cold slot. Nothing is loaded and nothing is pinned | §13.8 |
| Cell | `set_residency` on a Cell is `Missing("residency")` before any admission or load, so it pays nothing | §14.3 |
| L64(e) | "with the writer partition full of a held call's pins" becomes "with the writer partition full of parked calls' pins (up to all of `writer_pin_bytes`, the room a held call owns alone, §13.3 "Termination")". The claim, "the fold still completes, and no caller's `requested` changes", and the mutant `fold_on_writer` are unchanged | L64 |

- **Prior art.** bbolt (ADOPTED, as in v2.74): reads on the paused writer need no budget of their own beyond the step's. SQLite's `VACUUM INTO` and RocksDB's `CompactRange` (ADAPTED): a whole-collection rewrite runs as one long foreground operation whose cost is the collection's size, not a background budget. Hirsel takes the same shape and charges the images to the writer. Goose (NOT TAKEN): it has no paged tier, so it has no residency change.
- **Rejected.** (1) A load outside any cut, on the Db task between calls. It would need its own payer for pins and a second publish path, while a schema commit already has a cut. (2) Charging the load's pins to `writer_pin_bytes`. A parked call's pins would then refuse a residency change, as `fold_on_writer` shows for the fold. (3) Charging the images to `dirty_bytes`. A Memory collection's images are not dirty Tiered pages, and a collection larger than `dirty_bytes` / 2 could then never move. (4) A new `load_budget` Option to bound the pause. The caller asked for the whole collection, so a budget could only turn the call into a refusal.
- **Open items 1 and 2 of CHANGES-v2.74 are closed** by this version.
- Reversibility: text only. The load step's shape can change before §13 is built.

## CHANGES-v2.74.1 (sim level world ownership)

The task that creates a `LevelWorld` owns its CONC-1 task handle. Dropping an unjoined task cancels the world. Each open gets a sendable `LevelSync` (§12.1).

## CHANGES-v2.74 (store-cut-payer-2083: the cut pin quota; not yet built)

Spec text only. The orchestrator ruled the shape. Cuts stay inline on the paused writer, as v2.38 (Sam's gavel ruling 1, rows C1, C2 and C4) made them. No checkpoint task, channel or open-time cut reserve with credit comes back. Nothing goes to Sam.

| # | Finding | Fix | Where |
|---|---|---|---|
| 2010 | **2010 fold funding: stale checkpoint-task payer after C2.** v2.38 deleted the checkpoint task, its channel, its partition and `checkpoint_task_bytes`. The §13 and §14 text that v2.59–v2.64 added still charged a Tiered cut's reads to "the checkpoint task's partition" or "limit". These were the fold's GETs, the materialization walk, the probe's PACK_INDEX and interior reads, the `deferred_frees` drain and a trim's Hot-share walk. §1.1 and §13.8 never defined that payer, so the fold had no funded room. Its reads could not go on the writer partition either: L64(e) requires the fold to finish while that partition is full of a call's pins | a **cut pin quota** K. The Db task owns its admission. K is reserved once inside r at open and backs the pager's allocations of cut-pinned frames. It is separate from `writer_pin_bytes` and from the shared side. Every Cold frame a cut pins is admitted against K alone, never against a call's or a Reader's partition | §1.1, §13.2, §13.5, §13.6, §13.7, §13.8, §13.11, L64, §14.7, §14.10, §14.12 |

| Item | Change | Where |
|---|---|---|
| Size | K = `CUT_PIN_BYTES` = 4 × `HEIGHT_MAX` × `PAGE_PIN` + `INDEX_PIN` + `BLOB_PIN` = 4 × 65 × 8,400 + 1,048,592 + 16,711,704 = **19,944,296** bytes. The terms are: `PAGE_PIN` = 8,384 + 16, the largest PAGE pin; `HEIGHT_MAX` = 1 + ⌈log₂(2⁶⁴ − 1)⌉ = 65, AP3's height bound for u64 row counts; `INDEX_PIN` = 1 MiB + 16, one PACK_INDEX pin; and `BLOB_PIN` = 16,711,688 + 16, one BLOB pin. It is a constant of format 5, not an `Options` field | §13.8 "The cut pin quota" |
| Proof | A cut works one **step** at a time, and a cut pin never outlives its step. Whatever a walk carries to its next step it copies into its own state on the writer's limit: keys, stamps, parent entries and the values it compares. Within a step a cut pins one root's pages in one Tiered tree, at most 4h of them, plus one PACK_INDEX frame and one BLOB frame. The 4h covers each kind of step. A merge step (one overlay key) pins the path and each path page's left and right sibling, at most 3h. A trim pins at most 4h touches (§14.5), and a trim evidence row two boundary paths (2h). A probe, closure or compaction descent pins one path (h), and a drain step one page. **The cut's settle** (AP15's n-way walk, AP16 item 2) advances one root's cursor per step and pins that root's path (h) and the one BLOB it compares. So R participating roots cost R cursors of walk state, never R paths of pins. Read-ahead goes through unpinned hints. `probe_budget` and `free_budget` bound how many steps a cut takes, never how much it pins at once | §13.8 |
| Floor | r ≥ C + K + D + P + 64 MiB in §1.1's Tiered plan, `sized`, §13.8's floor and the Memory → Tiered promotion check. A store with no Tiered collection reserves no K | §1.1, §13.8 |
| Over quota | Never, by construction. A frame over its kind's payload limit fails its load checks before it is pinned. A frame that passes AP20's checks is our writer's output (§2.8), so its tree obeys AP3's occupancy, h ≤ `HEIGHT_MAX`. A step's pins therefore fit K. A cut has no `Cache` refusal, and the fold's capacity refusals are `Resident` and `Allocation` only | §13.3, §13.7, §13.8 |
| Other payers | GET range buffers stay with the pager. Walk state, queues and lists stay on the paused writer's own limit (C2): merge and descent stacks, the probe cursor, drain-queue edits, the replaced and dropped lists the fold rebuilds, and the Hot-share walk. An allocation refusal there refuses the cut and loses nothing (AP16). Merged and copied pages are this cut's fresh pages, and the overlay's `dirty_bytes` charge does **not** cover them. An entry costs its key, its value and 36 bytes, while one key can rewrite up to 3h pages of up to 8,384 bytes. They are on the writer's own limit too: the Hot images of the path being merged, and the encoded frames in AP16's one output buffer | §13.7, §13.11 |
| Steps | A cut's steps are the Db task's, outside every call's bound (AP16). The pager and the journal task keep their own tasks | §13.11 |
| Tiered → Memory | `set_residency`'s page load (§13.8) is not a cut. It takes no cut pin, and v2.74 does not change its payer (open item 2). **Superseded by v2.74.2:** the load is a load step of the schema commit's cut, on K | §13.8 |
| Stale sites | CHANGES-v2.63's fold-cut row and open item 4, §1.1's open-refund list, §13.5 step 3 and its closing paragraph, §13.6's probe, §13.7's fold cut, §13.11's table and the sentence under it, L64(e) (case and mutant unchanged; only the payer's name changes), §14.7's payers, §14.10's Hot-share walk and §14.12's freeing sentence | as listed |

- **Prior art.** bbolt (ADOPTED, as in v2.38): a commit's rebalance and spill run on the one writer while it holds the lock, and read pages straight from the mmap with no budget. Hirsel keeps the paused writer. It has no mmap to lean on, so it funds the reads explicitly. RocksDB `compaction_readahead_size` (ADAPTED): compaction reads come from a buffer separate from the block cache, so compaction cannot evict or starve foreground reads. Hirsel keeps that separation. But RocksDB makes it a tunable size, and Hirsel's quota is a constant proven from the walk, so no setting can make a cut fail. Goose (NOT TAKEN): §10's Goose row is a verified loader that reads a whole file. It has no paged tier and no cut read budget to compare.
- **Rejected.** (1) A new `cut_pin_bytes` Option. A bound exists, so a setting could only be set too low and turn a fold into a refusal. (2) Charging cut reads to `writer_pin_bytes`. That fails L64(e) and is the `fold_on_writer` mutant. (3) Taking cut reads from the shared side. A full cache or the live Reader grants would then refuse a fold, which makes room depend on other callers. (4) A pinned read-ahead window, whether fixed or doubling like a call's. A doubling window grows with the walk's length, so no constant could bound it, and §13.2's unpinned hints already give the read-ahead. (5) A settle that holds one pinned path per participating root. Its pins would grow with pins and forks, so K could not be a constant. (6) A new level ≤ 64 decode rule. AP3's occupancy already bounds the height of every tree our writer emits (§2.8).
- **Open item 2 (closed by v2.74.2: the load is a step of the schema commit's cut, its pins on K, its images on the paused writer's limit).** `set_residency` Tiered → Memory "loads every page of the collection through the pager" (§13.8), and no text names the payer of those loads. It is outside this finding: it is not a cut, and it is admitted against `resident_limit` before it changes anything. It needs its own ruling before §13 is built.
- **Open item 1 (closed by v2.74.2: L64(e)'s premise now reads "parked calls' pins").** §13.3 lets no call enter held mode while an overlay exists, and AP16's Drain waits for a held call to end. So when the fold starts, the writer partition can be full of *parked* calls' pins, but never a held call's. L64(e)'s premise is read that way. The case and its `fold_on_writer` mutant are unchanged, and only the payer named in case (e) is substituted. The ruling's reason ("a held call owns `writer_pin_bytes` alone") does not literally arise at the fold. The quota still holds, because parked calls' pins can fill the writer partition just as well.
- Reversibility: text only. K's terms can change before §13 is built. A format change to PAGE, PACK_INDEX or BLOB limits changes K with it.

## CHANGES-v2.73.1 (law schedules)

- **L55-1(i).** Hold only A's finite range of head PUT attempts from 9, with `count` equal to A's attempts and `until: Never`. B's claim PUT is outside the hold.
- **L56-W.** Open the follower before the GET of `mark-1`. Its first `at_least` returns `Behind` at 6 because it already applied the main branch (§12.5). The second observes 7.
- **Runner L8.** A write to a key read before the step stop fences that session. Stale content fails the release check and the client resubscribes (RUNNER §3). A write to an unrelated key does not move the fence.

## CHANGES-v2.73 (m2-open-spec-2072: weak sim levels, the holder's fresh read, the owed M2 cases, a stopped look's reads)

Spec text only. Every ruling is reversible: sim@1 stores exist only in laws, Q2 describes the built journal, and the `Looked` change is a source type. No recorded byte of an objects@1 or log@1 store changes. Nothing goes to Sam.

| Item | Ruling | The /wholehog end-state | Where |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Q1, weak sim levels** | sim@1 is the objects@1 protocol, unchanged, over a `std.sim.s3` client. Its register is the sim service the law starts once: it persists across every open, crash and holder change, and every client made from its `Endpoint` reaches it. The grant carries the ordered declaration (`SimLevels`) and a `LevelSync`, the open's endpoint to a law-owned `LevelWorld` that holds or completes each weak level's barrier and logs ground truth. A weak level survives 0 only. `survives` 2 on a weak level is refused by construction: a holder change keeps exactly the head chain, and "the head is B" is Q5's only commit evidence, so a weak level that survived would be a second commit point. `survives` 1 names no event, because every sim@1 writer open claims. | One protocol, one register, one commit point. sim@1 differs from objects@1 only in its declaration and its weak barriers, never in how a commit becomes durable. A register with a second frontier, or a `Plan` that carries a register, would be a second protocol to qualify. | §1.1, §12.1, §12.2, §12.4 |
| **Q2, the holder's fresh read** | There is no overlap. The journal task makes one authority request at a time over its one `std.object.Requests`. `fresh_snapshot` drains `durable`, then the journal makes one head GET between advances. An advance is decided only by its own Q5 replies. A fresh GET or an idle poll decides only the holder's pair for later work. A one-lane Bucket is valid. No second transport exists, so §12.9's two head buffers stay exact. Creating entry p + 2 while p + 1's head write is outstanding is withdrawn until a measurement asks for it. | One decision point with one request in flight, which is what the built journal already does. Concurrent completions would buy a race that §12.3 then has to referee, and "first evidence wins" was that referee. | §12.3, §12.5, §12.9, L57 |
| **Q3, qualification** | §12.10 "Owed cases (v2.73)" gives each owed case its schedule, home, oracle and the bug only it catches. `std.sim.s3` gains request holds and an application-order log, which L52, L54, L55, L57, L59 and VP1 use. L59 moves to the objects@1 laws. | Each law is built once, in its named harness, with oracles that come from outside the store. | §12.10 |
| **`Looked`'s reads** (RUNNER v2.55.1's open "Read sets" gap) | Every early end of `look` carries the read log up to its end: `Failed(Causes, List[Read])`, `Refused(Capacity, List[Read])` and `Stopped(OutOfTime, List[Read])`. A read is logged before its value reaches the body. The log lives outside the isolate and the `steps` scope, and `look` reads no bound. A covering gap is rejected. | Like ends alike: a look has a read set however it ends, so a write into anything it read fences it. A covering gap would fence on every write. | §1.5, the M1 types, L32 |

**Why a partial read set is enough.** Up to the stop, the body's path is a function of `s` and the values its reads returned. The step count is a per-build fuel bound and is not observable (§13.0 Q1, MEM-30). So a write that touches no logged key leaves every value the body saw unchanged, and a rerun under the same bound takes the same path and stops at the same point. A rerun under a larger bound is a new run with its own read set. Fencing on the partial set therefore misses no change that could alter the outcome, and fences nothing the body did not read.

## CHANGES-v2.72

D1 amendment: open performs no checkpoint; a due cut runs at the first commit. Every open refusal precedes the transport handoff or recovers it from the unsent startup message, so every Refusal returns the Authority whole. Refusal and Authority are task-affine (not send), as §1.10 requires of the Client's Workers.

## CHANGES-v2.71 (follower retirement and unchanged log captures)

- A closed follower command or service endpoint reports `Retired` (§12.5, L85). A provider that reports `Closed` still gives `Io(Closed)`. The §12.5 accounting is unchanged.
- Log catch-up verifies the synced MANIFEST and journal input witness. When both match, it revalidates the referenced packs and keeps the previous capture without building another AP15 state. Historical floor recovery admits the historical roots beside the published capture.

## CHANGES-v2.70 (store-spec-d1d2-2058; with RUNNER-SPEC v2.54's D1 and D2, whose referents are fixed in place)

| Item | Change | Where |
|---|---|---|
| D1: a refused open hands its Authority back | `pub type Refusal { error: Error, authority: Authority }` and `open(tasks: Tasks, take a: Authority, c: Clock, r: Random, o: Options) -> Result[Db, Refusal] waits`. **Every** failure of `open` returns `a` in `authority`, whole: on memory@1 the value, on log@1 its `Dir`, on sim@1 its plan, and on objects@1 the same `Client` with its lanes running and its level and `op_ms`. Before it returns, the attempt's tasks are joined and every reservation it made (§12.9's fixed buffers, the journal and checkpoint task limits, the timer, a claimant's private state) is refunded, so the caller holds what it held before the call, plus the error's own storage. **Why take, not lend.** A successful open moves the transport into the Db: the Client's task-affine `Workers` stay in the Db and its `Requests` endpoint moves into the journal task (`std.object.split` in `open_object_writer`). With a lend the open would have to leave an emptied `Authority` behind, a value that is representable but unusable. Taking it, with a typed hand-back, needs no empty state. **Why every failure, not "the refusals before the claim".** `Lost`, `Remote`, `Undecided`, `Corrupt` and `Full` each arise both before §12.3 step 3's reservations and after step 5's replace. A rule keyed to the claim would split each of them by where it happened, and the caller would have to tell the two apart. In every case the transport is whole when `open` returns, because the open recovers the Requests endpoint if the startup send fails after the split (v2.72). The epoch, holder and position belong to the attempt, not to the Authority, so an Authority handed back after a won replace whose CLAIM advance failed holds no claim: another `open` with it claims afresh, and a `follower` with it only reads. RUNNER v2.54 proposed `(Error, Option[Authority])` with `Some` only for the refusals that write nothing. That Option is never None in the build, so it is dropped. **The others that take an Authority.** `follower` (§12.5) and the standalone import (§12.7, built as `import_authority`) still consume theirs on failure; see "Open" below | §1.1, §12.3, L61 |
| D2: `follower_starter_bytes()` | `pub fn follower_starter_bytes() -> u64` = 288 + max(I, P, K), with I, P and K defined in §12.5 "The starter's charges": 288 is the service task's MEM-24 charge beyond `bytes` (256 + 0 for its `()` result + 32); the owner endpoint adds 0, because `channel.sender` admits nothing (CONC-4); I is the startup reply channel, P a slot request's box and reply channel, and R one follower request's box and reply channel. **Two rules make the sum exact.** (1) A `FollowerPlan` holds no heap storage, so copying it allocates nothing. (2) The service and the worker free a request's box before they reply and drop the reply `Sender` right after the send. The requester takes the reply and then waits for its reply channel to close before its call returns. So at most one transient is live on the starter at a time, and each is refunded before the next starts. Every other allocation is inside `bytes` or a worker's N. **At the current build's types:** I = 128, P = 440 + 530 = 970 and K = 288 + 376 = 664, so the figure is **1,258** (1,194 before v2.141 added `Options.limits`). The derivation is in §12.5. RUNNER v2.54's original list (task, owner endpoint, one reply channel) left out the slot request, the boxes and the startup channel | §1.1, §12.5, §12.10 L85 |
| Open | `follower` and the standalone import consume their Authority on failure. Import's success consumes it too, and its one caller (the standalone install) ends either way, so a hand-back there has no reader. For `follower`, the transport sits in the provisioned worker, and if that worker ends holding an accepted bind, the transport ends with it. A follower hand-back would therefore need either an Option or a worker that returns its transport on cancellation. Neither is decided here. The consequence is §12.5's "a first `follower` call's `Reclaimed` returns to the caller, who calls `follower` again", which needs a fresh slot **and a fresh Authority**. The Runner's capture does not retry inside a start (a failed read is `PlaceCurrent` with no read, RUNNER §14.3), so nothing current needs more | §12.5 |

## CHANGES-v2.69 (store-spec-v268-2051; not yet built; v2.68 was taken by runner-slot-union-spec-2050)

| Item | Change | Where |
|---|---|---|
| B: owner-provisioned follower apply worker (MEM-30) | A follower's recovery, `catch_up`, `follower_floor` and the apply of `at_least` and `fresh` run on one worker per follower, provisioned by a `FollowerService` the process owner starts outside every step bound (new: `FollowerService`, `follower_service`, `follower_owner`, `close_follower_service`). Its grant is a `FollowerPlan`'s envelope F = T − L from the §1.1 sizing, split into the slot's control storage Q and one task charge 288 + N. A move-only `FollowerSlot` binds one open to the worker. The worker serializes requests and prepares candidates; the live call publishes by one infallible swap; cancellation and retirement follow ownership, never a finalizer. §12.9 names N as the payer of every follower allocation and admits each before its read. Adopted from astra mem30-apply-grant-2043 rank 1; m2-finish-2021's MEM30-SPEC-DELTA (an `apply_bytes` argument) is superseded. **Changed from the ADVICE**, each because the ADVICE left a referent undefined: (1) `FollowerService` is new: the ADVICE gives `FollowerOwner` no constructor and no holder for the service's Task handle, which a `send` owner cannot hold (CONC-1/2). (2) The service has its own root limit `bytes`, against which every slot's Q and task charge are admitted, so a refusal's `available` names that limit's room. (3) `follower_plan(kind, o: Options)` takes the effective Options that `sized`/`sized_with` returned instead of (share, Overrides), so every non-size field a follower reads (`resident_limit`, `rebase_levels`, `dirty_bytes`, `touch_slots`, `prefetch` and the Tiered fields) still reaches it, and T is §1.1's checked sum at those figures. (4) A request to a worker that is gone is `Retired` (v2.71); every apply failure is its own value. (5) `plan_bytes` and `service_bytes` are new, so a caller can size the service's limit. (6) The v2.69 B referent fix keeps `Shortage` as the sizing failure. The opener refuses an unbindable plan at the authority check. **Open:** the holder-derived envelope (astra, moderate confidence) can refuse a small read-only deployment and leaves an idle worker with spare room; a follower sizing law would tighten it. RUNNER §14.3's host capture opens a follower with `o.expect`: who starts its `FollowerService`, and that service's bytes in B23's root-limit admission, is settled by RUNNER v2.54 §14.3 "The capture's service", which asks STORE for D1 and D2 (RUNNER CHANGES-v2.54) | §1.1, §1.10, §12.5, §12.9, §12.10 L85 |
| A: branch dividers move up | A branch split pushes up the right piece's first entry's stored logical separator, and a branch borrow rotates the stored dividers through the parent (already AP3's text). Only a leaf split computes the shortest separator from real keys; the bulk build keeps it at every level. The divider is read from the page after the edit, so an entry just inserted carries the separator its child's split pushed up. A stored divider still separates the pieces (every key of child k − 1 is below it and every key of child k is at or above it), so no page below the branch is read and apply stays inside §13.3's plan pins. After deletes a branch divider may be longer than the shortest separator between its neighbours' keys; that is allowed, and AP3's decode rules never checked separator bounds. In an append-only Log the moved divider equals the shortest one, because the boundary's keys are the ones its leaf split used, so L83's numbers are unchanged. Postgres nbtree moves the existing pivot on an internal split and truncates suffixes only at leaf level (the ruling's citation); LMDB's `mdb_page_split` also takes a branch's existing node key at the split index as the separator (read at mdb.c 10909–10916). ADOPTED. H6 has no leaf borrow, so there is none to change | AP3, §13.3, §14.5, L49, L83 |

## CHANGES-v2.68 (slot images; with RUNNER-SPEC v2.53)

- §14.9's "Cover-only hit" and "Rerun" bullets are replaced by a pointer to RUNNER v2.53 §3 "Slot images". The image of a slot that has both a fixed range and a tail is the union of the two, so `window_changes` alone is wrong for it. A slot never switches Logs. The store API is unchanged. §14.9.

## CHANGES-v2.67 (Tiered Reader reservations)

- `reader` and `follower_reader` wait for pager admission and return `Full(Cache(c))` when that shared partition cannot make room. Reader drop releases the grant by ownership. §§13.2, 13.4, L62.

## CHANGES-v2.66 (bucket-backing-t1-2022: the loopback simulator qualifies by proof)

| Item | Change | Where |
|---|---|---|
| Loopback simulator | `std.sim.s3.serve_loopback` serves one path-style bucket over a loopback listener. It holds the credential root, checks SigV4 for the date each request names, and answers every authenticated request with `x-hirsel-sim-proof` | §12.2 |
| Proven client | a `std.object` client whose endpoint is `http://`, path style, at an IPv4 loopback literal refuses every answer without a valid proof (`Fatal(Malformed)`) | §12.2 |
| Qualification | such a client qualifies after one proven `GetBucketLocation`; a failed or unproven answer is `Unqualified("sim:proof")`. The https rule, host matching, AWS and Tigris rules are unchanged; a loopback host never qualifies without the proof | §12.2 |

## CHANGES-v2.65 (W3a B4)

An abort-only transaction whose body returns `Ok` yields `Outcome.Failed(StoreFailure.Conflict(name))`. The Store-owned failure also wraps runtime `Causes` via `Raised`. `serve` reports `Stop.Conflict(name)`.

## CHANGES-v2.64 (replay-delta-fix-2023: astra replay-delta-attack-2020 findings on v2.63; not yet built)

The Store M2 locator uses the pack ranges that its children cover. An unchanged child keeps its original name. A cut retry can write a distinct digest-addressed chunk at the same checkpoint position. §12.6 reclaims the unreferenced chunk after a later cut or a change of holder.

| # | Finding | Fix | Where |
|---|---|---|---|
| B1 | A Many entry's prior was 1 for a covering payload, so a 6-byte overwrite replayed as an insert of 6 bytes (+8) | prior = 1 + the full encoded length for every kind; a `()` entry alone gives 1; entry(20, 6) overwrite carries 7 | §13.1, L68 (a) |
| B2 | A Tiered trim checked only a floor at fold, so offsetting errors and masked suffix appends passed | per-trim evidence {ordinal, branch, lo, hi, c} (36 bytes), s from the overlay's suffix appends, c = d − s, s ≤ d checked at replay, each row checked at fold | §13.1, §13.7, §14.6, L79 (d), (e) |
| B3 | Replay charged retained-root commits additively, but AP15 admits the proposed union, which can be lower | `account_memory` recomputed by an exact Memory walk at open (no Cold read); priors charge `account_tiered` only, which never settles at a commit | §13.1, §13.7 step 8, §13.8, AP15, L68 (b) |
| B4 | Format-4 bytes written by main parse under the prior grammar | format word 5; formats 3 and 4 `Unsupported` at the first HEADER read, before claim, cleanup or rotation, on log@1 and objects@1; HEADER 5; format-4 stores re-created | AP1, §13.1, §14.1, L48, RUNNER-SPEC (format-5 record) |
| B5 | Prior ceiling tied to the API write limit, so a 12 MiB row under an 8 MiB limit could not be deleted | ceiling FORMAT_VALUE_MAX + 1 = 16,711,681; above it `Corrupt(Malformed)` | §13.1, L45 |
| B6 | A pin or fork of an overlaid Snapshot published its bare Cold root | §13.5 step 3 materializes the overlay copy-on-write before publish, holding the overlay share and lease | §13.5, L69 |
| B7 | The fold "changed no account" yet is a cut that settles | apply without re-charging, then publish X for both classes with `exact` true (M + 86 → M − 86) | §13.7, L68 (c) |
| F1 | Removed count b − f, and §14.5 and §14.6 both subtracted | min(b, n) − f; bytes′ computed once; b < f or d > bytes is `Corrupt`; Memory trims compared at apply | §14.6, L79 (a)–(c) |
| F2 | Independent per-branch folds recorded a forked Cold edge twice | ownership from the ordered suffix: an edge belongs to the branch named by `first` of its earliest overlay entry; overlay entries gain `first` | §13.7, L68 (d) |
| F3 | A deferred base mismatch surfaced as a capacity refusal | a corrupt fold takes the store error path: `Error(Corrupt(Malformed))`, `Before(Corrupt(Malformed), None)` | §13.3, L63 (f) |

- **B3: recompute, not record.** The brief allowed recording post-settle class figures in the deciding commit or recomputing where no Cold read is needed. Only Memory admission settles at a commit (§13.8), and a Memory class walk reads no Tiered page, so recomputing it at open costs no Cold read and no frame bytes. `account_tiered` settles only at a cut, so its additive replay stays exact. Recording would add a field to every settling commit and a second source of truth for a figure open can derive. Rejected for that reason.
- **B4: word 5, not a decoder rule.** A length test cannot tell the grammars apart (the two grammars overlap), and "trust our own implementation" excludes heuristics. No dual reader: format-4 stores are refused, as the host rules require.
- **F2: rebuild from the suffix, not a durable owner map.** The suffix already orders every write and FORK; `first` (ordinal, branch) is 12 bytes of overlay memory per entry and nothing in the frame.
- **Rejected:** a floor-only trim check with a running sum (B2's offsetting pair passes it); recording `account_memory` per settling commit (B3); materializing an overlaid Snapshot lazily at first read through the pin (the MANIFEST has no slot for an overlay, so a crash after publish would lose it).
- **Open items:** none that block S2b or S3. The checkbox redeploy from genesis must run on a build with S2b (format word 5).
- Reversibility: text only. The evidence row's width, `first`'s shape and the open-time Memory walk can all change before S2b and S3 land; the format word 5 becomes durable when S2b writes its first store.

## CHANGES-v2.63 (m3-replay-delta-spec-2012: exact replay without Cold reads; not yet built)

| Item | Change | Where |
|---|---|---|
| Op grammar | format-4 data put = tag ‖ key ‖ prior u32 LE ‖ value (13 + k + v bytes), delete = tag ‖ key ‖ prior (9 + k), trim = tag ‖ id ‖ bound ‖ dropped u64 BE (21); prior 0 = absent, else 1 + \|v_old\|; prior > VALUE_LIMIT + 1 is `Corrupt(Malformed)`; streams ops unchanged | §13.1, AP1a, §14.1 |
| Writer | priors and `dropped` come from the deciding attempt's plan pins at the applied root; the order is body, plan, encode; a rerun at a moved head plans again; fixed-width fields keep `Limit(Transaction)` arithmetic | §13.1, §13.3, §14.10 |
| Replay | priors update `CollectionRoot.rows`/`bytes` and the class account in exact mode; replay never edits a Tiered page and reads no Cold frame | §13.1, §13.7 steps 6 and 8, §14.6 |
| Overlay | per (branch, Tiered collection) ordered map key → {base, now} plus a Log floor; charged to the resident account and `dirty_bytes`; read as a staged layer by every consumer; bounded `count` from `base` | §13.7 |
| Fold cut | open's first cut merges each overlay through the cut pin quota (v2.74; v2.63 named the deleted checkpoint task's partition), rebuilds the replaced and dropped lists, writes no journal frame; writes into an overlaid collection wait for it; a refusal is `Refused` with the cut's cause | §13.3, §13.7 |
| Mismatch | a prior that contradicts the Memory leaf or the overlay is `Corrupt(Malformed)` at replay; a Tiered first op's prior is checked by the fold cut | §13.1, §13.7 |
| Followers | apply Tiered ops into their own overlay, never fold, rebuild it at re-base | §13.5, §13.7 |
| Accounts | `account_tiered` changes between cuts only by commit charges, so replay equals the crashed writer's figure | §13.8 |
| Sizes | one-key put 101 bytes, delete 63; worked replied commit 317 (341 / 342 / 350 with settle); AP15 cap example f = 63; trim 21 bytes | AP1a, AP15, L29, L50, §14.6, §14.7, L80 |
| Laws | L68 the K-present / K-absent pair (rows 2 vs 3, `account_tiered` M vs M + 86, zero Cold reads at open) with `prior_free_replay`, `recovery_cold_read`, `fold_no_replaced`; L62 (e) overlay reads; L63 (d), (e) `stale_prior`, `write_into_overlay`; L64 (e) `fold_on_writer`; L79 replayed `dropped` (`dropped_from_walk`); L80 the 21-byte op | §13.12, §14.13 |

- **No new format word, and L48 is unchanged** (withdrawn by v2.64: the word is 5). §2.7 rule 6 changes the word when a kept kind's payload changes in a format that stores hold. Since 4570e2eb main has written format-4 COMMITs only on log@1 and objects@1 test stores whose collections are all Memory (a `memory` store encodes none) and has never written a Tiered COMMIT, and the deployed checkbox genesis predates format 4. Format 4 is therefore still being defined, as D-Q2-1 ruled for Cells and Logs. A format-4 store written before the S2 follow-up is an unsupported test store: re-create it from genesis, as Sam's standing test-service ruling allows (DECISIONS, "Test services: standing authority"); its open is usually `Corrupt(Malformed)` and is not guaranteed to be. A second bump would be a stepping stone (/wholehog).
- **Rejected:** recovery-only reads of the Cold targets the suffix touches. Open's bucket reads would grow with the suffix, and AP21-T's "open decodes no class 3/4 frame" would fail.
- **Open items:** (1) §14.0's ruled bullet said "one 13-byte op"; the op is now 21 bytes and still one constant-size op, so the ruling's substance (O(1) journal cost) holds, noted for Sam. (2) The pending checkbox redeploy from genesis (FOR-SAM) should run on a build with the S2 follow-up, or be re-created again after it; a store written between 4570e2eb and that follow-up does not open afterwards. (3) The overlay costs key + value + 24 bytes per entry, bounded by the suffix (U). (4) The fold cut's bucket reads grow with the suffix's Tiered keys, and writes to an overlaid collection wait for it: the rejected alternative's cost, moved after open and off the open path, charged to the cut pin quota (v2.74).
- Reversibility: text only. The ops' widths, the overlay's shape and the fold cut can all change before S2's follow-up lands.

## CHANGES-v2.60 (w3a-spec-fix-1997: astra w3a-check-astra-1995 findings; with RUNNER v2.46; not yet built)

Each row closes the counterexample of the finding it names (`w3a-check-astra-1995/FINDINGS.md`); re-traces are in `w3a-spec-fix-1997/TRACES.md`.

| Finding | v2.60 | Where |
|---|---|---|
| #1 endpoint diff | A diff is taken only from a Snapshot at exactly `image.position` (held, Local pin slot, or a Follower checkpoint at that version); a rebuild resumes only against its exact target, else it is discarded and retired; an older pin, a lower checkpoint, `Reclaimed` or a domain mismatch is never a base | §1.14 "The base", "A rebuild", L72 (a), (b), (c) |
| #4 publication | `image` (published) is apart from `pass` (private) and `retiring`; an in-place update is one commit; a rebuild publishes the whole image at its last page; `live` is None until the first image; requests coalesce in `again`; a generation is allocated only when `retiring` is None; exhaustion is `Limit(Ids)` | §1.14, L72 (d), (e) |
| #5 binding | The row binds StoreId, branch and table id; a rebind retires the image and rebuilds; every commit compares the stored row whole and a mismatch is `Reloaded`; `projection` (shareable, in H) is split from the task-local `projector` | §1.14, L72 (f) |
| #6 Local chase | An unchanged source root or an empty diff is `Idle` with no transaction and no pin | §1.14 step 3, L72 |
| #8 callbacks | `apply` and `drop` are plain functions with a `shareable` env (§13.3); outside effects go through a durable outbox row keyed by (binding, generation, `at`, key), at least once; a projector delivers net changes, and every-event consumers tail an append-only table or Log | §1.14, core/std/api/store.md |
| #11 slot operand | `published_value` is erased per slot from the collection a static selector returns at binding | §1.5 |
| #14 Tiered sizes | One equation carves W, C, D, P out of r with Wmin = 2V + 1 MiB; the plan is chosen from the catalog before reservation or claim; non-Tiered reserves zero; Bucket's §12.9 buffers count once; checked arithmetic | §1.1, §13.8, RUNNER L60 |
| #15 L73 | The oracle is the greedy partition by both limits | L73 |
| W3B-O3 | The projector's source is a Table, so `trimmed` is empty; non-empty is `Corrupt(Malformed)` | §1.14 |

## CHANGES-v2.59 (m3-spec-fix-1994: astra m3-check-astra-1990 findings; not yet built)

| Item | Change | Where |
|---|---|---|
| #1 probe | The probe descends every participating root by collection and key, compares the root PagePtr and every edge, and no stamp comparison prunes it. A match is an exact (pack, offset) with equal payload length and checksum. The verdict belongs to the publishing cut, and a cut installing a historical root restarts unfinished probes. Loads check the location order rule only, never a stamp order | §13.6, §13.7, L69 |
| #2 drop | The drop boundary is min(`archive_drop`, captured floor). `drop_archive` above the head erases nothing reclaim keeps. The out-of-band deletion rule is removed | §13.9, L71 |
| #3 archive index | `archive_on`, the `archive` list-tree index of disjoint ranges and `archive_drop` replace the four archive scalars. Toggles are cuts. Every cut seals its window, a disabling cut included. The ARCHIVE frame 0x00E0 is withdrawn | §13.1, §13.7, §13.9, L68, L71 |
| #4 historical promotion | A pin or cut-path fork of a non-head Snapshot on a Tiered store holds its lease or capture, references in place when no Tiered pack left since its generation, and otherwise copies the frames of unlisted packs into fresh class 3/4 packs. An expired lease is `Reclaimed(floor)` | §1.9, AP17, §13.5, L69 |
| #12 sweep | Rule 1 is a one-page-per-pass sweep of `e-` from a durable MANIFEST cursor that wraps at the floor | §12.6, §13.1, §13.9, L71 |
| #5 entry state | `run`, `run_on`, `look`, `serve`, the projector and `reexecute` take `body: fn(.., E)` with `E: inert` on every store. D-M3-2 replaces D-M3-1; `steps: None` is `Missing("steps")` on a retry-capable call. M3-O1 is closed | §1.3, §1.5, §13.3, FOR-SAM M3-F1 |
| #6 nested fuel | A pin batch is the target, the key path to its leaf and a doubling window; untouched pins are evicted newest-first; a root change starts an empty map. Fuel inside one root object is identical on one build; across layouts it is FOR-SAM M3-F2 | §13.0, §13.3, L62(a) |
| #7 latch | A miss sets the attempt's latch: `Err(Missed)` and every later operation fails, and the driver checks the latch before any exit | §1.3 Error, §13.3, L63 |
| #8 held mode | Entering held mode revokes every other parked grant; demands and deliveries are tagged (call, grant generation, root); a parked call whose delivery does not fit enters held mode at once | §13.3, L64 |
| #9 publisher reads | RUNNER-SPEC v2.47 §4 G4: staged reads, fences before R = b, Reset with owed `Told`s, restart from a fresh durable base. M3-O2 is closed | §13.5, RUNNER §4 |
| #10 BLOB length | PACK_INDEX `len` is PagePtr.len for a PAGE and 8 + BlobRef.len for a BLOB; the AP8 vector's 1,033-byte payload is a 1,049-byte range; the pin charge is payload + 16-byte head | §13.1, §13.2, §13.3 |
| #11 recorded stop | Settle 1 is followed by a stop byte (Raised, OutOfSteps + bound, OutOfBytes, Conflict); `Recorded` gains `seed` and `stop`; replay's verdict order compares stops first; an `OutOfSteps` mismatch is `Unknown(Residency)`. Inside format 4 | AP1a, §1.3, §13.3, RUNNER §7 |
| #13 read routes | `count`, `scan_page`, `find_page`, `scan_next`, `find_next` and `changes` take a Reader and wait; Tx cursors are `TxRows`, stepped by `next_in` | §1.3, §13.4 |
| #14 read log | The read log is the body's logical reads (Points and Spans), never the pages it touched | §13.3, L62(b) |
| #15 miss route | A miss names its verified target and a direction; a parent cache key is sent only when the pager holds a Cold parent; hints never delay the target | §13.2, L63(c) |
| Coalesced GETs (Sam, 2026-11-23) | Logical pages stay 4 KiB and the NVMe copy is byte-identical to the pack. Every bucket GET is ≥ 128 KiB (clamped to the object) and ≤ 256 KiB unless one frame is larger, on up to `get_streams` parallel streams. Hint depth scales with each tier's measured latency, between `prefetch` and `prefetch_max`. Extra whole frames are unpinned hints in the shared cache, dropped when they do not fit; edge bytes are dropped. Charging and step counts never depend on either. The cache is pager-private | §13.0, §13.2, Options, L84 |
| AP2 | Only a Cold frame load needs a sealed, located image; a Tiered traversal reads a Hot page through its live slot array | AP2 |
