## CHANGES-v2.55 (store-runner-soften-1974: Spec Q1, Q4, Q5; with RUNNER v2.42; not yet built)

L60 now requires `Unsupported(0x0050)` for the retired AUTHORITY, as specified in §12.2. The checked MANIFEST payload limit applies after the object envelope is removed. One process-owned `Followers` source numbers captures. Branch identity is store-wide.

| Item | Change | Where |
|---|---|---|
| Q1 | `rollback_writes_on_overrun`'s 40 / `n + 40` / `n + 41` step counts leave `budgets.md`; §1.3 states O(1) entry, O(depth) reads, O(rows + depth) merge, no refund on overrun, and that an overrun or refusal moves its frozen layer back, so consecutive overruns leave the depth at the nesting depth. Laws find the threshold on one build instead of pinning it | §1.3; budgets.md MEM-30 |
| Q4 | AP2's canonical form at every commit and AP3's identical live/replay partition are deleted. A published in-memory page may be unsealed within a private page's bounds; the cut seals. Live and replay are equal in rows, snapshot, scan, `find` and `changes` answers and in the account (AP15, unchanged), and no law compares images, partitions or stamps across loads | AP2, AP3 |
| Q4 | A `memory` store computes no COMMIT size and refuses no transaction by size (it was arithmetic for a frame it never writes). `Limit(Transaction)` is a journal store's. L29 tests the journal arithmetic against the encoder | §1.1 limits, §3.1 step 3, L29 |
| Q5 | HEAD's 32-byte field and `o.image` are renamed `binding` / `o.binding` (same bytes, same place in the 136-byte HEAD) and hold the runner's identity digest (RUNNER v2.42 §6 "One identity"). `HeadView.image` is `binding`. The reconciler names a read's `Describe` by its identity digest | §12.1, §12.2, §12.7 |
| Q5 | L54's moved-activation histories install "NEW's code" at the records' allowance; (ii′) is the shared-identity history, decided by the deploy's outcome; the no-record recovery's hand install is `./place --hirsel-n-place=1048576 --hirsel-install`<!-- doc-ref: external --> and adopts OLD because the deploy failed; the named-launch variants are deleted with `--hirsel-assembly`<!-- doc-ref: historical --> | L54 |

## CHANGES-v2.54 (store-m2-spec-1970: the host and the launcher, not yet built; fixes v2.53)

The v2.53 check (astra `m2-v253-astra-1967`) found two broken items, both blocking and both errors in L54's examples that a check against the code would have caught, and two new ones. The store's rules and formats are unchanged; the rules are RUNNER-SPEC v2.41's.

| Id | v2.53 defect | v2.54 rule | Where |
|---|---|---|---|
| J1 (astra 1, broken, blocking) | L54 hosted OLD and NEW at 32 MiB. A serving launch copies the hosted allowance into its session configuration and `session_problem` checks it against the 16,777,216-byte per-session ceiling (runner.hirsel `operate`, `WIRE_SESSION_CEILING`), so OLD could never serve at 3 or adopt at 12 | 524,288 bytes configured and 1,048,576 hosted, the existing serving fixture's allowance (`runner_calls.hirsel`, `wire_defaults(65536, n)` serving at 1 MiB). Checked: with `wire_defaults(65536, 1048576)` the peak is 15,771,732 bytes; the largest `n_place` that fits is 1,551,318; an unnamed install at 524,288 returns before `session_problem`. The wrong-allowance case names 524,288 | L54; RUNNER §14.3 "Across sessions" |
| J2 (astra 2, broken, blocking) | The hand install put `--hirsel-install`<!-- doc-ref: external --> first. The native launcher reads its options only from the leading run of private options (`crates/hirsel-core-rt/src/entry.rs`, the prefix loop), so that argument ended the prefix and the name and allowance became `Args`, which `launch_selection` skips: an unnamed install at `PlaceConfig.n_place` that advances the store | The argv is `./place --hirsel-assembly=<OLD-hex> --hirsel-n-place=1048576 --hirsel-install`, and the prefix rule is stated where the private options are. The misordered argv is a case of L54 (a bare install) | L54; RUNNER §6, §14.3; launch.md "Describe launch", "Place activation", "Immutable launch identity" |
| J3 (astra 3, new) | A start that reported `PlaceCurrent(11)` with a failed read and exited 1 while the host was down looked, after the restart, like one that exited 1 before reporting: the launcher relays a serving refusal without keeping it (`launcher/mod.rs`, `drain_endpoint` and `ended`), so the host could not tell the two required suffixes apart | The launcher keeps a serving child's last answer (a `PlaceCurrent` with any read, a refusal, or `PlaceStoreMismatch`) with its unacknowledged end and lists it, with the child's launch selection, in the inventory; the host applies it before the end and before it sends `Acknowledge` for the end. A second application changes nothing. L54 gains the two histories | RUNNER §14.5 "The control session", "Failing and backoff"; launch.md "Restarts"; L54 |
| J4 (astra 4, new) | launch.md said an `Adopted` mark carries no cause "until the relaunch's open clears it", with no way back to `Unmatched` when a later read matches nothing | Adoption clears the previous cause; a later no-match read sets a new cause at the activation it read; the first accepted `PlaceOpened` clears the mark; a relaunch that dies before reporting leaves `Adopted` and contributes its exit status. L54 gains the move to 13 | launch.md "When the host starts"; L54 |

Reversible: every item before 1.0. Build items are listed in RUNNER-SPEC CHANGES-v2.41.

## CHANGES-v2.53 (store-m2-spec-1966: the host and the launcher, not yet built; fixes v2.52)

The v2.52 check (astra `m2-v252-astra-1965`) found two broken items and two new ones, the first blocking. The store's rules and formats are unchanged; the rules are RUNNER-SPEC v2.40's.

| Id | v2.52 defect | v2.53 rule | Where |
|---|---|---|---|
| I1 (astra 1, broken, blocking) | The documented hand install could never reproduce either candidate: a standalone install registers and describes at `PlaceConfig.n_place` (runner.hirsel `operate` → `operate_install` → `head_prepare`), not at `--hirsel-n-place`<!-- doc-ref: external -->, so with a configured allowance other than the hosted one its `Describe` matched neither OLD nor NEW, at every later activation | A launch that names an assembly record takes its allowance from the launch in every mode, as a hosted launch does: registration, `Describe`, bounds, the task budget, `Status` and Welcome. The native launcher refuses `--hirsel-assembly`<!-- doc-ref: planned --> without an explicit `--hirsel-n-place`<!-- doc-ref: external --> as a malformed private option, since the runtime cannot tell an absent option from its default. The no-record cause prints each candidate's allowance and the read's, so a wrong value is visible. launch.md says "the marked deploy's OLD or NEW". L54 runs the history with 16 MiB configured and 32 MiB hosted (v2.54 J1: 524,288 and 1,048,576, since 32 MiB cannot serve) | RUNNER §6 "One n_place", "A named launch runs at the launch's allowance", "One assembly name"; launch.md "Immutable launch identity", "Describe launch" options, "Place activation"; L54 |
| I2 (astra 2, broken) | A retry that ended before `PlaceCurrent` or `PlaceOpened` showed only an exit status, since only `PlaceCurrent` handling picked the mark's cause | `Status.failing[].last` is stated once (§14.5 "Failing and backoff") and formatted from the durable mark: an `Unmatched` mark's cause, then `; last start: ` and the start's own refusal or exit status, for every end, including one with no report and one recovered from the inventory. Adoption turns the mark `Adopted`, which carries no cause, so a relaunch that dies after an adoption shows its own exit status, not a stale no-match | RUNNER §14.3 "What serves", "Leaving no record", §14.5; launch.md "When the host starts"; L54 |
| I3 (astra 3, new) | An Undecided same-record probe at `Current(10)` refused at 3 with a failed read was told the store's `Describe` at 3 differs, by comparing with the group's recorded 3 | A start's own cause compares the refusal's activation with the activation that start was launched to open (a probe's intent, otherwise the generation's selection) and names the record it ran; only equal activations infer a `Describe` mismatch | RUNNER §14.5 "Genesis"; launch.md "When the host starts"; L54 |
| I4 (astra 4, new) | "No retained assembly matches" denied a match to a record the group retains outside the candidates | `No recovery candidate matches the store at activation a`, keeping the read's assembly and the candidate list. Replaces the headline of CHANGES-v2.52 H2 | RUNNER §14.3; launch.md; L54 |

Considered and not taken for I1: the host deriving the allowance itself from the record by running the install, which would make a wrong value unrepresentable. The launcher half exists: `InstallMode(g, Standalone(a))` launches `--hirsel-install-child`<!-- doc-ref: external -->, and runner.hirsel's install-child branch already installs at the launch allowance. Only an operator request (`Install(G, record)`) is missing, and it would choose an assembly, which v2.51 removed with `Select`; the launch rule plus the printed allowances closes the defect without one. Reversible: every item before 1.0. `GroupRow.unmatched` becomes `recovery: Option[Unmatched(attempt, a, assembly, n_place) | Adopted(attempt, a, record)]`, and the host keeps each outstanding start's selection with its generation (open items for the build lane).

## CHANGES-v2.52 (store-m2-spec-1962: the host, not yet built; fixes v2.51)

The v2.51 delta check (astra `m2-v251-astra-1961`) found one broken item and one new bug, both in what follows a decided deploy with no matching record. The store's rules and formats are unchanged. §12.7's objects@1 capture also reads the activation table's `current` from the same fresh head, so a read names the assembly of the store's `Describe`; the host gains a durable mark and a read (RUNNER-SPEC v2.39).

| Id | v2.51 defect | v2.52 rule | Where |
|---|---|---|---|
| H1 (astra 3, broken) | After a no-match decision the group kept OLD at 3 and every retry was refused there. A correct hand install of OLD's record at 12 changed nothing: `PlaceCurrent(12)` was ignored, the deploy was terminal, and a hot deploy needs a live OLD | The no-match transaction writes a durable mark in the group's row. Each refused retry of a marked group takes §12.7's capture without a witness row (objects@1 follower, log@1 under its own `LOCK`). When the read's image is the `Describe` digest of the attempt's OLD or NEW that the group still roots, one transaction, conditional on the incarnation and the same mark, makes it current at the read's activation, writes the index there and applies Roots. The deploy's outcome and index entry at t stay. While nothing matches, the selection stays. L54 (ii′) gains the history | RUNNER §14.3 "What serves", "Leaving no record", §14.5; launch.md "When the host starts"; L54 |
| H2 (astra 5, new) | The cause read "under another image" or "the host selected 3" when both launches ran one executable | `No retained assembly matches the store at activation a`, with the read's assembly name (or none, or undecodable) and the names of the adoption's candidates, kept by the mark across stale-version retries, failed reads and restarts. launch.md says a bare install exits 0, and that a hand install for a hosted group passes the record's `--hirsel-assembly=<hex>`<!-- doc-ref: planned --> and allowance, pointing at H1 | RUNNER §14.3, §14.5; launch.md "Immutable launch identity", "Place activation" |

Reversible: every item before 1.0. The host's `GroupRow` gains `unmatched` (open item for the build lane, with v2.51's `Describe` field).

## CHANGES-v2.51 (store-m2-spec-1960: the host, not yet built; fixes v2.50)

The v2.50 delta check (astra `m2-v250-astra-1959`) found one broken item and two new bugs, all in v2.50's handling of two assembly records with one `Describe` digest; 1b (Roots) and the log@1 read held. v2.51 makes that state unrepresentable instead of adding host machinery around it. The store's rules and formats are unchanged; the runner's `Describe` gains a field (RUNNER-SPEC v2.38).

| Id | v2.50 defect | v2.51 rule | Where |
|---|---|---|---|
| G1 (astra 1a, broken) | A probe refused before OLD's delayed write of D at 10 landed read D at 10 through its follower, recorded Installed, found no index entry at 10 and offered OLD as an operator choice for the activation this attempt installed for NEW; an opening probe would have served NEW at 10 | The decision transaction of an Installed outcome writes NEW's record into the host's activation index at the attempt's version t, as an opening probe's commit does, whatever activation the read reports. With a = t NEW is current at t; with a above t, row a's image alone decides what is current. L54 adds the refusal → write → follower order with NEW at 10 | RUNNER §14.3 "The outcome", launch.md "When the host starts", L54 |
| G2 (astra 1a, new) | A `Select` retried after a later deploy passed the new choice's checks and decided that deploy | Deleted with `Select`: no operator request chooses an assembly | RUNNER §14.3, launch.md "The operator socket", "The operator CLI" |
| G3 (astra 1a, new) | A restart before `Select` committed probed the pending group's OLD below the head | Deleted with the pending choice: no state suppresses or bypasses backoff. A failing no-record group's retries are refused before handles and before any claim, and write nothing | RUNNER §14.3, §14.5 |
| G0 (the cause) | Two distinct assembly records (recipes that differ outside the image, or one recipe under two toolchains) give one `Describe`, so the store's evidence could not name the record | Launch metadata gains `assembly`, the 32-byte name of the assembly record the launch runs (empty when none is named), and `Describe` carries it. Distinct records give distinct digests, so matching the head's image picks one record or none. A launch of the other record is refused `NotCurrent` before handles, like another `n_place`; OLD installs NEW's exact `Describe`, so the record travels with the activation. Only the platform launcher names a record by default; `hirsel run`, evaluator, embedded and bare launches leave it empty, so their restarts are unchanged, and a hand install on a hosted store names the record with `--hirsel-assembly`<!-- doc-ref: planned --> or matches none. An opening probe always matches the record it opened, so v2.50's "stop the probe before `PlaceOpened`" branch is deleted. Chosen over a field in `releases`: the open guard compares `Describe` already, so one digest both guards the open and selects the record, and no reader needs a second row in its capture | RUNNER §6, launch.md "Immutable launch identity", "Describe launch" |

Reversible: every item before 1.0. `Describe`'s codec gains a field. The runner, the manifest and the describe helper are built, so a store or manifest written before this change is rebuilt or rewritten under the no-compatibility rule (open item for the build lane, with v2.48's release-row field).

## CHANGES-v2.50 (store-m2-spec-1954: §12 and the host, not yet built; fixes v2.49)

The v2.49 delta check (astra `m2-v249-astra-1953`) found two new host bugs and one broken rule; the row decision of an unknown install and the claim's fixed pair held. The fixes are host rules (RUNNER-SPEC v2.37 §14.3, launch.md); the store text changes only for the log@1 read. Sam's rulings Q1–Q5 stand. Nothing here is built, and no store format changes.

| Id | v2.49 defect | v2.50 rule | Where |
|---|---|---|---|
| F1 (astra 1a) | OLD and NEW can differ only in their recipe records (a `Dir` binding given at launch, which `Describe` does not hold), so both match the head's `Describe`. "Whichever matches" then allowed two catalog and collection decisions from the same store evidence | Matching first collapses identical assembly records. Of two distinct matching records, the one the host's activation index already names for the head's activation serves. With none named, the host records the proved outcome, selects nothing, keeps OLD current and NEW retained, and the group is failing until an operator `Select` names one. The store cannot name a host recipe, and the records differ in granted authority, so the host never picks. As astra proposed | RUNNER §14.3, launch.md, L54 (ii′) |
| F2 (astra 1b) | §14.3 "Across sessions" restated the root rule and made NEW collectable when no assembly matched, against "Roots" and L54 (iii) | "Across sessions" refers to "Roots", the one statement: a failed attempt drops NEW only when OLD was selected to serve | RUNNER §14.3, launch.md "When the host starts" |
| F3 (astra 3) | On log@1 a hosted deploy left Undecided, after a standalone install took t or moved the activation past it, was refused at every probe and stayed Undecided | A log@1 probe refused at the post-open comparison keeps `LOCK` and reads, from its own recovered state, the store ID, the activation, the digest of the encoded current `Describe` and `releases[t]`, without application handles, and reports them. Recovery's directory barrier made that state durable and `LOCK` excludes every writer, so an absent row is final: Installed or failed, never unknown. The objects@1 outcome, selection and root rules then apply. As astra proposed: no offline follower, no holder pair, no format change | §12.2, §12.7, L54, RUNNER §6, §14.3, launch.md |
| — (astra 2) | The intro and E2 said every head GET of a claim compares its pair first | Aligned with §12.3: the replace's Q5 GET tests its exact body first. No algorithm change | intro, CHANGES-v2.49 E2 |

Reversible: every item before 1.0. `Select` is a new operator request with no caller yet. This closes CHANGES-v2.49's open item.

## CHANGES-v2.49 (store-m2-spec-1951: §12, not yet built; fixes v2.48)

The v2.48 delta check (astra `m2-v248-astra-1950`, cited "Check #n") found one broken rule and one new bug; its third item, retain-1 recovery, held. Sam's rulings Q1–Q5 stand: a claim is still one GET and one conditional replace, one round trip apart, and v2.49 adds no request to that pair. Nothing here is built.

| Id | v2.48 defect | v2.49 rule | Where |
|---|---|---|---|
| E1 (Check #1) | The host decided an unknown install only by an open at t. Once another install moved the activation to t + 1 the probe got `PlaceCurrent(t + 1)` and retried forever with the deploy Undecided, though `releases[t].prepared` decided it; a standalone install of the same executable at t with another `n_place` was refused at the image check before its empty witness was seen | A refused probe takes a follower read of one capture (`follower_head`: identity, activation, image, pair; and `releases[t]`). Row t decides the attempt even when the head is past t; the unknown case stays for an absent row under OLD's pair, which OLD reports with its import (`claim_of`). The attempt's outcome is recorded apart from what serves, which follows the head's activation and image, so Installed at t never rolls a group back from t + 1. Chosen as astra proposed: an open at the moved activation would need an assembly of the head's image, which the host may not have (the `n_place` case) | §12.5, §12.7, L54, RUNNER §6, §14.3, §14.5, launch.md |
| E2 (Check #2) | A claimant whose read of the incumbent's checkpoint was reclaimed by a *rival* claimant's cut rebased onto the rival's head, recorded the rival's pair as admitted and fenced it: two opens from epoch e both returned a Db | Step 1's (epoch, holder) is the claim's pair for the whole open. Every head GET before the replace applies (witness and its retries, final GET, catch-up witness) compares it first; the replace's Q5 GET first tests for its own exact body, which is Claimed, and compares only other bytes with the pair. Another pair is `Lost` before that head is classified, read or adopted. A same-pair `Reclaimed` still rebases | §12.3, §12.6, L52 |

Reversible: every item before 1.0. No store holds §12 bytes; `follower_head` and `claim_of` are new API with no caller yet. Open item: on log@1 a hosted Undecided deploy whose activation another install moved still retries; a log@1 follower needs writer exclusion the probe cannot assert, so it is left to the build lane.

## CHANGES-v2.48 (store-m2-spec-1949: §12, not yet built; fixes v2.47)

The v2.47 delta check (astra `m2-delta-astra-1948`, cited "Delta #n") found two broken rules and one new bug. Sam's rulings Q1–Q5 stand: a claim is still one GET and one conditional replace, one round trip apart. Nothing here is built.

| Id | v2.47 defect | v2.48 rule | Where |
|---|---|---|---|
| D1 (Delta #3) | Reconciliation compared the attempt's PREPARED digest D with `releases[v + 1].digest`, which the runner fills with the executable image digest I, so an applied, overtaken install read as absent. Reconciliation also read OLD's activation + 1 instead of the commit version | The release row gains `prepared`, the attempt's validated import digest, set by the install from the import (empty for a standalone install and genesis). Every reconciler compares `releases[t].prepared` with D at t = exported main head + 1, the host's recorded version; HEAD `activation` is t. Chosen over comparing images because I names an executable, not an attempt: another install of the same image can take t with other `Describe` or prepared bytes | §3.5a, §11.1, §12.7, L54, L61, RUNNER §6, §14.3 |
| D2 (Delta #5) | A claim fenced the incumbent and only then caught up the delta, so a claimant short of memory, or one whose delta crossed a `retain` 1 checkpoint, deposed a healthy holder and could not serve | A claim fences only the head it has recovered and admitted: GET, recover, admit memory and reserve the writer's buffers, then a final GET; at exactly the admitted bytes it replaces at once, and at a moved head of the same pair it writes nothing, catches up privately (rebasing on a newer checkpoint) and repeats. Qualification runs once per open. v2.47's step 5 and its residual are deleted; an open-time cut refused for memory no longer fails the open | §12.3, §12.9, L52, L61 |
| D3 (Delta #2) | Every CHECKPOINT read compared its floor with its predecessor's, which a `retain` 1 reclaim lawfully erases, so a cold open of a healthy store was refused | No read check names a predecessor checkpoint. HEAD `floor` = MARK `floor` = the smallest retained position, checked where the MARK is loaded; every earlier retained checkpoint has a MARK row; monotonicity is the writer's retained-set rule (L58). A follower holding both generations compares them. `prev_first` is c on objects@1. Every object a cold read of the head needs is shown live for the head's own checkpoint | §12.2, §12.6, L58, L60 |

Reversible: every item before 1.0. No store holds §12 bytes. The release row's new field changes the built runner's `ReleaseRecord` codec, so under the no-compatibility rule a log@1 store written before it is rebuilt from genesis, never read by a dual reader (open item for the build lane).

## CHANGES-v2.47 (store-m2-spec-1940: §12, not yet built; fixes v2.46)

Two blind re-checks of v2.46 (astra `m2-recheck-astra-1932`, numbered #1–#23; Fable `m2-recheck-fable-1933`, L/S/A ids) found three blockers on their own. Sam's rulings Q1–Q5 stand. Nothing here is built, so each format change below defines a never-built kind afresh or allocates a new one; no store holds v2.46 bytes.

| Id | v2.46 defect | v2.47 rule | Where |
|---|---|---|---|
| B1 (astra #1, Fable L1) | A claim copied the head's position but HEAD had no epoch for the latest entry, so after a claim the latest entry could not be named | HEAD gains `entry_epoch` (a claim copies it, an advance sets it) and `image`; it is 136 B. When `position = c_position` the entry and checkpoint references must be equal. LINK and CLAIM epoch rules. Head buffers 131,552 B. L60 gains the claim-over-GROUP and double-claim vectors | §12.2, §12.9, L60 |
| B2 (astra #3, Fable S1, A1) | Reclaim's rule 2 took chain names from recovery's walk, which names nothing below the open's base checkpoint, so with `retain` ≥ 3 it erased live entries | Rule 2's range is max(floor, c₀ + 1)..P with names from recovery's walk and the holder's own advances; [floor, c₀] waits for rule 1. G, N and the floor come from the last Advanced CHECKPOINT, captured at pass start. Charge 24 × (P − c₀) | §12.6, §12.9, L58 |
| B3 (astra #2) | A Fenced activation head write reported "nothing activated" although the activation could have applied before the other holder's claim | Fenced or Undecided at the activation head write means unknown. OLD stops; reconciliation is a qualified read comparing `releases[v + 1]`'s digest with the attempt's. Only a failure before the head write proves nothing activated | §12.7, L54, RUNNER §14.3 |
| astra #4 | A CHECKPOINT's own operation (delete, pin, activation) had no defined place in the state it certifies | A CHECKPOINT at c certifies the state after its own operation, from the settled prefix through c − 1 | §12.2 |
| astra #5, #21, Fable L3 | A rejected image or identity could depose the serving holder; an empty namespace with an expected identity made a new store | `Options.image`; opening checks before any write; no head with `expect` is `NotFound`; claim recovers read-only before its replace | §1.1, §12.1, §12.3, L61 |
| astra #6, Fable L2 | A crash before the genesis head left keys that refused every later genesis | Genesis ignores debris-shaped keys of epoch 1 | §12.3, L54 |
| astra #7 | Export wrote a directory the prepare opener could not read | The scratch is an ordinary log@1 image with one segment `j-<c>` | §12.7, L61 |
| astra #8, Fable A2 | The 404 witness lacked the evidence for its verdict | The verdict is by the follower's capture against the head's retained set; a lost witness retries to the deadline | §12.5, L59 |
| astra #9, #10, Fable open question | Class-2 packs had no read bound; a legal commit could make every covering locator too large; the unreached-row check assumed an unstated invariant | A locator tree (role 8 chunks, MARK `depth`); the read order with each allocation bounded first; leaf rows must equal L ∪ C | §2.7, §12.2, §12.9, L60 |
| astra #11, Fable A3 | A fresh read could race the terminal result of its own advance | One owner per advance; first evidence wins; an acked ticket stays Ok | §12.3, §12.5, L57 |
| astra #12, Fable A4 | Two-level sim had no protocol | Register is the strongest level; weaker levels have their own barrier events; readers see the strongest prefix | §12.4, L55, L56 |
| astra #13, Fable L5 | A won replace did not mean a returned Db; the loop kept deposing fresh claimants | Same-pair retry only; a changed pair returns `Lost`; budget bounds every request; L52 counts fences separately | §12.3, L52 |
| astra #14, Fable L6 | A fence never reached an idle runner | `health(db)` watch cell; RUNNER ends abnormally on it | §12.3, RUNNER §2.1 |
| astra #15 | Follower API had unreachable results and missing inputs | `Unseen`; fallible `snapshot_follower`; `follower_index` takes `index`'s inputs; `Missing("schema")` | §1.1, §12.5 |
| astra #16 | v2.46 dropped AUTHORITY `style` and grew 0x0050's payload | AUTHORITY is the new kind 0x00D0 with `style`; 0x0050 is retired | §2.7, §12.2 |
| astra #17, Fable S2 | Qualification read the recorded endpoint and counted unknown Tigris locations as Single-region; ETag non-recurrence unstated | The live client is qualified at every open, https only, exact hosts; Tigris by an exact allow-list of observed `GetBucketLocation` replies, shipped empty; AWS only without KMS or SSE-C; the residual ETag assumption stated | §12.2 |
| astra #18 | Multipart completion was retried like a PUT | 200-with-Error is an error; 409 or `NoSuchUpload` restarts the upload with a fresh id | §12.2, L54m |
| astra #19 | The log follower could show a MANIFEST a power cut takes back | O1 (`sync_dir`) before reading | §12.5 |
| astra #20 | Standalone import made an empty store visible first | Import is genesis with the imported base | §12.7 |
| astra #22, Fable nit | A random follower number could not guarantee `Foreign` | A process-wide counter | §12.1 |
| astra #23 | `position` needed a table of issued tickets | `Mark.Journal` carries branch and version | §1.6, §12.5 |
| Fable L4 | Recovery was serial and fetched the suffix twice | LIST as a fetch hint, prefix reads of 112 B, the chain from the head decides | §12.3 |
| Fable nits | 2 × `ENTRY_LIMIT` reserved; poll cost and interaction; failed-poll count; `retain: u32`; AWS hosts | 2 × (112 + `group_bytes`) plus a charged on-demand singleton; the poll stops when the place is closed and during a cut; `Ok(n)` counts failed polls; `retain: u8`; non-standard AWS hosts stated as unqualified | §1.1, §12.2, §12.3, §12.9 |
| Fable L6 (callers) | The deleted read-only API's callers were not listed | Six callers listed with their destination | §11.19 |

## CHANGES-v2.46 (store-m2-spec-1930: §12, not yet built; supersedes v2.45)

v2.45 was never applied. Its design is part of v2.46: one opener over an `Authority` value (`open`, `open_bucket` and `memory` deleted), the whole history in the authority, the head as fence, per-authority levels, `Follower` in place of §11.19, reclaim from history, and laws L52–L60. The items below are where v2.46 differs from or adds to v2.45.

| # | Item | Verdict | What v2.46 does | Where |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| AV1 | Q1: the v2.43 hybrid, cut shape and trigger | **ADOPTED** | No compatibility reader. Genesis refuses a non-empty prefix. objects@1 keeps the full history. Cuts are AP16's paused cut: a CHECKPOINT at c names the state after c − 1. New trigger `checkpoint_entries`. Non-blocking uploads and two-level authorities are deferred. | §11.18, §12.2 |
| AV2 | Q2: release level | **ADOPTED** | The runner releases at the strongest level. `release_level` is gone. `position` waits `durable`. | §1.6, §12.4 |
| AV3 | Q3: no leases, idle poll | **ADOPTED** | Queries are RAM reads. `fresh` and `fresh_snapshot` each cost one head GET. The idle head poll runs every `head_poll_ms` on the monotonic clock, and a foreign head makes the holder sticky `Fenced`. | §12.3, §12.5 |
| AV4 | Q4: claim shape | **REPLACED v2.45's claim** | A claim is GET plus one conditional head replace that bumps (epoch, holder) at position p, then a CLAIM entry at p + 1. `head.epoch ≥ entry epoch`. Bounded `Lost` retry until `claim_ms`. Identity, AUTHORITY, declaration, activation and provider are checked first. | §12.1, §12.3 |
| AV5 | Q5: unknown outcomes | **REPLACED v2.45's resolution** | Every head write, genesis included, resolves Uncertain by an exact resend. On 412 it GETs the head and compares exact bytes: own bytes mean Advanced, other bytes mean Fenced or Lost, and a failed GET means Undecided with the claim dead. New `std.object.store_if`, which never resolves. | §12.2, §12.3 |
| AV6 | Frame binding was circular (the name held a digest of bytes whose checks bound the name) | **FIXED** | Frames keep §2.1's binding (seq = position, generation or pack seq). The object digest is xxh3-64 over the whole object, carried in the name and in references. HEADER role, seq and epoch must match the name. | §12.2 |
| AV7 | A PagePtr names only a seq; a cold reader could not find packs | **FIXED: LOCATE** | A CHECKPOINT entry's LOCATE rows list (seq, epoch, len, digest) for every reachable pack, class-2 and inherited ones included. The rows are checked both ways against AP21. | §2.7, §12.2 |
| AV8 | HEAD and AUTHORITY layouts, the empty base | **FIXED** | HEAD is 96 B, twelve u64 with `floor` and the checkpoint name. Overflow is `Limit(Ids)`. AUTHORITY carries a level declaration. Genesis writes an empty MANIFEST and CHECKPOINT entry 1. | §2.7, §12.2, §12.3 |
| AV9 | The floor could move down and revive a checkpoint | **FIXED** | MARK lists the retained set. The new set is the latest k of the old set ∪ {c}, so the floor is monotonic. | §12.6 |
| AV10 | 409 read as Mismatch | **FIXED** | 409 is retried with the same tag and body. 412 is Mismatch or Exists. | §12.2 |
| AV11 | Followers had no table binder, shared open numbers, and read 404 ambiguously | **FIXED** | `follower_table`, `follower_index` and `follower_branch`. Open numbers are 2⁶³ \| r. A 404 takes a second witness: `Reclaimed` or `Corrupt(.. Absent)`. Catch-up publishes atomically. | §1.12, §12.1, §12.5 |
| AV12 | A log@1 follower stopped at END | **FIXED** | It reads offline under writer exclusion, as §3.4 steps 4–6, past END to the first bad frame, syncing each segment as step 5 does. | §12.5 |
| AV13 | Install and import were one operation | **SPLIT** | A prepared install is a CHECKPOINT advance on the validated head, with `activation` in HEAD. Standalone import is genesis of a new store. Export captures one head and restarts whole on `Fenced`. | §12.6, §12.7 |
| AV14 | Provider consistency | **NEW contract** (v2.77) | objects@1 requires a linearizable conditional head write, which is the operator's to provide: the store checks no provider brand. Any https S3-compatible endpoint opens. Genesis refuses an endpoint that applies a PUT whose `If-None-Match` or `If-Match` failed. A bucket that is strongly consistent only within a region (for example Tigris Global, which the dev bucket is) needs every writer and follower on one region endpoint. | §12.2 |
| AV15 | Memory and steps of the new buffers | **FIXED** | Two entry buffers and two head buffers are reserved at open. Chain list, fetch, LOCATE and LIST allocations are charged and refused before requests. Store work runs on store tasks. | §12.9 |
| AV16 | Laws L52–L60 | **TIGHTENED** | Each law has an independent oracle (register history, sim ground truth, hand-built bytes) and the bug it alone catches. Added L58b. | §12.10 |

Reversible: every item before 1.0 (no old-format readers). Q1–Q5 are Sam's rulings.

## CHANGES-v2.43 (store-bucket-1836: §11.18, built; superseded by v2.46)

| # | Item | Verdict | What v2.43 does | Where |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| BK1 | `Options.authority = Bucket(b)` was refused with `Unsupported(1)` | **built** | `open_bucket(tasks, d, c, r, o, take client, op_ms)` opens it. `open` still refuses Bucket with `Unsupported(1)`, and `open_bucket` refuses Directory with `Unsupported(0)` | §1.1, §11.18 |
| BK2 | Rows 11 and 12 reserved CLAIM for j-* and gave AUTHORITY no layout | **built** | CLAIM (24 B: epoch, holder) and AUTHORITY (the Bucket config) are MUST frames after a Bucket store's MANIFEST frame; HEADER `authority` is 1 in that MANIFEST only | §2.3, §2.7, §2.9, §11.18 |
| BK3 | A writer cut off from the bucket could publish over a newer writer | **built** | Each generation is published by `store_no_clobber`; `Exists` halts the writer with `Fenced(epoch)` | §11.18 |
| BK4 | An upload left by a crashed writer stayed pending | **built** | Open aborts every pending multipart upload under the prefix (`std.object.pending_uploads`) | §11.18 |
| BK5 | A fenced writer's late pack could take the next writer's pack number, so the next writer's no-clobber upload would halt it | **built** | On `Exists` for a pack the writer lists the generations after its own: none means the pack is an orphan no generation references, which it overwrites; any means it is fenced | §11.18 |

## CHANGES-v2.42 (store-scrub-1786: §11.17, built)

| # | Item | Verdict | What v2.42 does | Where |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| SC1 | An open Store answered from resident pages after its pack bytes went bad, and nothing reread them until the next open | **built** | `begin_scrub` and `scrub_step`, an operator-requested tour under a byte budget | §11.17, §11.16 |
| SC2 | A cut could unlink a retired pack while a tour still had to read it | **built** | The tour's lease names the packs it listed. A cut carries a leased retirement, as it does for an export lease (§3.5a). The first cut after the tour is dropped unlinks the pack | §11.17 |
| SC3 | Envelope checks alone accept a stale frame that was correctly sealed for the same place, for example a lost write over a leftover of the same pack number | **built** | A reachable frame must also match the length and plain xxh3 of the generation's pointer to it | §11.17 |

| # | Item | Verdict | What v2.40 does | Where |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| C-1 | AP1's kind table kept 0x0081 "unchanged", although format 3 deletes `checkpoint_task_bytes` and install must check NEW's pack, checkpoint and journal limits | **TARGET, not built** | 0x0081 gets its own row and its own layout: the codec encoding of `PreparedMeta`, with field offsets and widths, 212 + d bytes. The writer stores NEW's effective options and measures the encoded buffer against 1,048,832 (d ≤ 1,048,620), and the reader decodes exactly n bytes. Install checks only the four decoded values, bound by the import digest. Law L51 (e′) covers the floor and one byte below with identical OLD options. | §11.1 kind table, T10, AP1b, AP4; L51 (e′); store.md Install |
| C-2 | AP16 step 4 encoded the live holder records, so a `delete_branch` cut could name the deleted branch | **TARGET, not built** | Plan item 0 builds the prospective holder delta H, with its record storage and slot reserved. Step 4 encodes the live records with H applied, every root at its candidate location; `first_live` is the successor segment and `next_pack` is one past the last pack written. Step 5 applies H without allocation, and a refusal drops it. Live counters (stamps, a cut-path fork's id) are kept apart from prospective ones (`next_table`, T, the last published generation). | §11.12 AP16 steps 2, 4, 5, H, refusal; L28 (e) |
| C-3 | AP15's "every frame producer" gave `serve` and `conclude` no result, and resident `NoRoom` would have counted journal pressure toward settle 2 | **TARGET, not built** | A producer table. `serve` returns `Before(Full(Journal(c)), None)`: the position stays Admitted and re-admission gives `Abandoned`. `conclude` returns `Before(Full(Journal(c)), Some(p))`, handing its Pending back so a retry after a cut keeps the original context. `NoRoom` stays resident or allocation only. **Ruling (a) deviation, small:** `ServeError.Before` gains an `Option[Pending]`, because `Before(Error)` cannot return the Pending that `conclude` took. | §11.11 AP15; §11.16; L50 (e); store.md Journal cap |
| C-4 | A segment was said to hold at most `checkpoint_bytes` + `FRAME_LIMIT`, which refused rotations, extension frames and torn tails all break | **TARGET, not built** | U is the accepted COMMIT/FORK bytes and the publication limit. A bounded streaming segment reader (65,536-byte chunk plus one `FRAME_LIMIT` frame buffer) keeps the checksum, END, `prev_end` and torn-tail rules for recovery and `recorded`. HEADER/END pairs are counted for open rotations and for cuts. No physical-disk bound is given; one would be a separate contract. | §11.11 AP15 segment reads, Disk |
| C-5 | The floor proof had no restart precondition, and `checkpoint_bytes` near u64::MAX overflowed the floor and `requested` | **TARGET, not built** | The proof applies to an uninterrupted interval from U = 0 after a published cut. `since` is 0 at open, and U keeps the recovered suffix (worked restart example). The comparison is U > J, then f > J − U, and `requested` saturates. The options step runs before LOCK, so it can be checked without a store, and covers out of range (> 18,446,744,073,692,774,319) and below the floor. The attempt schedule is fixed to 83,886,160 / 167,772,320 / 251,658,480, and L50 (e)'s maximal frame is two puts. | §11.11 AP15; AP21 open order step 0; L28 (d), L50 (e) |
| C-6 | AP16's plan computed list edits before the data layout, and the optional retry undid only the worklist | **TARGET, not built** | The plan order is H, fresh set, settle (the mandatory retry base), optional relocation, data layout, list edits, list stamps, class-2 layout, buffers; encoding is children first. A refused optional part restores the base, discards dependent state and re-emits from the installed `next_pack`. Leftovers are contiguous, and consumed stamps stay consumed. L41 (vii) adds a refusal after an earlier rename, checked against an independent parse. | §11.12 AP16 step 2, relocation, list edits, refusal; L41 (vii) |
| C-7 | §3.4's retained order cleaned up before AP21 validated, and the prepare opener and NEW's output were unvalidated | **built** | The open order is restated: options, LOCK, MANIFEST, O1, AP21 steps 0–4, cleanup from the validated result, rebuild, journal, account, rotate, streams, the journal task alone. The inventory excludes members of R that were already unlinked. List reads are bounded (`len` ≤ 8,384 and offset arithmetic) before reading. The prepare opener validates with no cleanup, and NEW's private output and prospective MANIFEST are validated before the rename. Validation, capacity and I/O failures stay distinct. **v2.131:** the install's check reads only the packs its own cut wrote and takes the published generation's packs as settled (AP21 "Settled packs at a cut"): the install adds no unread byte to what an open reaches, a fault in a settled pack is one the published generation already has, which open's full AP21 still finds, and each import pin cut reads back its own packs the same way. **v2.133:** every Directory cut made under an export lease reads back its own packs, so every pack an install settles was read once while OLD served. | §11.12 AP21; §11.1 T10; AP16 step 4 |
| C-8 | AP1a let a second op of the CALL's stream disagree with the reply, and W2's fallback could pick another refused cut | **TARGET, not built** | Section tags must be ascending and unique, and the call-owning key has exactly one streams op. The built completion/record stream check is kept (`Before(Foreign, None)`). W2 step 2 uses the last *published* generation. L29 (d) adds 337-byte settle 1 and 2 cases comparing every `Recorded` field, duplicate cases, and the two-refused-cuts case. | §11.1 AP1a; L29 (d) |
| C-9 | L28 (a), L28 (c) and L51 (l) asked for observations their oracles cannot make | **TARGET, not built** | L28 (a) drains before the copy and adds a late insert refusal and crash images across step 4. L28 (c) parses the saved prefix and checks heads through the prepare opener. L51 (l) compares incoming edges as multisets by stable holder identity, through an injective serial-to-name projection, with lease history. | §11.15 L28, L51 |
| C-10 | The cap check sat "before taking an id", before a transaction's final f was known | **TARGET, not built** | The check runs once the final f is known: after the body returns, before the settle, publication, the frame-room wait and a fork's id. A refused COMMIT may consume private stamps, and step charging is unchanged. | §11.11 AP15; AP17; L50 (e) |
| C-11 | The index list's bound was stated on its length while its charge is its granted capacity | **TARGET, not built** | 8n occupied bytes < 14,913,152. The capacity is 8 × G, with G the granted slots and no fixed bound claimed. `try_reserve` is kept: no exact reservation or allocator grant proof is needed, because no hard scratch capacity is promised and the charge is the actual grant. L50 observes 10,000 occupied slots, 8 × G and no key copy. | §11.11 AP15 frame being built, summary; L50 |
| C-12 (LATER) | AP16 and AP18 understated pause and fork cost | **TARGET, not built** | t_drain spans the queued groups, and t_remove and the END sync are named. A fork's direct cost is stated apart from its end-to-end cost when it crosses the threshold: a refused cut is not the fork's failure, and cut I/O poisons it after the FORK is durable. L44's fixture is unchanged. | §11.12 pause; §11.13 AP17 threshold cuts, AP18 |
| C-13 (LATER) | The §11 rewrite list omitted §3.1, §3.3, §3.4 and §3.5a | **TARGET, not built** | They are named, with §1.3, §1.8 and §4's table, and the removed-task text and withdrawn changelog entries are marked as history. | §11 status |
| (a) | Ruling (a): each operation keeps its own result shape | **TARGET, not built** | `run`/`run_on` → `Refused(cause)`; committing `admit` and live-head `fork` → `Error(Full(cause))`; `serve`/`conclude` → `Before(Full(cause), …)`/`After`. The one change is C-3's `Option[Pending]`. | §11.11 AP15 producer table; §11.16 |
| (b) | Ruling (b): one store-local cause | **TARGET, not built** | `StoreCapacity = Journal(Capacity) \| Resident(Capacity) \| Allocation(Capacity)`, built at the refusal site. It is used in `Error.Full`, `Outcome.Refused`, `Looked.Refused`, `Served.NoRoom` and `DeployError.FullNew`. Core `Capacity` and `Stop.OutOfBytes(Capacity)` are unchanged, and no frame, allocator or replay identity changes. Every §11 refusal and law value is retagged. | §11.16; §11.1, AP15, AP16, AP17, L27–L51; store.md Errors |

## CHANGES-v2.31 (the store is the one durability contract)

| # | Item | Verdict | What v2.31 does | Where |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| AO1 | A same-open admit whose admit map was full committed `Admitted(p)` again; §1.8 (D6, AH3, AE3) says the same-open `Abandoned` commits nothing | **CODE FIXED; §1.8 unchanged** | `admit` reserves its open entry before it commits. If there is no room, the admit is `Full` and commits nothing, which is §1.8's `Full` row. After the commit the reserved entry is filled in place without allocating. A reserved entry left by a refused commit answers nothing, so the next admit of p re-commits. The old path could not be reached under charge accounting: a commit's transient memory exceeds the map write, so a commit that fit always left room for the write. Case: `journal_law_streams` refuses an earlier open's re-commit once with `Full`, then admits p under this open; the mutant `reserved_answers` (a reserved entry answers `Abandoned`) is CAUGHT. | §1.8, L13 |
| AO2 | §1.8's `Admission` comment cited REPLAY-SPEC §7.3 rule S3, which REPLAY-SPEC v3 no longer has | **TEXT** | It now cites the store's own rule: D6, re-admission under a new open. | §1.8 |
| AO3 | The trigger build needs two store-level names that STORE-SPEC did not have | **ADDED, text only** | §1.13 names the refusal `StaleAttempt` (the attempt fence) and the run state "held" (the hold mark), from ENDSTATE storage::triggers and storage::steps. The store adds no API, kind or tree for them. | §1.13 |

## CHANGES-v2.30 (ruling C: the store does no authorization; RUNNER-SPEC v2.19)

This version supersedes every scope, policy and authority item of v2.26–v2.29: AJ1, AJ3, AE4, AK1, AK3's scope slot, AL1's policy execution, AL3, AL5's `authority`, AM3's `digest`, `scope` and `seq`, AM8 and the L14 scope law. Amended ruling 73 (RUNNER-SPEC §3) replaces what the scope digests guarded: queued subscription data is re-checked against current state before it is sent, and a call's saved reply is delivered as it was committed.

| # | Item | Verdict | What v2.30 does | Where |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| AN1 | The store authorized each call through a policy, a narrowed Tx and a scope digest beside every reply (ruling C withdraws store-level authorization) | **DELETED** | Deleted: `KeyScope`, `Access`, `scope`, `scope_keys`, `union`, `narrow`, `narrow_view`, `everything`, `none`, `scope_within`, `span`, the `OutOfScope` error; `declare_authority`, the authority tree, `authorize`, `Authorized`, `Digest`, `catalog_table`, `snapshot_seq`, the `seq` counter, the `Latest[Seq]` register, `AuthorityReader`, `authority_reader`, `dup` and `load`; `Stop::Policy`; `Plan.n_p` and `Plan.s_p`; the policy argument and `caller` of `serve`; `Receipt.digest`, `scope` and `seq`; the scope digest in `Pending`, `Stored` and the streams value; `CallRecord.authority`. A Tx may read and write every key of its store. `serve` runs one isolated execution, the body's, under `(n_e, s_e)`. `Plan` is `{ n_e, s_e, reply_max }`; `Receipt` is `{ ticket, reply }`. `CallRecord.code` holds the code that ran: u64 n ‖ u64 s, big-endian, then the replay identity (32 bytes, or none when the launch has none). | §1.3, §1.4, §1.8 |
| AN2 | A stored reply carried a 32-byte scope digest | **CHANGED: 41 + R, format word 2** | The streams value is (position 8, request digest 32, state 1) and, when replied, the reply R: 41 B admitted, 41 + R replied. `Stored` is `{ reply }`, and a duplicate returns its saved reply unchanged. The HEADER's format word is 2. A reader accepts only format 2; any other value is `Unsupported(format)`. The CALL section's last blob is `code` (16 or 48 bytes). | §1.8, §1.11, §2.3, §2.4 |

## CHANGES-v2.29 (caller-scope third attack; RUNNER-SPEC v2.18)

This version supersedes v2.28 AL1's `NoRoom`, `Committed(Ticket)`/`Kept(Ticket)` and `policy` closure. It also supersedes AL4's commit unit and AL6's `ADAPTER_*` text, and v2.28's "one bounded execution" summary.

The checker accepted `serve`'s two isolated executions (probe `sp3`, with controls refused by MEM-30 and FAIL-16). It accepted a static policy's `StaticFn` shape (probe `auth`, with control `authc` refused by PROG-9); that probe checked the shape only. **ATTACK-4 corrections, in place:** `Auth` is deleted for a narrowed `Tx` with `catalog_table` naming grant targets, `KeyScope` is `send`, the register is a `Latest[Seq]` published before the followed snapshot, `AuthorityReader` is `send` with `dup`, `Receipt` and `Pending` keep the run's `seq`, `NoRoom` carries its `Capacity`, `CallInfo` costs 44 B, `recorded` returns its settle context with `settle` 2, the replay identity is derived in `place_head`, and `scope_within` is public (AM1, AM3, AM5–AM8). Checked by probe `/tmp/cs1128-probe/p5s` (a copy of the worktree's `store.hirsel` with these declarations added), accepted: a `StaticFn[fn(Tx, Caller) -> ..]` policy that fetches from one table through `catalog_table`, grants `scope_keys` over another table named the same way, tests it with `scope_within`, and sends the scope through a channel inside a `send` record; and a `Latest[Seq]` reader made, `dup`ed and `load`ed. Controls: `p5c` (`KeyScope` not `send`) is refused with CONC-2, and `p5sx` and `p5sy` (a type error put in the policy body and in the channel function) are refused, so both bodies are checked. A first try named the function `within` and was refused with LANG-7, since `within` is already a parameter name in the store; so the public name is `scope_within`. The catalog lookup is a stub and nothing was run, so the probe checks signatures and ownership only.

| # | Item | Verdict | What v2.29 does | Where |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| AM1 | A refusal dropped the run, so keyed recovery lost its authority and reason. A fault lost its CallInfo (RUNNER-SPEC v2.18 B4, M6) | **CHANGED** | `Served = Committed(Receipt) \| Kept(Receipt) \| Failed(Pending, Stop) \| NoRoom(Pending, Capacity)`. `Pending` (opaque, not send) holds the Completion, the run's digest, scope and authorization `seq`, the record, the run's `CallInfo` and its base version, and on `NoRoom` the outcome the commit would have stored. `NoRoom` is a refusal **before the publish point**: nothing is stored, and its `Capacity{requested, available}` is that admission's (ATTACK-4 B7). A stream position drops the `Pending`, so it stays `Admitted`. A keyed claim's third resident refusal (http.md K-8) passes it to `conclude(mut db, take p, take r, Reserve.Claim)`, with `r` = the encoded `Failed(OutOfBytes(Room{requested: c.requested, available: c.available}))` from the returned `Capacity`; it writes that control-only reply with the digest, record, CallInfo, base, `settle` 2 and the claim's expiry and index mutations, from the claim's reserve, once. The body never runs a fourth time. `untried(take c, take rec, info: CallInfo)` gives `none`'s digest for a gone key. | §1.8 |
| AM2 | A migration's operations each checkpoint, so a failed migration left a partial catalog (B5) | **CHANGED: one catalog publication** | Inside `migrate`, `create_table`, `create_index`, `drop_table` and row `migrate` write their pages, but none publishes a manifest. When the phase returns `Ok`, one checkpoint publishes the new manifest with every change and advances the catalog version once. An `Error`, a fault or a crash before that checkpoint leaves the old manifest. Pages that no manifest names are discarded at open, as an interrupted checkpoint's are, so rule 12's promise of the old catalog holds. The open contract is unchanged: an open sees the old manifest or the new one (the §3.5 cut table's `migrate` row, now for the whole phase). | §1.2 :536; §3.5 |
| AM3 | A fresh success returned only a ticket, so the head could not build the first `Told` (M5) | **CHANGED: a receipt** | `pub type Receipt { ticket: Ticket, reply: Bytes, digest: Digest, scope: KeyScope, seq: u64 }` (not codec, not send). `serve` returns the reply bytes it stored, by move (it stores a copy), together with the digest, scope and authorization `seq` of the run (ATTACK-4 B2: the runner stamps the first `Told` with that `seq`, never with a later register load). `conclude` returns one for the fixed fault. The head builds the first `Told` from it, with no read-back, so first delivery never sets HTTP's `Idempotent-Replayed`. | §1.8 |
| AM4 | A post-publication error could follow a durable reply, yet promised `Admitted` (B6) | **CHANGED: before and after handoff** | `serve` and `conclude` return `Result[Served, ServeError]`, with `ServeError = Before(Error) \| After(Error)`. `Before` is any refusal or failure before the publish point (`run` step 4, §3), and leaves the position exactly as it was. `After` is any error from the publish point on (frame handoff, a cut, rotation). The runner treats it as poison: it answers nothing and exits (RUNNER-SPEC §2.1 Poison), and reopen recovers whatever prefix is durable, which may hold the reply (then a re-send gets `Duplicate`). Cancellation: during settle it drops the draft (I4); at any later wait it is a crash cut. An HTTP disconnect does not cancel the head. No `Pending` survives a restart. | §1.8; §3 |
| AM5 | `ADAPTER_STEPS` and `ADAPTER_BYTES` had no derivation; variable work lay outside both bounds (B7) | **CHANGED: derived** | **Byte grants.** Let P = `n_p` and E = `n_e`. The policy's execution reserves P, and its result L ≤ P stays charged. The body's execution holds a clone C ≤ L, plus the body's whole draft (dirty roots, op lists, issued-ID cell, the return value and the stored reply copy), all under E. **The body's byte bound** covers the reply write and creation and growth of the read log and the issued-ID cell's node. **The body's step bound** also covers frame encoding, whose bytes come from the head's admitted `frame_max`. **Outside both bounds:** the issued ids are one high-watermark `Cell[u64]` (MEM-29, v2.89), finalized by one `cell.into_inner`; no step grows with them. **I4's settle is the head's (VERIFY-5 B8).** `settle_admit` runs after `serve`'s step bound has returned its unused steps, under no call bound. That covers `current_state`, `cut_state` with its `state_account` walk over the pinned graph, the frame's re-encode and its checksum's 64-byte stripes. Its bytes are the Db's (a refusal is `Refused(c)`, as in I4), and its time is the head's, as frame encoding is under `frame_max`. So a cost that grows with pinned state never decides a call's outcome. **Peak:** L + C + E ≤ 2P + E; the handed-over L is not counted again. **Bytes reserved** = 2·P + E + A_b, where A_b is the sum of the MEM-24 allocations actually charged outside P and E. The body isolate contributes its 32-byte cause cell. The policy isolate's cause cell stays under P. Inline isolate results and the inline fields of `Pending` and `Receipt` contribute zero, including the 32-byte `Digest`, the 44-byte `CallInfo` and the moved reply's list handle. The moved reply's storage remains charged to E. L33 checks exact admission at the sum and refusal one byte below it. **Frame encoding and the commit** use the head's own admitted `frame_max`, not this reserve. **Steps reserved per draft attempt** = `s_e + 6`. Outside the body's step bound, the metered draft pays for entering `draft_call` (1), entering `isolate` and its callback (2), extracting the issued-ID cell (1), finishing the read log (1; the cell extraction is inside core), and entering `finish_stage` (1). A prepared reply always has a stream edit, so finalization skips the data-row scan. A raised fault can call `raised_capacity` in place of finalization and uses fewer steps. The Db driver owns the attempt record, latch handling and pager delivery outside this reserve (§13.3). Each rerun gets the same draft reserve. L33 checks admission and the six steps on a saturated body. **Registration** uses checked addition for both reserves and returns `Bounds` on overflow. | §1.8; budgets.md |
| AM6 | A concluded fault's record replayed under the conclude frame's CallInfo (M6) | **CHANGED** | Tag 2 gains `settle u8` after `authority`. 0 means the record ran in this frame (its CallInfo is the frame's). 1 means it was concluded after a body fault, and 2 that it was concluded after the run's commit was refused for room (ATTACK-4 M3); both are followed by `base u64-BE ‖ at u64-BE ‖ seed u64-BE`, the attempted run's. `recorded` returns the record with this settle context: `pub type Recorded { record: CallRecord, settle: u8, branch: Branch, base: u64, at: u64, seed: u64 }`. Replay forks at `base` (v-1 for settle 0), derives the attempted id from `base` and `branch`, and uses `at` and `seed`; settle 2 is replayed as `Unknown(AdmissionRefused)`. The one-row call example grows by 1 byte to **374 + L B**, or **406 B** for L = 32; a concluded record is 24 bytes longer. | §2.4 |
| AM7 | Launch identity, namespaces and an obsolete size (M7, M8, m1) | **CHANGED** | **Replay identity (ATTACK-4 M4).** The launch digest stays the launcher's hash of the image bytes (launch.md), fixed before main. `place_head` derives the replay identity when it is given the selected constructors: H(launch digest ‖ u32 len ‖ `place`'s identity ‖ u32 len ‖ `migrate`'s ‖ u32 len ‖ `handles`'s), and `CallRecord.authority` carries it in place of AK4's launch digest (so L = 32 when it is known, 0 when not). A policy without a revision (PROG-9) records 32 zero bytes as its revision; an unavailable identity is empty (L = 0). Recorded-code mode answers `Unknown(RevisionUnknown)` for either. **Namespaces.** The `namespaces` entry is compared on kind and sender names at bind as well as at open, and a retired op's binding is compared with its own op record **at open**, namespace included (http.md K-1, K-9; ATTACK-4 M6: `bind` runs before the store is open). **§2.4.** "A reply adds R" becomes "a reply adds 32 + R" (the scope digest and the bytes). | §2.4 :1008; http.md K-1 |
| AM8 | The release design's hooks (RUNNER-SPEC v2.18 B1–B3; corrected in place by ATTACK-4 B1–B3, M1, M5) | **NEW** | **Authority tables.** `declare_authority(mut db, names: List[Str]) -> Result[(), Error]`, called once at open. A name that is not a table in the catalog is `Missing(name)`. An authority commit is a commit on main that replaces main's `authority` or `catalog` root, and every `adopt` (VERIFY-5 B3). A cut or rotation shares its roots and is not one. **The authority tree (VERIFY-5 B3).** Every snapshot of main carries `authority`, an in-memory tree holding a copy of every row and index entry of the declared tables, keyed as in `data`. `declare_authority` builds it at open from `data`. A commit that writes a declared table also applies those writes to it, admitted with the commit and charged to the Db. The policy's reads go to it, while bodies use `data`. It is never written to disk, so the format, cuts and replay are unchanged. So only an authority commit rebuilds what a policy can read, and two evaluations at one `seq` are equal. A commit on another branch never is: the register protects main. **The counter.** Every snapshot of main carries `seq: u64`, in memory only: 0 at open, plus 1 for each authority commit. **The register (B1).** A `Latest[Seq]` root (CONC-13; `Seq { v: u64 }`, `shareable + send`), made at open with `Seq{v: 0}` and charged to the Db. An authority commit admits its next `Arc[Seq]` before its last refusal point (`run` step 3; `adopt` before `cut_install`), so a failed admission is `Refused(c)`, a `Before`. `publish(db, main)` stores it with `latest.publish` **before** the followed snapshot's `latest.publish`, the commit's first publication another task can observe; a `memory` store does the same. **The reader.** `pub opaque type AuthorityReader: send` wraps one `Latest[Seq]` handle; `authority_reader(db)` makes one, `dup(r)` another (`latest.handle`, never fails), and `load(r) -> u64` is one `latest.read`. **Authorize.** `pub type Digest { a: u64, b: u64, c: u64, d: u64 }` (plain, `shareable + send`). `authorize(v: Tx, caller: Caller, policy: StaticFn[fn(Tx, Caller) -> Result[KeyScope, Capacity]], n: u64, s: u64) -> Result[Authorized, Stop]` runs the policy in an isolated execution under (n, s) on `v` narrowed read-only to the declared tables (B2: v2.29's opaque `Auth` is deleted). `Tx` is `pub opaque scoped` (PROG-2; VERIFY-5 B2), so a policy cannot read its fields. **One metered path (B3):** on every path the policy's reads go to a fresh, empty read log made inside its bound; a read the log cannot record, or any other capacity failure of a read, stops the evaluation with `Stop::Policy` and is never returned to the policy. On success inside a `look`, that log is appended to the look's log; at the head it is dropped. It checks every entry's `StoreId`, empty scopes included (`Foreign` → `Stop::Policy`), and returns `Authorized { scope: KeyScope, digest: Digest, seq: u64 }`, with the digest by AL3. `serve`'s policy execution calls it. **Grant targets (B2).** `catalog_table[K, V](tx: Tx, take name: String, take key: Key[K], take value: Codec[V]) -> Result[Table[K, V], Error]` names any table in the Tx's catalog with a handle's codec check (`Missing`, `Schema`), and commits nothing; naming a table does not make it readable. **Containment (M5).** `scope_within(outer: KeyScope, inner: KeyScope) -> bool` is `narrow`'s relation, the existing private function made public: equal `StoreId`, and each inner entry inside one outer entry of the same table with equal or greater access. **Sendable scope (B2).** `KeyScope` and its entries are `send`. | §1.4; §1.5; §1.8; §3.1 |

## CHANGES-v2.28 (caller-scope second attack; RUNNER-SPEC v2.17)

It supersedes v2.27 AK2's `Schema.migrate` and AK3's public `reply` and `record`. The `serve` shape was accepted by the checker with the Tx built inside the bound; lending an outer `mut Tx` to the bound was refused (MEM-30), and moving the `Completion` inside the closure was refused (LANG-14) (RUNNER-SPEC v2.17 header).

| # | Item | Verdict | What v2.28 does | Where |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| AL1 | The runner's callback could not consume a `Completion`, a crashed open's position had none, and a fault lost the run's digest (RUNNER-SPEC v2.17 B4, B2) | **CHANGED: `serve` owns the sequence** | `pub fn serve(mut db: Db, take c: Completion, plan: Plan, take rec: Option[CallRecord], policy: scoped[view] fn(Tx) -> Result[KeyScope, Capacity], body: scoped[view] fn(mut Tx) -> Result[Bytes, Bytes]) -> Result[Served, Error] waits`. `Plan { n_p, s_p, n_e, s_e, reply_max }` holds the policy's and the body's byte and step allowances and the longest reply. `serve` runs two isolated executions over the head's snapshot, with nothing committed between (it holds `mut db`). The policy's, under `s_p`, builds a Tx from views, runs the policy and computes the scope's digest (AL3), returning both. Between the two, outside both, `serve` sets that digest in its local `c`, so it survives any later exit. The body's, under `s_e`, builds a second Tx narrowed to a `clone` of the scope, moves it into the body's bound, gets it back on every normal exit, and writes the reply (`Replied(r, scope_digest)`) from a view of `c`. A raise is caught by that execution's `isolate`. After the body's execution it attaches `rec`, encodes the frame and commits, so the record and the reply commit together or not at all. `pub type Served = Committed(Ticket) \| Kept(Ticket) \| Failed(Pending, Stop) \| NoRoom` and `pub type Stop = Raised(u32) \| OutOfSteps(u64) \| OutOfBytes(Capacity) \| Policy`: `Committed` for `Ok`; `Kept` for `Error` (the reply alone, F2); `Failed` for a raise, an exhausted bound, a reply over `plan.reply_max`, or a policy that faulted, ran out or returned a `Foreign` scope (AL3), with the Tx dropped, which rolls it back; `NoRoom` when the commit passes `resident_limit`, with the Tx, the record and `c` dropped and the position left `Admitted`. `pub opaque type Pending` (not copy, codec or send) holds `c` with its digest (the run's, or `none`'s for `Policy`) and `rec`. `pub fn conclude(mut db: Db, take p: Pending, take r: Bytes) -> Result[Served, Error] waits` writes `r` with the Pending's digest and record in one streams-only commit and runs no code; it gives `Committed` or `NoRoom`. `pub fn untried(take c: Completion, take rec: Option[CallRecord]) -> Pending` gives `none`'s digest, for a position that runs nothing (a gone key). `Admission` becomes `Admitted(Completion) \| Abandoned(Completion) \| Duplicate(Stored) \| Rejected(u64) \| RequestMismatch \| HistoryUnavailable`; `Abandoned` carries a `Completion` for both of its rows, and `ticket(c)` gives the admit's ticket. The public `reply` and `record` are deleted; no body ever holds a `Completion` or a `Pending`. A copy of store.hirsel with `serve`, `conclude` and `untried` was accepted (63 files). These were refused: the digest set inside the policy's execution (MEM-30), a nested `isolate` around the body taking the Tx mutably (FAIL-16), and both executions inside one outer bound (MEM-30). The store already has local names `answer` and `pending`, hence `conclude` and `untried` (LANG-7). `serve` returns `Error` for a store failure, after which the position stays `Admitted`. | §1.8 (:547-548, :737-740, :786) |
| AL2 | A duplicate returned bytes without the digest it ran under (RUNNER-SPEC v2.17 B2) | **CHANGED** | `Duplicate(Stored)` with `pub type Stored { reply: Bytes, scope_digest: [32]u8 }`, for streams and keyed claims alike. The store decides nothing about release; the runner's writer compares the digest (RUNNER-SPEC v2.17 §3). | §1.8 |
| AL3 | Two stores could give two scopes one digest (ATTACK-2 B2) | **CHANGED: the digest is local to its store** | The digest input becomes `u8 15 ‖ "hirsel.scope.v1" ‖ StoreId (hi u64-BE ‖ lo u64-BE) ‖ body`, 16 bytes more than AK1. `scope_digest(db: Db, s: KeyScope) -> Result[[32]u8, Foreign]`: a scope with an entry whose table or index belongs to another store is `Foreign`, and `serve` reports it as `Failed(_, Policy)`. `none` digests body `u8 0 ‖ u32-BE 0` under this store's id. | §1.4 |
| AL4 | `Schema.migrate` let H keep a bit derived from row data (RUNNER-SPEC v2.17 B3) | **CHANGED: two phases** | `pub type Migration` (not codec, not send) is made by the runner for the `migrate` phase only. It offers `create_table`, `create_index`, `table`, `index`, `migrate` (§1.2) and `drop_table`, which commit as §1.2 says, and nothing else. `Schema` keeps `table` and `index` and only **opens**: a name not in the catalog is `Missing(name)`, a schema that does not resolve is `Schema(i)` as before, and it never commits, so `table` over a `Schema` never appends a schema number. A schema change therefore belongs to `migrate`. | §1.2 |
| AL5 | Replay could not compare the authority (RUNNER-SPEC v2.17 M1) | **CHANGED: the CALL section carries it** | Tag 2 gains `authority (u32 length ‖ bytes)` after `revision`. The bytes are AK4's: 64 B (`code.revision(policy)` 32 and four u64) plus the launch digest's length L. A call section that ends before it is `Corrupt(Malformed)`. | §2.4 (:968, :979) |
| AL6 | The sizes still said 41 + R, and the read log was unbounded (RUNNER-SPEC v2.17 M2, M4) | **CHANGED** | An `Admitted` value is 41 B and a `Replied` value 73 + R (position 8, request digest 32, state 1, scope digest 32, reply R). A replied row costs 62 + r8(S) + r8(73 + R); an admitted row is unchanged at 118 for S = 8. Pack leaves: 49 + S admitted, 81 + S + R replied. The one-row call example grows 273 → 305 from the digest, and to 373 + L with AL5's authority. An admission frame stays 92 + S. L29's generator uses both value sizes. `serve` keeps no read log for the body (the head is the only writer and no transact result uses a read set); a `look`'s log is its read set, charged as RUNNER-SPEC v2.17 M4 says. (v2.29 AM8, ATTACK-4 B3: the policy's evaluation has one fresh read log inside its bound on every path, dropped at the head.) `serve`'s own work outside the two bounds is derived in AM5 (ATTACK-4 B8: the issued-ID loop is outside, as built, so `ADAPTER_STEPS` is replaced by A_s; VERIFY-5 B8: I4's settle is the head's, outside `serve`'s step bound); the digest, its sort and its encoding run inside the policy's bound. | :868, :979, :981, §3, L29 |

v2.27 (RUNNER-SPEC v2.16, lane caller-scope-1128, astra's attack on v2.26): text only. A reply is stored with the digest of the scope it ran under (AK1). `handles` gets a schema-only handle (AK2). Completing a position needs a `Completion` that only `admit` makes (AK3). A call record names the policy that authorized it (AK4). AJ1's fallback is corrected, and misses and empty ranges are reads (AK5). The streams value and `CallRecord` gain bytes (AK1, AK4); no store has been deployed, so the cutover is a fresh store (RUNNER-SPEC v2.16 m3).

## CHANGES-v2.27 (caller-scope attack fixes; RUNNER-SPEC v2.16)

| # | Item | Verdict | What v2.27 does | Where |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| AK1 | A stored reply could be released to a caller who lost the right to read it (RUNNER-SPEC v2.16 B2) | **NEW: `scope_digest` beside every reply** | The streams value becomes (position, digest, Admitted \| Replied(reply, scope_digest)), and a keyed claim's reply the same. `reply` takes the digest from its `Completion` (AK3), which the adapter fills before the body runs. `scope_digest(s: KeyScope) -> [32]u8` is SHA-256(`u8 15 ‖ "hirsel.scope.v1" ‖ body`). For the outermost scope, body = `u8 1`. Otherwise body = `u8 0 ‖ u32-BE count ‖ entries`, each entry encoded as `u32-BE table ‖ u8 whole ‖ u8 write ‖ len32 lo ‖ lo ‖ len32 hi ‖ hi`, sorted bytewise with duplicates dropped, so two scopes with the same entries have one digest whatever order the policy built them in. Cost: 32 B per reply. | §1.4, streams (:731) |
| AK2 | `handles` received `mut Db`, so H could hold a `Snapshot` and read outside every read set (RUNNER-SPEC v2.16 B3) | **NEW: `Schema`** | `pub type Schema` (not codec, not send) is made by the runner from its `Db` for the duration of `handles` only. It offers `table`, `index` and `migrate` as `Db` does, and nothing else: no `snapshot`, `look`, `run`, `admit` or row read. It refuses a name in the reserved `hirsel.` namespace with `Exists("reserved")`. | §1.2, §1.4 |
| AK3 | `reply` took a stream name and position, so any `mut Tx` could complete any position (RUNNER-SPEC v2.16 B4) | **CHANGED: `admit` returns a `Completion`; `reply` and `record` consume it** | `Admission.Admitted` carries `Completion`, an opaque value that is neither copy nor codec nor send. It names the stream (or claim), the position and the bound request digest, and holds a slot for the scope digest that the adapter sets once (`set_scope(mut c, s: KeyScope)`). `pub fn record(mut tx: Tx, c: Completion, take rec: CallRecord) -> Result[(), Error]` views it, and `pub fn reply(mut tx: Tx, take c: Completion, take r: Bytes) -> Result[(), Error]` consumes it. So in one Tx `record`, when there is one, comes first, and `reply` is last, and a position is completed exactly once. The stream-and-position forms are deleted. A `Completion` whose scope digest was never set is `Missing("scope")` at `reply`. When the policy itself fails, there is no scope to digest: the adapter sets `none(tx)`'s, and the reply-only `Failed` it writes carries no application bytes, so the digest is never compared. | §1.4 (:536, :727-728) |
| AK4 | Replay could not tell that the policy had changed (RUNNER-SPEC v2.16 M1) | **NEW: `CallRecord.authority`** | `authority: Bytes` = `code.revision(policy) ‖ u64-BE policy n ‖ u64-BE policy s ‖ u64-BE entry n ‖ u64-BE entry s`, followed by the launch digest when the runner has one. The store treats it as opaque bytes and journals it with the record. | §1.7, §1.8 |
| AK5 | AJ1's fallback ran the policy in a separate `look` and passed its scope in, which would lose the policy's reads | **CORRECTED** | If a nested view cannot share `look`'s read log, `look` runs the policy and then the scoped body itself, on one snapshot and one log. A scope precomputed by another `look` is never used. In either form a `fetch` that finds nothing records its Point, and an empty `range` records its Span (I1), so a policy that grants because a row is absent re-runs when that row appears. | §1.4 (:4268 in store.hirsel) |

v2.26 (RUNNER-SPEC v2.15, lane caller-scope-1128, GAPS.md gap 3): text only. A query can now run narrowed to a caller's scope: `narrow_view` (AJ1). A span named without reading obeys `range`'s scope rule: `span` (AJ2). The two whole scopes are public (AJ3). §1.7's forward note on the principal is superseded (AJ4). No byte of the format changes, and no model changes.

## CHANGES-v2.26 (a caller's scope over views; RUNNER-SPEC v2.15)

| # | Item | Verdict | What v2.26 does | Where |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| AJ1 | A query (a view `Tx` inside `look`) could not be narrowed: `narrow` takes `mut Tx` | **NEW: `narrow_view`** | `pub fn narrow_view[R](tx: Tx, take within: KeyScope, body: scoped[view] fn(Tx) -> R) -> Result[R, Error]`. It has `narrow`'s containment rule and errors (AE4 e), and it runs the body on a view Tx that shares `tx`'s roots, read log and issued log, with `within` as its scope. Reads made through it are recorded in the same `Seen.reads`. Nothing is swapped back, because `tx` itself is unchanged. **Build probe:** whether a scoped `Tx` can hold a second view of the read log, as `look` makes the first (store.hirsel:4282). If it cannot, see AK5 (v2.27): `look` runs the policy and then the scoped body itself. A scope precomputed by a separate `look` is never used. | §1.4 |
| AJ2 | A Cover names a span without reading it, so no scope check applied to it | **REPLACED: `key_span` → `span`** | `pub fn span[K, V](tx: Tx, t: Shape[K, V], lo: Bound[K], hi: Bound[K]) -> Result[Read, Error]` gives the data-tree span `key_span` gave. It is `OutOfScope` when the span is not within the union of `tx`'s scope entries for `t`, of any access, by AE4 b's rule. It records nothing in the read set. `key_span`, whose only caller was RUNNER's `cover`, is deleted. | §1.4 |
| AJ3 | A place's policy must say "everything" or "nothing" | **NEW: `everything(tx)`, `none(tx)`** | `pub fn everything(tx: Tx) -> KeyScope` is the outermost scope (AE4: one ReadWrite entry per table, tables made later included). `pub fn none(tx: Tx) -> KeyScope` has no entries, so every read and write under it is `OutOfScope`. | §1.4 |
| AJ4 | §1.7: "the principal joins in M5a as a new non-MUST kind" | **SUPERSEDED (RUNNER-SPEC v2.15 item 7)** | The Caller is the stream key's prefix (`caller_key ‖ u32-BE(stream)`), so `CallRecord.stream` already names it, and replay decodes it from there. No frame kind is added, and the reserved kind 0x0060 PRINCIPAL is retired, never reused. | §1.7, §2.7 |

## CHANGES-v2.25 (keyset pages; gap study 1117 #6)

| # | Item | Verdict | What v2.25 does | Where |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| AI1 | GAPS.md #6: `scan` and `find` return a cursor, but no bounded page and no exact resume position exist. `find` takes `Bound[J]` only, so a caller cannot resume between entries with equal J | **NEW: pages over the existing byte cursor; an in-process continuation** | `scan_page`/`find_page` and `scan_next`/`find_next` (§1.5). The limits are rows and bytes, and zero is `Missing`. A page holds at least one row when one remains. A one-key peek ends the last page with `next` = None. `Resume` binds the Snapshot's identity, the table or index id, the upper bound on the logical key and the last full logical key, and resumes at Excluded(that key). Another Snapshot is `Moved(version)`, and another incarnation is `Stale(id)`. **Rejected:** a byte limit that refuses an oversized first row (a new `Limit` case, and a caller below one row's size is stuck); a continuation that holds the Snapshot (the caller already owns one, and a hidden hold would keep old nodes alive with nobody accounting for it); a `codec` continuation now (a served token must also bind caller and scope, gap #3, and is http.md B-11's). | §1.5, L36 |

## CHANGES-v2.24 (request identity at stream positions; Sam ruled C)

| # | Item | Verdict | What v2.24 does | Where |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| AH1 | AG2 left P2 and P3 to the client: `admit` took no request bytes, so changed bytes at a retained `p` could get another request's `Duplicate` or run after its `Abandoned` | **RULED C (Sam, DECISIONS 2026-11-17). This is the /wholehog option:** one digest, computed by the server, compared by the store at admit and by both runner join paths, so no path shares an answer without it. **Rejected:** A, the v2.23 client-only rule (smaller, and Kafka/Convex practice, but a buggy or non-SDK client gets another request's reply); B, the digest without `HistoryUnavailable` (with latest = 7, new bytes at 0 still got `Superseded`) | **The digest.** `request_digest(key, args)` is SHA-256 (std, std-sha256-1071) over `u8 17 ‖ "hirsel.request.v1" ‖ u32 len(key) ‖ key ‖ u32 len(args) ‖ args`: versioned, domain-separated, length-delimited. `key` is the immutable entry Key (16 identity ‖ 8 args_fp ‖ 8 result_fp); `args` are the exact argument bytes. The slot, the deadline and the server's executable revision are not inputs: a reconnect remaps slots, a retry changes its deadline, and deployed code may change. The runner computes it at ingress (RUNNER-SPEC v2.11.3 row 1) and never takes a digest from a client. **At admit:** the stream value is (`p`, digest, Admitted \| Replied(reply)). At `p` = latest the digest is compared first; a different digest is `RequestMismatch`, with nothing committed and nothing run. An equal digest gives `Duplicate` or `Abandoned` as before. Identical bytes at a new admissible `p` are a new call. **The digest is kept** through a reply, a reply-only `Failed`, a typed error's reply (F2), and an earlier open's `Abandoned` re-commit. Only `Admitted` binds a digest: pre-admission refusals, `Rejected`, `RequestMismatch`, `HistoryUnavailable` and `Full` bind nothing. **Model:** `model_v224_client.py`: the three v2.23 client mutants run as buggy clients and the store keeps law I for each; seven store mutants, all CAUGHT. | §1.8, L33, L35 |
| AH2 | `Rejected(l)` with `p` < l was the proof of `Superseded`, but the store keeps one state per stream, so it holds no digest for an older `p` | **RULED: `HistoryUnavailable`** | Below latest, `admit` answers `HistoryUnavailable` and commits nothing. The store cannot say whether `p` ran these bytes. The answer is terminal for the sender: the stream retires, and no replacement work is started automatically (WIRE W4c fences; work whose bytes were lost stays `LocallyUnknown`). The store does not mark the stream: a later `p` = latest + 1 from the stream's owner still admits. Keeping historical digests would break one state per stream (M4-PLAN §2.4). `Rejected(l)` now always means `p` > l. RUNNER's `Superseded` loses its store basis (followups to runner-spec-1020). | §1.8 |
| AH3 | Byte cost of the digest | **ACCEPTED as ADVICE "Byte cost" derives it** | The digest is embedded fixed-size, with no length. Stream value 9 + R → 41 + R.
